# Autopilot Architecture & Refactor Analysis

*Deep-dive technical reference for a future significant refactor of the Autopilot agentic workflow engine. Two complementary passes, described in full where each begins: eleven sections of static analysis of the current codebase (with file:line citations throughout, reconstructing ~45 illustrative bug-fix commits along the way — HEAD `cb8c01a`, 2026-08-14), followed by eight sections mining the actual diffs behind a systematic clustering of all 591 `fix:` commits in this repo's full history — see [Bug-Fix History: A Second, Independent Pass](#bug-fix-history-a-second-independent-pass) for that pass's own methodology.*

## Why this document exists

Autopilot is HephaestusNG's core engine: it takes a design document, decomposes it into features, runs each feature through a ~14-phase pipeline (requirements → architecture → development → adversarial/architectural/design review → QA → product validation → security/forensics → git commit) driving tmux-hosted CLI coding agents (pi, Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Droid), and self-heals the many ways a long-running, restart-prone, multi-process background system can drift into an inconsistent state.

It has also accumulated a long tail of subtle concurrency and state-consistency bugs — the recent git history (`git log --oneline -- src/autopilot src/agents src/phases src/monitoring src/services src/workflow`) is dominated by `fix:` commits closing races between the periodic background sweep, the synchronous request-time path, and the orchestrator's own retry/arbitration logic. This document exists to make that accumulated knowledge legible in one place before a structural refactor, rather than leaving it scattered across a codebase's worth of "observed live: …" comments and easy-to-miss commit messages.

**Scope.** Eleven subsystems were analyzed in depth, each by an independent deep-read pass with mandatory file:line citation for every factual claim about code structure — the first seven in an initial pass, the remaining four added after a gap-check identified files that were heavily referenced but never themselves opened (see [Coverage Notes](#coverage-notes)):

1. **Orchestrator Core** — `src/autopilot/orchestrator.py` (10,246 lines), `phases.py`, `service.py`, `spec.py`, `okf_markdown.py`
2. **Phase Management** — `src/phases/*`, `src/workflow_engine/*`, the phase YAML graph
3. **Agent Lifecycle Management** — `src/agents/*`, tmux process handling
4. **Worktree Management & CLI Interface Abstraction** — `src/core/worktree_manager.py`, `src/interfaces/cli_interface.py` (all six concrete CLI implementations)
5. **Monitoring** — `src/monitoring/*` (Guardian, Conductor, orphan reaper, trajectory context)
6. **Task & Dispatch Services** — `src/services/{agent_dispatch,agent_communication,queue,task_blocking,task_completion,task_enrichment,task_similarity}_service.py`
7. **Result, Ticket & Validation Services** — `src/services/{result,workflow_result,result_validator,ticket,ticket_history,ticket_search,cost_collection,embedding}_service.py` + validation helpers
8. **Validator Agents & Memory/RAG Backend** — `src/validation/{validator_agent,check_executors}.py`, `src/memory/{store_factory,embedding_factory,turbovec_store,vector_store,rag}.py`
9. **Workflow Termination & API Surface** — `src/workflow/termination_handler.py`, `src/mcp/autopilot_api.py`, `src/mcp/projects_api.py`
10. **Core Agent & Task API** — `src/mcp/api.py` (3,225 lines, the dashboard-facing FastAPI router)
11. **Additional API Surface** — `src/mcp/agents_api.py`, `src/mcp/tickets_api.py`, `src/mcp/messaging_api.py`

Sections 12-20 are a second, independent pass over the same subject matter from the opposite direction — starting from all 591 `fix:` commits in this repo's history rather than the current code — see [Bug-Fix History: A Second, Independent Pass](#bug-fix-history-a-second-independent-pass) for that pass's own scope and method.

**What this document is not**: a proposed refactor design. It is the reference material a refactor design should be built on — an accurate map of what exists, why it exists (tied to specific historical incidents where the git history preserves that context), and where the structural seams are. Section-level "Refactor observations" note concrete problems worth addressing; the final [Cross-Cutting Themes](#cross-cutting-themes) and [Prioritized Refactor Roadmap](#prioritized-refactor-roadmap) sections synthesize patterns that recur across subsystem boundaries.

---

## System overview

```
Design queue (files + AutopilotDesign rows)
        │
        ▼
run_continuous_pipeline()  ──── per-project asyncio loop, orchestrator.py
        │  pick_next_design()
        ▼
run_single_design()
        │
        ├─▶ run_phase0()  ──▶ Feature Architect workflow ──▶ Feature rows
        │
        └─▶ run_feature_pipelines()  ── dependency-ordered, up to 4 parallel
                    │
                    ▼
            run_single_workflow()  ── per-Workflow poll loop (15s tick)
                    │
                    ▼
            _advance_phases()  ── the phase state machine (mutex-protected)
                    │
        ┌───────────┼────────────────────────────┐
        ▼           ▼                            ▼
  _create_phase_task()   _fire_phase_transition()   _trigger_arbitration()
        │                     │                            │
        ▼                     ▼                            ▼
  AgentDispatchService /  PhaseManager.mark_phase_complete   one-shot LLM
  create_agent_for_task_direct   (src/phases + workflow_engine)   arbiter agent
        │
        ▼
  AgentManager.create_agent_for_task()  ── tmux session + CLI process
        │
        ▼
  agent works, calls update_task_status / complete_my_task (MCP tools)
        │
        ▼
  TaskCompletionService  ── verify output → commit → fire_spec_gate_if_ready
        │                                         │
        ▼                                         ▼
  (loops back to _advance_phases via          (synchronous fast path,
   the next poll tick — self-healed              bypasses the poll tick
   by the background sweep)                      when a gated phase closes)

  In parallel, every 60s: MonitoringLoop (Guardian per-agent trajectory
  analysis, Conductor system-wide coherence, orphan-session reaping,
  stuck-task nudge/fail sweep, diagnostic-agent escalation)
```

Two structural facts fall out of this picture that matter for everything that follows:

- **Phase completion has two independent entry points** (`run_single_workflow`'s poll tick calling `_advance_phases`, and `TaskCompletionService.fire_spec_gate_if_ready`'s synchronous fast path) that both converge on `PhaseManager.mark_phase_complete`. Both had to independently earn their own concurrency protection over the course of the fix history documented below — the synchronous path notably had none for a period (`fe9a141`).
- **Dispatch has two independent implementations** (`AgentDispatchService` for the HTTP route-handler path, `create_agent_for_task_direct` for the orchestrator's in-process phase-transition path) with different duplicate-guard coverage. A task's protection against being double-dispatched depends on which of the two paths created it.

Both facts recur as root causes across multiple subsystem sections below, and are the throughline of the [Cross-Cutting Themes](#cross-cutting-themes) section.

## Orchestrator Core

### 1. Purpose & Responsibilities

`src/autopilot/orchestrator.py` (formerly 10,246 lines — by far the largest module in the codebase) has since been decomposed (per `design_docs/backend_module_decomposition.md`) into the `src/autopilot/orchestrator/` package: `__init__.py` (3,473 lines — top-level pipeline flow: `OrchestratorLogger`, `run_single_workflow`, `run_phase0`, `run_feature_pipelines`, `run_design_aggregate`, `run_continuous_pipeline`, `main`), `phase_transitions.py` (3,188 lines — the phase-advancement state machine, task-creation-claim primitives, arbitration), `engine_client.py` (653 lines — direct-DB dispatch/termination/pause primitives), `worktree_integration.py` (843 lines), `features.py` (703 lines), `queue.py` (716 lines), `policy.py` (477 lines), `state.py` (542 lines), `reporting.py` (183 lines). All line citations below reflect this post-decomposition layout. It remains the runtime engine of the Autopilot subsystem, owning:

- **The design queue → feature → phase pipeline**: scanning a project's design-document queue, decomposing a design into features (Phase 0 / Feature Architect), running each feature through the ~12-phase `autopilot` workflow (product requirements → architecture → development → reviews → QA → product validation → git commit → forensics), and aggregating per-design results.
- **Phase-transition state machine**: the single source of truth for advancing a `Workflow`'s phases (`_advance_phases`, `src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:436`), including task/agent creation, retry, goto, and arbitration decisions.
- **Concurrency and idempotency primitives** guarding against duplicate task/agent creation across the multiple independent callers that can all decide "this phase needs work" at once (the polling loop in `run_single_workflow`, the background sweep, synchronous request-time hooks in `server.py`/`task_completion_service.py`, and arbitration).
- **Self-healing / recovery** for the many ways a long-running, restart-prone background process can leave a `Workflow`/`Task`/`Feature`/`Agent` row in an inconsistent state (stale claims, orphaned tasks, abandoned worktrees, lost workflow links, exhausted retries).
- **Review-mode gating** (human-in-the-loop pause/approve) layered orthogonally on top of the autonomous pipeline.
- **Persistence of pipeline-level state** across backend restarts (`PersistentPipelineState`, `src/autopilot/orchestrator/state.py:314`) and per-project service lifecycle (`src/autopilot/service.py`'s `AutopilotService`/`AutopilotServiceRegistry`).

`src/autopilot/phases.py` is a much smaller, purely declarative module: it loads `config/workflows/autopilot/*.yaml` once at import time and exposes `AUTOPILOT_PHASES`, `AUTOPILOT_WORKFLOW_CONFIG`, `AUTOPILOT_LAUNCH_TEMPLATE`, `AUTOPILOT_ORCHESTRATOR_CONFIG`, `SESSION_ROLES`, and `get_session_id()` (`src/autopilot/phases.py:48`) — the deterministic pi session-ID hash that lets a goto back to a phase resume the same agent session (model is folded into the hash so a model change doesn't silently resume a pinned stale model, `src/autopilot/phases.py:60-73`).

`src/autopilot/spec.py` is the **hybrid completion gate**: it reads each gated phase's OKF report (`.hephaestus/<phase>/…md`, parsed by `src/autopilot/okf_markdown.py`) and turns BLOCKER/verdict/pass-rate signals into a numeric `score` (0.0–1.0) that `_fire_phase_transition` feeds into `PhaseManager`'s engine evaluation to decide continue/goto/retry. `src/autopilot/okf_markdown.py` is a tiny, self-contained frontmatter+body reader/writer with no orchestrator-specific logic.

`src/autopilot/service.py` is the in-process asyncio wrapper (`AutopilotService`) that used to be a subprocess — it owns per-project start/stop/pause-for-restart lifecycle, persisted "was running" state, and a `AutopilotServiceRegistry` enforcing `max_concurrent_projects` via an atomic reserve/release protocol (`try_reserve`/`release_reservation`, `src/autopilot/service.py:505-537`) that closes a TOCTOU race plain `can_start()` has on its own.

### 2. Key Entry Points

**Called by other subsystems into this module:**

| Caller | Entry point | Location |
|---|---|---|
| `AutopilotService._run_pipeline_sync` (service.py) | `run_continuous_pipeline(args)` | `src/autopilot/orchestrator/__init__.py:2861` |
| CLI (`python -m src.autopilot.orchestrator`) | `main()` → `run_continuous_pipeline` | `src/autopilot/orchestrator/__init__.py:3408` |
| `server.py`'s `background_phase_advancement_sweep` | `_try_advance_phases(workflow_id, logger)` and `_maybe_resolve_arbitration(workflow_id, logger)` | `:185`, `:6307` |
| `server.py` background sweeps | `sweep_completed_workflow_worktrees`, `heal_orphaned_agent_branches`, `_sync_stale_feature_statuses`, `_sync_stale_design_statuses`, `_resync_pipeline_registry`, `_recover_abandoned_workflows_missing_worktree`, `_recover_abandoned_workflows_with_completed_phase`, `_retry_exhausted_paused_workflows` | `:2953`, `:3061`, `:3396`, `:3487`, `:3543`, `:3624`, `:3742`, `:3825` |
| `task_completion_service.py` (spec-gate immediate-fire path) | `_advance_phases`/`_fire_phase_transition` indirectly, plus `build_phase_output` from spec.py | via `src.autopilot.spec` |
| `PhaseManager._complete_workflow` / review-approve endpoint (out-of-band Phase 0 completion) | `finalize_phase0_workflow(workflow_id, logger, project_id, skip_review_gate)` | `:8595` |
| `autopilot_api.py` (POST /start's concurrency-cap check, `rerun_design`) | `_get_or_create_project_id(project_path)` | `:398` |
| `cost_derivation.py` (budget enforcement) | `pause_project_workflows(db, project_id, paused_by, definition_ids)` | `:924` |

**This module calls OUT to:**

| Target | Call | Location |
|---|---|---|
| `src.phases.PhaseManager` (phase-evaluation engine) | `pm.mark_phase_complete(phase_id, …, phase_output=…)` — the only place phase transitions are decided | `:5811-5821`, `:6452-6462` (arbitration outcomes), `:6291-6295` (dispatch-failure force-fail) |
| `src.core.app_context.get_app_state().agent_manager` | `create_agent_for_task(...)` (async, run via `asyncio.run` inside a background thread) | `create_agent_for_task_direct`, `:1162-1175` |
| `src.core.worktree_manager.WorktreeManager` | worktree create/remove, branch merge, staleness checks | `_create_integration_worktree` (`:2786`), `_cleanup_worktree` (`:2854`), final-merge block in `run_single_workflow` (`:7849-7893`) |
| `src.monitoring.signals.get_signal_queue()` | consumes Guardian/Conductor stuck-pattern signals into the impasse check | `run_single_workflow`, `:7918-7947` |
| `src.autopilot.spec.build_phase_output` / `GATED_PHASES` | scores a gated phase's OKF report into the engine decision | `_fire_phase_transition` `:5803-5806`, `_phase_currently_passes` `:6034-6036` |
| `src.core.cost_derivation.check_budget_before_new_work` | budget gate before launching a design or a feature | `pick_next_design` `:2451`, `_run_one_feature` `:8818`, `:8876` |
| `src.sdk.HephaestusSDK` | `sdk.start_workflow(...)`, `sdk.start(...)`, `sdk.shutdown(...)` | `run_single_workflow` `:7612`, `run_continuous_pipeline` `:7771` and `:10178` |
| `scripts/ash` (AWS Automated Security Helper) | mandatory pre-agent security scan for `security_review` | `_run_ash_scan` `:6562` |

### 3. The Main Control Loop(s)

There are three nested loops, each with a distinct responsibility, plus a background sweep that runs in parallel with all of them.

#### 3.1 `run_continuous_pipeline` — the design-queue loop (`:9634`)

Step-by-step, one iteration of the `while True` body (`:9816`):

1. **Stop check** — `_should_stop(current_project_id)` (`:9529`), keyed per-project via module-level `_stop_events: Dict[str, asyncio.Event]` (`:9526`) so two concurrently-running projects (`AutopilotServiceRegistry`) can never steal each other's stop signal.
2. Every `DESIGN_QUEUE_SCAN_INTERVAL` (60s, `:106`):
   a. `get_active_workflows(project_path, project_id=...)` (`:1372`) then `_escalate_stale_active_workflows` (`:1475`) — self-heals a workflow that's been `active` with zero agent/task activity for `STALE_ACTIVE_WORKFLOW_CONSECUTIVE_CHECKS` (10) consecutive scans by marking it `failed` (`:1518-1524`), so a lost-mid-restart workflow doesn't block the queue forever. Anything with real activity resets its streak.
   b. If still blocked and no other design has resumable work (`_has_resumable_active_design`, `:2370`), sleep and `continue`.
   c. Re-checks `state.current_workflow_id` belongs to *this* project (`_workflow_belongs_to_project`, `:1321`) before trusting it — cross-project leakage here previously force-failed an unrelated project's paused workflow (documented incident at `:9903-9906`).
   d. `is_design_fully_complete(state.current_workflow_id, logger)` (`:1538`) — checks workflow status, no pending/active tasks (excluding diagnostic tasks), no unresolved failed tasks, no active agents, no unmerged `agent-*` branches, and done-task count vs. real phase count.
   e. If not complete: `attempt_recovery` (`:1868`) — retries failed tasks (delegates to `_retry_failed_tasks`, `:1643`), cleans stale `assigned`/`in_progress` tasks pointing at terminated agents, cleans a dirty repo (`git merge --abort` / `reset --hard` / `clean -fd`), and terminates only *genuinely dead* agents (tmux session liveness check, `:1991-2004` — an earlier version killed every "working" agent unconditionally and was observed live killing a `security_review` agent three times in six minutes). Capped at 5 `_recovery_attempts` before escalating to force-`failed` (`:9958-9988`).
   f. Otherwise: `pick_next_design(queue_dir, processed_hashes, logger, project_id)` (`:2406`) — reads from `AutopilotDesign` DB rows first (falls back to file scan), prioritizes an already-`active` design with incomplete features over a brand-new `pending` one (documented regression this fixes: a design whose only blocking dependency just finished could otherwise starve behind a new design forever, `:2462-2474`), and retries a design whose latest workflow `failed` up to `MAX_DESIGN_RETRIES` (3, `:121`) before giving up.
   g. `run_single_design(sdk, next_design, project_path, logger, state, max_iterations, project_id)` (`:9465`) — the 3-stage coordinator (Phase 0 → feature pipelines → aggregate report), described below.
3. Checkpointing: `persistent_state.save(state, processed_hashes)` is called up to 6 times per outer loop iteration at various checkpoints (`:9851`, `:10001`, `:10020`, `:10037`, `:10051`, `:10105`) so the `/autopilot/status` endpoint's live view doesn't go stale for the entire duration of a design run (documented rationale on `save_state_only`, `:596-624`).
4. `finally:` — persists final state, force-pauses only *this project's* active workflows (`:10165-10175`, scoped the same way as (c) above), and calls `sdk.shutdown(graceful=True, timeout=15)`.

`run_single_design` (`:9465`) is a thin 3-stage coordinator:
- **Stage 1** `run_phase0(...)` (`:7997`) → `features_json`, `designs_folder`.
- **Stage 2** `run_feature_pipelines(...)` (`:9077`) → `Dict[feature_key, status]`.
- **Stage 3** `run_design_aggregate(...)` (`:9229`) → `(DesignStatus, FeatureReport)`.

`run_phase0` (`:7997`) has a 3-tier idempotency ladder before doing any real work: **Tier 1** — `Feature` rows already exist for the design → skip entirely (`:8032-8043`); **Tier 1.5** — Phase 0's own workflow is currently paused for review → re-enter the wait directly (`:8053-8067`); **Tier 2** — Phase 0's workflow already reached `completed` in the DB but `_create_feature_records` never ran (crash between the two) → resume from the persisted `designs_folder/features.json`, falling back to reading it out of the `feature_architect/<design_id>` git branch if the worktree is already gone (`:8076-8142`). Only past all three tiers does it create a fresh integration worktree and call `run_single_workflow(sdk, "feature_architect", ...)`.

`run_feature_pipelines` (`:9077`) resolves a dependency-ordered execution plan via `_resolve_execution_order` (Kahn's algorithm with parallel/sequential batching, `:4262`), then runs each layer either inline (single feature) or via `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=MAX_PARALLEL_FEATURES=4)` for a parallel batch, calling `_run_one_feature` (`:8744`) per feature. A non-terminal result (`interrupted`/`timeout`) halts the walk before dispatching later dependency layers (`:9205-9219`) — deliberately does **not** treat `failed`/`skipped` as halting, since a failed dependency's dependents used to be permanently skipped even if a later retry of the dependency succeeded (`:9135-9143`).

#### 3.2 `run_single_workflow` — per-workflow poll loop (`:7403`)

Launches (or resumes, via `existing_workflow_id`) one `Workflow` execution and polls it to a terminal outcome. Per `POLL_INTERVAL` (15s, `:104`) tick inside the `while True` (`:7720`):
1. `_should_stop(project_id)` check → `"interrupted"`.
2. Timeout check against `timeout_seconds` (default from `hephaestus_config.yaml`, `_get_workflow_timeout`, `:64`) → `"timeout"`.
3. Snapshot workflow/agent/task counts, log phase transitions (`_log_phase_transitions`, `:7683`) and agent spawn/terminate events.
4. **`_try_advance_phases(exec_id, logger)`** (`:7770`) — the actual phase-progression call; everything above/below is monitoring/bookkeeping.
5. **Re-fetch all counts** (`:7777-7789`) — `_advance_phases` may have just created a new task+agent, so the pre-advance snapshot is stale; without this refresh the loop could see "no agents, no work" one line before the new task appears and prematurely declare the workflow done or stuck (fixed by commit `054b73a`).
6. Terminal check: `wf_state in ("completed", "failed", "paused")` → return that status directly (`:7807-7810`).
7. "Looks done" check: no active agents, no pending/in-progress/non-terminal tasks → verify no `PhaseExecution` is still `pending`/`in_progress` (`:7829-7845`) before declaring `"completed"`, then does the **final merge** of the shared design branch into `main` (`:7849-7893`, aborts and preserves the branch on conflict rather than failing hard).
8. Credit-exhaustion check (`check_api_credits`, `:2017`) and monitor-signal consumption (`:7918-7931`) feed into `detect_hard_error`/`detect_impasse` (`:2075`, `:2094`), which escalate to `prompt_human` (`:2175`) after `STUCK_THRESHOLD` (3) consecutive impasse observations.
9. `finally:` — always terminates any agent still `working`/`idle`/`starting` for this workflow and pauses it if left `active` (`:7976-7994`) — this is why `pause_existing` and cross-project scoping matter so much at the launch step (`:7480-7519`): this cleanup is destructive and must never hit an unrelated project's live agents.

`_register_monitored_workflow`/`_unregister_monitored_workflow`/`_is_workflow_monitored` (`:158-168`) mark a workflow as "actively polled by this loop" so the background sweep can skip it — see §6.

#### 3.3 `_advance_phases` — the phase state machine (`:4525`)

This is the single source of truth for phase progression, called both from `run_single_workflow`'s poll loop and the background sweep, always through the `_try_advance_phases` mutex wrapper (§6). Body, in order:

1. If `wf.status == "failed"`, force-fail any lingering `pending`/`in_progress`/`assigned` task and return (`:4547-4562`).
2. Bail if `wf.status not in ("active", "paused")` (`:4564-4565`).
3. If `paused`, try `_try_auto_resume_paused_workflow` (`:4634`) — un-pauses only if `paused_by in (None, "system")` (never a user/budget/review pause) **and** the stalled phase already has a `done` task.
4. Two workflow-wide self-heals run before phase statuses are even read: `_release_stale_task_creation_claims` (`:4688`) clears any `task_creation_claimed_at` held past `CLAIM_STALE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (480s, `:140`); `_release_pending_phases_with_done_tasks` (`:4752`) flips at most one `PhaseExecution` stuck `pending` back to `in_progress` if it already has a `done` task and no other phase is currently `in_progress`.
5. Self-heals a `done` task that also carries a `failure_reason` (gate validation rejected it after completion) back to `failed` for a clean retry (`:4585-4600`).
6. `_get_phase_statuses(db, workflow_id)` (`:4830`), then dispatches through **four cases in priority order**, each returning `Optional[bool]` (`None` = doesn't apply):
   - **Case 0** `_case_start_first_phase` (`:4942`) — no in-progress/completed phase, first `pending` phase exists → claim + `_create_phase_task`.
   - **Case 0b** `_case_in_progress_no_tasks` (`:4968`) — an `in_progress` phase has zero tasks (the engine set the status but a task was never created) → claim + `_create_phase_task`.
   - **Case 1** `_case_completed_with_successor` (`:4999`) — last completed phase has a pending successor never started. Honors the completing task's own `action`/`action_target_phase` (a goto/retry bypassing intermediate phases) over "next by order" (`:5009-5044`) — without this, a `development`→`adversarial_review` goto would re-trigger an already-passed `architectural_review` every cycle (documented live incident). Scopes "already fired" detection to tasks created since the *predecessor's* `completed_at`, not all-time (`:5054-5071`) — an unscoped check let `product_validation` stall 2+ days because its only task was a 3-week-old `done` row.
   - **Case 2** `_case_in_progress_complete` (`:5154`, ~400 lines, by far the largest case) — for each `in_progress` phase: skip if claimed elsewhere (arbitration in flight); mark orphaned `pending` tasks (>1 min old, never dispatched or dispatched to a dead agent) as `failed` (`:5252-5292`); mark tasks past `max_task_retries` as `failed` (`:5302-5318`); if no active tasks remain and `done_count == 0` but the cycle itself has zero tasks (stale `started_at`), dispatch a fresh one (`:5359-5364`); else `_maybe_retry_failed_tasks` (`:5556`); if `done_count > 0` but there are also `failed` tasks in the same cycle, retry the retryable ones directly (inline, not via `_maybe_retry_failed_tasks`, `:5401-5492`) or fail the phase+workflow if all are past cap (`:5480-5491`, closing a bug where an exhausted-retry phase silently advanced as if it had passed); otherwise claim + `_fire_phase_transition` (`:5516-5525`).

All four cases funnel into either `_create_phase_task` (`:6714`) for a brand-new/continuing dispatch or `_fire_phase_transition` (`:5755`) for an evaluate-and-transition. `_fire_phase_transition` calls `PhaseManager.mark_phase_complete`, reads the resulting `action` (`continue`/`goto`/`retry`/`arbitrate`/`already_completed`), and for `goto`/`retry` prefers the gate's own specific finding (`spec_gate.reason`) over the generic workflow.yaml condition text (`:5844-5875`) — this is the mechanism that lets a `development` task see "6 BLOCKER(s) found: …" instead of a bare "returning to development."

### 4. State Machine

| Entity | Values | Set by (file:line) |
|---|---|---|
| `Workflow.status` | `active`, `paused`, `completed`, `failed` (`WorkflowStatus`, `src/core/database.py:75`) | `active`→`paused`: `pause_workflow_direct` `:872`, `pause_project_workflows` `:924`, `_pause_for_manual_handoff` `:5128`, `_maybe_retry_failed_tasks` exhausted-cap `:5637-5643`, `_pause_feature_for_review`/`_pause_phase0_for_review` `:8439`/`:8525`. `paused`→`active`: `_try_auto_resume_paused_workflow` `:4634`, `_retry_exhausted_paused_workflows` `:3825`, `_get_or_create_project_id`'s user-pause clear `:488`. `active`/`paused`→`failed`: `fail_workflow_direct` `:902`, `_escalate_stale_active_workflows` `:1518-1524`, `_advance_phases`'s all-past-retry-cap branch `:5484-5491`, `_trigger_arbitration`'s cap-exhausted branch `:6178-6184`. →`completed`: `complete_workflow_direct` `:888`, PhaseManager (out of scope for this section) via `mark_phase_complete`. |
| `Workflow.paused_by` | `None`, `"user"`, `"budget"`, `"review"`, `"system"`, `"system-exhausted"` | user stop → `pause_workflow_direct`/`pause_project_workflows`; budget → `pause_project_workflows`; review → `_pause_feature_for_review`/`_pause_phase0_for_review`; system → `_maybe_retry_failed_tasks` `:5640`; system-exhausted → `_retry_exhausted_paused_workflows` `:3880`; manual-only phase → `_pause_for_manual_handoff` `:5147`. |
| `Feature.status` | `pending`, `active`, `paused`, `completed`, `failed`, `skipped` (`FeatureStatus`, `database.py:86`) | `_update_feature_status` `:3359` is the single write path; also `pause_project_workflows` `:980`, `_sync_stale_feature_statuses` self-heal `:3396`, `_run_one_feature`'s dependency-skip `:8800-8804`. |
| `PhaseExecution.status` | `pending`, `in_progress`, `completed`, `failed`, `skipped` (`PhaseExecutionStatus`, `database.py:100`) | →`in_progress`: `_release_phase_task_creation_claim` `:4929`, `_create_phase_task` `:7004`, `_trigger_arbitration` (kept alive during arbitration) `:6202`. →`failed`: `_advance_phases`'s all-past-cap branch `:5482`. →`completed`/other: `PhaseManager.mark_phase_complete` (out of this section's scope). |
| `Task.status` | `pending`, `queued`, `blocked`, `assigned`, `in_progress`, `under_review`, `validation_in_progress`, `needs_work`, `done`, `failed`, `duplicated` (`TaskStatus`, `database.py:51`) | orchestrator.py touches `pending`/`in_progress`/`failed`/`duplicated` extensively — e.g. `update_task_status` `:797`, `_retry_failed_tasks` `:1643`, `_maybe_retry_failed_tasks` `:5556`, `_create_phase_task` `:6714`. `done` is set by the agent's own `update_task_status` call path (outside this module). |
| `AutopilotDesign.status` | `pending`, `queued`, `processing`, `decomposing`, `active`, `completed`, `failed`, `skipped`, `validated` | `_update_design_status` `:3947` is the single write path; also `pick_next_design`'s retry/give-up logic `:2541-2586`, `_sync_stale_design_statuses` self-heal `:3487`. |
| `AutopilotProject.is_active` | bool | `_get_or_create_project_id` `:444-470`, capped by `max_concurrent_projects` (never unconditionally evicts another project — documented CLAUDE.md invariant). |

### 5. Goto / Arbitration / Retry / Review-Mode Logic

**Retry** (task-level, within a phase): `_retry_failed_tasks` (`:1643`, sweep-scoped, called from `attempt_recovery`) and `_maybe_retry_failed_tasks` (`:5556`, called from within `_case_in_progress_complete` when *all* tasks in the current cycle are failed). Both read `max_task_retries` from `workflow.yaml` (default 5) via `load_workflow_definition`, and both treat orphaned/session-limit/stuck-task failures as always-retryable regardless of count (`_limit_failure`/`_stuck_failure` lambdas, `:5429-5437`, `:5604-5611`). Session-limit failures resolve a fallback CLI/model on retry (`:5705-5730`).

**Goto** (phase-level, engine-driven): `PhaseManager.mark_phase_complete` decides `action="goto"` based on `workflow.yaml`'s `evaluation_points` and the score `spec.py`'s `build_phase_output` computes. `_fire_phase_transition` (`:5755`) is where the decision becomes a dispatched task — `action_target_phase` on the *creating* task threads the goto's origin/destination through `_create_phase_task`'s `feedback`/`source_phase_name` params so the destination phase's task description embeds the `GOTO_REASON_PREFIX`-tagged reason (`:6976-6979`).

**Bounds on retry/goto**: `_create_phase_task` counts historical `retry`/`goto` tasks for a phase (created_by `"orchestrator"`) against `max_phase_attempts = 5` (`:6908-6929`); past that, it calls `_trigger_arbitration` instead of dispatching again.

**Review-run cap** (distinct budget, per-phase re-entry count rather than the goto-target's retry count): opt-in via `workflow.yaml`'s `max_review_runs` (`get_max_review_runs`, `spec.py:266`). `_create_phase_task` checks `run_count >= max_review_runs` (`:6947-6955`) and calls `_cap_out_review_phase` (`:6627`), which writes a **synthetic clean result** (`synthetic_clean_result`, `spec.py:1220` — schema-aware per phase, since a one-size-fits-all `{"blocker_count": 0}` scored `qa_validation` as a 0% pass rate, the worst possible outcome, in a documented live incident) plus the accumulated findings history, then fires the transition with `force_continue=True`. `force_continue` exists specifically because a *normal* `mark_phase_complete` call re-runs the engine's own retry-count gate, which (since `max_retries` and `max_review_runs` are commonly configured identically) has *already* tripped by the time the review-run cap engages — routing straight back to arbitration instead of past the cap (documented live incident: `design_review` capped at 4 runs, arbitrated to `architecture_design`, which re-ran and immediately re-hit the same cap, for hours). See `:5762-5782`.

**Arbitration** (`:5893-6511`): triggered when the engine returns `action="arbitrate"` (retry/goto budget exhausted) or `_create_phase_task`'s own `max_phase_attempts` bound trips. `_trigger_arbitration` spawns a one-shot LLM agent (`agent_type="diagnostic"` — not a real `"arbitration"` type, since `Agent.agent_type`'s CHECK constraint doesn't include one; `Task.created_by_agent_id == ARBITRATION_CREATED_BY = "arbitration"` is what actually identifies an arbitration task, `:6254-6276`) with the phase's recent task history (`_gather_arbitration_context`, `:5909`) and a prompt (`_build_arbitration_prompt`, `:5929`) instructing it to write `continue`/`goto`/`fail` to `arbitration_result.json`. Hard-capped at `max_arbitrations_per_phase = 3` since a "goto" decision re-enters the same `max_phase_attempts` budget and a confused arbiter could otherwise loop forever (`:6070-6078`). `_maybe_resolve_arbitration` (`:6307`, called every sweep tick) picks up a finished/failed arbitration task and calls `_resolve_arbitration_outcome` (`:6417`), which — critically — must itself call `_create_phase_task` for the decided next phase, since `mark_phase_complete` **never** creates tasks itself for any action (documented at `:6431-6443`; an earlier version discarded the return value and silently stranded the workflow).

**Review mode**: a per-project `AutopilotProject.review_mode` flag (`_should_pause_for_review`, `:8428`) gates two independent pause points — after each feature completes (`_pause_feature_for_review`/`_wait_for_review_clearance`, `:8439`/`:8465`, called from `_run_one_feature` `:8995-8997`, `:9062-9064`) and after Phase 0's decomposition (`_pause_phase0_for_review`/`_wait_for_phase0_review_clearance`, `:8525`/`:8554`, called from `run_phase0` `:8401-8408` and `finalize_phase0_workflow` `:8677-8684`). Both poll every 30s and respect `_should_stop`. `MANUAL_ONLY_PHASES = {"git_commit_push"}` (`:5106`) reuses the same `review_mode` flag via `_manual_handoff_required` (`:5109`) so a pipeline in review mode never autonomously commits/pushes — `_pause_for_manual_handoff` (`:5128`) parks the workflow instead of treating `AgentManager`'s intentional `PermissionError` rejection as an ordinary agent failure (which would otherwise retry forever, starving the queue). In full autopilot (`review_mode=False`), `git_commit_push` dispatches exactly like any other phase.

### 6. Concurrency & Idempotency Mechanisms

- **`_advance_phases` mutual exclusion**: `_try_advance_phases` (`:185`) holds a per-`workflow_id` `threading.Lock` (`_advance_phases_locks`, `:181`) so the background sweep and `run_single_workflow`'s inline call can never both be inside `_advance_phases` for the same workflow simultaneously; `_actively_monitored_workflows` (`:154-168`) is a cheaper, advisory-only optimization letting the sweep skip a workflow entirely rather than contend the lock.
- **`task_creation_claimed_at` claim** (`PhaseExecution` column, `database.py:561`): `_claim_phase_task_creation` (`:4847`) is a single `UPDATE … WHERE task_creation_claimed_at IS NULL`, atomic under SQLite's write serialization — closes a race between server.py's synchronous "create initial task" step and the orchestrator's own background self-heal that previously spawned duplicate task+agent pairs for the same phase. `_release_phase_task_creation_claim` (`:4880`) releases it and flips `pending`/`completed` → `in_progress`; uses `.populate_existing()` because `expire_on_commit=False` (StaticPool) means a `PhaseExecution` already loaded earlier in the same session is a stale cached object that a plain re-query would return unchanged, silently no-opping the release (documented, caught by `test_maybe_retry_failed_tasks_is_claim_protected`). The claim is reused for a *second* purpose — guarding `_case_in_progress_complete`'s evaluate-and-transition step against a concurrent re-entry while `mark_phase_complete`'s LLM-backed evaluation is in flight (`:5516-5551`).
- **Stale-claim self-heal**: `_release_stale_task_creation_claims` (`:4688`) clears any claim held past `CLAIM_STALE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (480s) — deliberately shorter than `STALE_ACTIVE_WORKFLOW_CONSECUTIVE_CHECKS * DESIGN_QUEUE_SCAN_INTERVAL` (600s) so a stuck-claim-only problem gets a chance to self-heal before the coarser stale-workflow escalation kills the whole workflow (`:140-144`).
- **`target_already_claimed` parameter** on `_create_phase_task` (`:6733-6749`): callers dispatching to the *same* phase they already hold a claim on (`_case_start_first_phase`, `_case_in_progress_no_tasks`, `_case_completed_with_successor`, `_case_in_progress_complete`'s empty-cycle branch) pass `True` and skip re-claiming; callers dispatching to a *different* target phase (`_fire_phase_transition`, `_resolve_arbitration_outcome`) leave it `False` so the function claims the target itself — closes a race where a slow `create_agent_for_task_direct` dispatch (worktree setup, tmux launch) looked like "pending, no agent yet" to a second caller within the 1-minute orphan-detection window, producing two goto tasks on the same target phase 85 seconds apart (documented live incident).
- **Sibling-task dedup**: `create_agent_for_task_direct` (`:1014`) checks for another active `Task` on the same `phase_id` created by `None`/the orchestrator before dispatching (`:1084-1107`); `_retry_failed_tasks` does the identical check before *and* after its own dispatch attempt (`:1729-1754`, `:1787-1820`), both scoped to `phase_id` being non-NULL — an unscoped SQLAlchemy `Task.phase_id == phase_id` on a NULL value compiles to `IS NULL`, which previously matched every phase-less debris task in the database regardless of age or workflow (fixed by commit `54d1814`).
- **CLI/model concurrency gate**: `create_agent_for_task_direct` calls `queue_service.resolve_cli_model_dispatch` (`:1126-1157`) to respect a per-CLI/model concurrency limit even on the orchestrator's own direct dispatch path, which otherwise entirely bypassed `QueueService`'s equivalent check.
- **Upsert instead of read-then-write**: `_set_project_context` (`:344`) uses SQLite's `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` rather than a `filter_by().first()`-then-insert sequence, closing a TOCTOU window on `ProjectContext.key`'s unique constraint.
- **`AutopilotServiceRegistry.try_reserve`/`release_reservation`** (`service.py:505-537`): atomic check-and-reserve under `threading.Lock` for the `max_concurrent_projects` cap, closing the same class of TOCTOU race as `try_reserve`'s own docstring documents for `can_start()`.

### 7. Known Fragile Spots / Code Smells

- **File size and function size**: `orchestrator.py` is 10,246 lines — one file holding queue scanning, worktree management, phase state machine, arbitration, review-mode gating, and the top-level CLI. Several functions are individually large: `_case_in_progress_complete` is ~400 lines (`:5154-5553`), `run_single_workflow` is ~590 lines (`:7403-7995`), `run_continuous_pipeline` is ~545 lines (`:9634-10179`), `_create_phase_task` is ~360 lines (`:6714-7076`), `run_phase0` is ~425 lines (`:7997-8422`). Each of these mixes multiple concerns (DB reads/writes, dispatch, logging, error handling) inline rather than delegating to smaller named helpers, making the control flow hard to follow and every future fix another inline patch.
- **Patch-on-patch accumulation is visible in the comments themselves**: a very large fraction of the file's prose is "observed live: …" incident post-mortems justifying a specific defensive check (dozens of examples throughout §3–§6 above). This is valuable historical context, but it also means the actual logic is now interleaved with narrative — e.g. `_case_in_progress_complete` alone documents at least 6 distinct historical bugs inline (orphaned pending tasks, terminated-agent pending tasks, retry-cap-exceeded pending tasks, empty-cycle stale `started_at`, all-failed-with-some-done, and the evaluate-race claim) rather than each being a separably-testable unit.
- **Duplicated retry logic**: `_retry_failed_tasks` (`:1643`, sweep-level, no phase claim) and `_maybe_retry_failed_tasks` (`:5556`, phase-claim-protected, called from within `_case_in_progress_complete`) implement near-identical logic (max-retry-cap read from `workflow.yaml`, orphan/session-limit/stuck-failure exemptions, per-task `enriched_description` rewriting) with materially different claim-safety guarantees. `_case_in_progress_complete` *also* has a third, inline, near-duplicate retry path for the "done + failed coexist" case (`:5401-5492`) that reimplements the same retryable-task filtering logic a third time instead of calling either helper.
- **`_create_phase_task`'s special-casing**: phase-name string comparisons (`if phase_name == "security_review"`, `"forensics_analysis"`, `"deploy"`, `:6787-6823`) hardcode per-phase behavior directly into the generic task-creation path rather than as declarative phase config (contrast with `spec.py`'s `GATED_PHASES`/`OPTIONAL_PHASES`, which *are* YAML-driven). Adding a new phase with special pre-dispatch behavior requires editing this function directly.
- **Two near-identical arbitration-outcome dispatchers**: `_fire_phase_transition` and `_resolve_arbitration_outcome` both end by calling `_create_phase_task` with almost the same argument shape, but the intervening logic (feedback extraction, `result_missing` special-casing at `:5857-5875`) lives only in `_fire_phase_transition`, so a future gate-feedback improvement there is easy to forget to mirror in the arbitration path.
- **Legacy HTTP helpers left in place**: `api_get`/`api_post` (`:773`, `:784`) are explicitly marked "Legacy HTTP GET — prefer direct DB access functions below" but are still defined and presumably still reachable; every other function in the file was migrated to direct DB access ("H-2 fix" comments throughout, e.g. `:797`, `:831`, `:872`) but these two were left behind rather than removed.
- **`MAX_WORKFLOW_TIME` / `MAX_PHASE0_TIME` module constants are dead**: both are explicitly commented `# deprecated: use config` (`:110`, `:119`) and superseded by `_get_workflow_timeout()`/`_get_phase0_timeout()`, but remain defined and could still be imported elsewhere.
- **`SWEEP_ENABLED = False`**: `_sweep_stray_files` (`:4399`) and its supporting `_SWEEP_REPORT_NAMES`/`_STRAY_DIRS` constants are fully implemented but permanently disabled (`:4374`, comment: "Enable once the allowlist has been validated in production") — dead code path carried for an indeterminate future.
- **Inconsistent async/sync bridging**: `create_agent_for_task_direct` uses `asyncio.run(...)` to bridge into the async `AgentManager.create_agent_for_task` (`:1162`), while `_resync_pipeline_registry` explicitly avoids `asyncio.run` in favor of `run_coroutine_threadsafe` onto the server's persistent loop because `asyncio.run` would silently orphan `AutopilotService`'s long-lived background task (`:3560-3566`). Both patterns coexist in the same file for structurally similar "call async code from a sync context" problems, with the choice between them undocumented anywhere except the one function's own comment.
- **`OrchestratorLogger` is a bespoke logging shim** (`:712-764`) — a hand-rolled file-append logger with its own lock, distinct from the module-level stdlib `logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)` (`:58`) used everywhere else in the file. Nearly every function takes a `logger: OrchestratorLogger` parameter threaded manually through dozens of call chains, in addition to (or instead of) the stdlib logger some helper functions use directly — two parallel logging paths through one module.
- **`_advance_phases`'s `except Exception` catch-all** (`:4629-4631`) swallows *any* exception raised anywhere in the 100+ lines of case dispatch above it into a single `logger.warning`, then returns `False` — silently absorbing bugs like the `UnboundLocalError` documented inline at `:5792-5801` (a redundant local `from pathlib import Path` shadowed the module-level import and permanently broke gated-phase advancement until diagnosed from log noise alone).

### 8. Refactor Observations

1. **No single, testable "phase transition decision" boundary.** The decision of what happens next (continue/goto/retry/arbitrate/fail) is scattered across `PhaseManager.mark_phase_complete` (external), `_fire_phase_transition`'s post-processing of that result (feedback extraction, `result_missing` override), `_cap_out_review_phase`'s synthetic bypass, and `_resolve_arbitration_outcome`'s separate re-implementation of "dispatch whatever the decision was." A refactor should collapse these into one function that always returns `(action, target_phase_id, feedback)` and a single dispatcher that always calls `_create_phase_task` from it — removing the current duplication between `_fire_phase_transition` and `_resolve_arbitration_outcome`.
2. **Task-creation claim + retry logic is three parallel implementations** (`_retry_failed_tasks`, `_maybe_retry_failed_tasks`, and the inline retry block in `_case_in_progress_complete`) that should be one function parameterized by claim-safety, not three independently-maintained copies of the same max-retry/orphan/session-limit filtering.
3. **`_advance_phases`'s four cases are order-dependent functions returning `Optional[bool]` as a sentinel for "didn't apply."** This is a hand-rolled pattern-match; a real state-machine table (phase status × task status → action) would make it possible to unit-test each transition in isolation instead of requiring a full DB-backed integration test per case, and would eliminate the risk (already realized multiple times per the git history) of a phase falling into a gap between cases where no case recognizes its state.
4. **Phase-name string literals drive special-case behavior in three separate functions** (`_create_phase_task`'s `security_review`/`forensics_analysis`/`deploy` branches, `MANUAL_ONLY_PHASES`, `GATED_PHASES`/`OPTIONAL_PHASES` in spec.py). These should be unified into one phase-capability declaration (already partially done via YAML `spec_gate`/`optional_phases`/`required_output`) so adding a new phase-specific behavior doesn't require editing orchestrator.py's control flow directly.
5. **`run_continuous_pipeline` conflates queue-loop orchestration, workflow-recovery, and DB status-sync bookkeeping in one 545-line function** with 6+ separate `persistent_state.save()` checkpoints scattered through it. The recovery logic (steps in §3.1.d–e) is substantial enough to be its own named function, separable from the queue-scan/design-dispatch loop body.
6. **Two independent worktree-cleanup owners with overlapping responsibility**: `_cleanup_worktree` (called from `run_phase0`, `_run_one_feature`), `sweep_completed_workflow_worktrees` (background sweep catching workflows that reached `completed` without their normal cleanup running), and `_archive_and_cleanup` (a third, apparently-unused-by-the-main-path variant at `:9405` with its own artifact-copy logic duplicating `_cleanup_worktree`'s tmux-transcript archiving). A refactor should establish one worktree-lifecycle owner with the sweep as its sole self-heal, rather than three separate implementations of "copy artifacts out, then remove."
## Phase Management

### Overview of the moving parts

Four layers cooperate to run a workflow's phase graph, and they don't live in one place:

| Layer | File | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Data models | `src/phases/models.py` | `PhaseContext` (runtime view handed to prompt assembly) and `PhasesConfig` (legacy `phases_config.yaml` shape) |
| YAML loading (thin) | `src/phases/phase_loader.py` | Back-compat facade — delegates to the real loader |
| YAML loading (real) | `src/workflow_engine/yaml_loader.py` | Reads `workflow.yaml` + per-phase `*.yaml`, builds `Phase`/`WorkflowDefinition` (sdk) objects |
| YAML validation | `src/workflow_engine/config_validator.py` | Startup-time schema/cross-reference checks, run from inside the loader |
| Gate evaluator | `src/workflow_engine/orchestrator.py` (`WorkflowOrchestrator`) | Pure condition evaluator — decides continue/retry/goto/arbitrate/fail from a score |
| Runtime orchestration | `src/phases/phase_manager.py` (`PhaseManager`) | DB-backed phase/execution lifecycle, dispatches to `WorkflowOrchestrator`, applies its decision |
| Periodic sweep + task creation | `src/autopilot/orchestrator.py` (huge, ~9900 lines) | Polls DB state, creates phase tasks, holds the task-creation-claim locks, drives arbitration |
| Synchronous fast path | `src/services/task_completion_service.py` | Fires the same transition the moment a gated phase's last task completes, instead of waiting for the sweep |

This split is itself notable: "phase management" as a concept is not owned by `src/phases/` alone. `src/phases/phase_manager.py` owns the DB-row lifecycle and evaluation dispatch, but the actual gate logic lives in `src/workflow_engine/orchestrator.py`, and task-creation/race-avoidance lives almost entirely in `src/autopilot/orchestrator.py`. A reader who only opens `src/phases/` will not find the code that closes the concurrency bugs the git log is full of.

---

### 1. Purpose & responsibilities

**`src/phases/models.py`**
- `PhaseContext` (`models.py:38-102`) — the runtime object handed to prompt assembly. Carries `phase_id`, `workflow_id`, the current `Phase` (sdk dataclass), `all_phases`, `current_status`, and task counts. Its `to_prompt_context()` (`models.py:57-102`) is the **only** place in the whole prompt path that surfaces a phase's `additional_notes` (per-phase step-by-step instructions) — the docstring explicitly flags this as a previously-missing wire-up (an agent had "no idea an ASH automated scan step existed").
- `PhasesConfig` (`models.py:105-154`) — legacy shape for `phases_config.yaml` (`has_result`, `result_criteria`, `on_result_found`, `enable_tickets`, `board_config`). Validated via a pydantic `field_validator` (`models.py:129-136`) that requires `result_criteria` when `has_result=True`.

**`src/phases/phase_loader.py`** — deliberately thin. `PhaseLoader.load_phases_from_folder` (`phase_loader.py:23-53`) just validates the folder exists and calls `load_full_workflow_definition` from `src/workflow_engine/yaml_loader.py` (`phase_loader.py:51`). `PhaseLoader.load_phases_config` (`phase_loader.py:55-98`) is a separate, legacy path that reads `phases_config.yaml` directly if present — this is a **second, independent config source** for the same kind of information `workflow.yaml`'s `workflow:` section carries (see §7).

**`src/workflow_engine/yaml_loader.py`** — the actual parser:
- `load_workflow_from_dir` (`yaml_loader.py:28-87`) — runs `config_validator.validate_single_workflow` first (raising on `severity=="error"`), then reads `workflow.yaml` for shared config and every other `*.yaml` in the directory as one phase file each, sorted by `id`.
- `build_phase` (`yaml_loader.py:90-135`) — maps a phase dict to the sdk `Phase` dataclass. Notably: `validation` becomes a typed `ValidationCriteria`, but `self_review` is passed through as a **raw dict**, not a typed object (`yaml_loader.py:114-116`) — an inconsistency that later causes a documented near-miss in `phase_manager.py:2269-2277` (see §6).
- `build_phase_list` (`yaml_loader.py:138-159`) — orders phases by `cfg["execution_order"]` if present, else `sorted(phases_by_id)`.
- `load_full_workflow_definition` (`yaml_loader.py:212-236`) — one-shot entry point returning a populated `WorkflowDefinition` (sdk).

**`src/workflow_engine/config_validator.py`** — schema/cross-reference checks: required phase keys (`id`, `name`, `description` — `config_validator.py:44`), `thinking_level` enum, `after_phase` must reference a real phase name, condition `if` strings must match `CONDITION_PATTERN` (imported from `orchestrator.py`, not duplicated — `config_validator.py:22`), and `goto` targets must reference a real phase (`config_validator.py:350-359`). Critically, `VALID_CONDITION_ACTIONS = {"continue", "goto", "retry"}` (`config_validator.py:53`) — narrower than the six values `OrchestrationAction` actually supports (`continue/retry/goto/fail/skip/arbitrate`, `orchestrator.py:55-63`). A YAML author cannot validly write `action: fail` or `action: skip` even though the engine has handlers for both (see §6/§7).

**`src/workflow_engine/orchestrator.py`** (`WorkflowOrchestrator`) — the pure gate evaluator: given a phase name, a `phase_output` dict, and history, decides continue/retry/goto/arbitrate/fail. Has no DB access and no knowledge of `Phase`/`PhaseExecution` rows; `PhaseManager` feeds it primitives and applies its `EvaluationResult`.

---

### 2. Phase graph model

Phases reference each other three ways, all resolved by **name or numeric order**, never by DB UUID at the YAML layer:

1. **Linear "next"** — implicit. `execution_order` in `workflow.yaml` (e.g. autopilot's `[1, 2, 3, ..., 14]`, `config/workflows/autopilot/workflow.yaml:8`) defines the sequence `build_phase_list` (`yaml_loader.py:158`) returns. At runtime, `PhaseManager._start_next_phase` (`phase_manager.py:1463-1657`) finds "next" by `Phase.order > current_phase.order` (`phase_manager.py:1583-1591`) unless overridden by #3 below.
2. **Conditional gate → goto target** — declared per-phase in `workflow.yaml`'s `orchestrator.evaluation_points[].conditions[].target` (a phase **name**, e.g. `target: architecture_design`). Parsed into `EvaluationPoint.conditions` (list of raw dicts — `orchestrator.py:83`, `orchestrator.py:126-139`), resolved to a real `Phase` row at goto time by `PhaseManager._find_phase_by_name_or_order` (`phase_manager.py:1417-1442`), which tries name-match first, then falls back to parsing the string as an `order` int.
3. **Explicit re-entry target on the completing task** — `Task.action` / `Task.action_target_phase` (`database.py:233-239`), written once by `PhaseManager._tag_completing_task` (`phase_manager.py:1128-1157`). `_start_next_phase` checks the most recently completed task for this phase (`phase_manager.py:1554-1580`) and, if it carries a `goto`/`retry` action with a target, resumes **there** instead of walking to the next phase by order — this is how e.g. `product_validation` sending the pipeline back to `development` correctly resumes at `product_validation` once the fix lands, instead of re-walking the entire review chain.

**Concrete example** — `config/workflows/autopilot/workflow.yaml:71-84`, the `architecture_design` gate:
```yaml
- after_phase: architecture_design
  evaluator: heuristic
  max_retries: 2
  conditions:
    - if: "score < 0.4"
      action: goto
      target: product_requirements
      reason: "Architecture fundamentally flawed, re-examine requirements"
    - if: "score < 0.6"
      action: retry
      reason: "Architecture needs improvement"
    - if: "score >= 0.6"
      action: continue
      reason: "Architecture approved"
```
Conditions are evaluated **in order**, first match wins (`orchestrator.py:514-533`); there is no fallthrough guard against overlapping ranges (a `< 0.6` before `>= 0.6` here works by construction, not by validation — nothing in `config_validator.py` checks that a phase's conditions are jointly exhaustive or mutually exclusive).

Each individual phase file (e.g. `config/workflows/autopilot/development.yaml`) carries the **content** side of the graph: `id`, `name`, `description`, `done_definitions` (checklist text, not machine-evaluated), `outputs`, `additional_notes` (the actual agent prompt body), `thinking_level`, `self_review`, `cli_tool`/`cli_model`. `qa_validation.yaml:5` additionally sets `spec_gate: true` — a field `build_phase` (`yaml_loader.py:90-135`) never reads at all; it's picked up by a wholly separate scanner, `src/autopilot/spec.py:_load_gated_phases` (`spec.py:44-89`), which does its own `yaml.safe_load` of every phase file in every workflow directory at import time to build `GATED_PHASES`. That means **three independent readers** of the same phase YAML files: `yaml_loader.build_phase` (structural `Phase` fields), `config_validator` (schema checks), and `spec._load_gated_phases` (raw dict scan for one boolean flag) — see §6/§7.

---

### 3. Phase advancement & gating logic

There are **two independent entry points** into phase-completion evaluation, both converging on the same function:

- **Synchronous fast path**: `TaskCompletionService.fire_spec_gate_if_ready` (`src/services/task_completion_service.py:570-710`) — fires the instant a *gated* phase's last task completes (checked via `GATED_PHASES` membership, `task_completion_service.py:589`), so the pipeline doesn't wait for the next sweep tick.
- **Periodic sweep**: `_advance_phases` → `_case_in_progress_complete` (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:1066-1467`) → `_fire_phase_transition` (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:1667-1807`), polled on an interval, self-healing anything the synchronous path missed (crashed process, phase already `in_progress` when the gate would have fired, etc.).

Both call **`PhaseManager.mark_phase_complete`** (`phase_manager.py:1159-1321`), the actual decision point:

1. **Idempotency guard** (`phase_manager.py:1218-1229`): if `execution.status == "completed"` already, return `{"action": "already_completed", ...}` without re-evaluating. This exists specifically because two callers (spec gate + sweep) can race to close the same phase.
2. **Force-action override** (`phase_manager.py:1231-1243`): if `force_action` is passed (used by `_resolve_arbitration_outcome` after an arbiter decides, and by `_cap_out_review_phase`'s forced-continue), skip the orchestrator entirely and go straight to `_handle_force_continue`/`_handle_force_goto`/`_handle_force_fail` (`phase_manager.py:791-864`).
3. **Get orchestrator** — `_get_orchestrator` (`phase_manager.py:1323-1393`). Cached per `workflow_id` in `self._orchestrators`, but the **static config** (`max_total_gotos`, `evaluation_points`, etc.) is re-read from `WorkflowDefinition.orchestrator_config` on every call and reapplied to the cached instance even on a hit (`phase_manager.py:1365-1377`) — a deliberate fix (see `ff02edc` below) for a config that used to go permanently stale once cached. `max_total_gotos` is then further overridden per-workflow-instance from `Workflow.launch_params["max_iterations"]` (`phase_manager.py:1369-1372`).
4. **Sync persisted counters into the (re-built-per-call) orchestrator**: `orchestrator.total_gotos` from `Workflow.total_gotos` (`phase_manager.py:1260-1263`) and `orchestrator.phase_retry_counts[phase.name]` from `Phase.retry_count` (`phase_manager.py:1270`) — both `WorkflowOrchestrator` fields are in-memory-only and reset to zero on every fresh instance, so without this sync the budgets would never actually run out (explicit comment, `phase_manager.py:1252-1259`).
5. **Evaluate** — `orchestrator.evaluate(phase_name, phase_output, phase_history)` (`phase_manager.py:1274-1278`, evaluator body at `orchestrator.py:237-386`):
   - Sequential-type orchestrators always return `CONTINUE` (`orchestrator.py:257-261`).
   - Find the `EvaluationPoint` for this phase name (`_find_evaluation_point`, `orchestrator.py:388-399`, matched by name or by `int(after_phase) == phase_order`).
   - **Retry-budget check happens before scoring** (`orchestrator.py:271-294`): if `phase_retry_counts[phase_name] >= eval_point.max_retries`, either return `ARBITRATE` (if `on_budget_exhausted == "arbitrate"`) or force `CONTINUE`. This ordering matters — see §6's `_cap_out_review_phase` note.
   - **Score** via the configured evaluator: `llm`, `heuristic` (`_heuristic_evaluate`, `orchestrator.py:426-468`), or the bare default (`_default_evaluate`, `orchestrator.py:470-496`) which returns `0.75` ("pass") unless `phase_output` carries explicit `status=="failed"` or `errors`.
   - **Condition matching**: `_evaluate_conditions` (`orchestrator.py:498-540`) walks `conditions` in order, first match via `_check_condition` (`orchestrator.py:542-600`) — a regex-constrained mini-language (`CONDITION_PATTERN`, `orchestrator.py:26`: `var op number`), deliberately not a real `eval()`.
   - **Retry/goto budget bookkeeping** (`orchestrator.py:324-367`): a `RETRY` action always increments `phase_retry_counts`; a `GOTO` only does so if `eval_point.goto_counts_as_retry` is set (opt-in — most eval points don't, so a repeating goto answers only to the workflow-wide `max_total_gotos`). Every `GOTO` increments `total_gotos`; exceeding `max_total_gotos` either escalates to `ARBITRATE` (if configured) or force-downgrades to `CONTINUE` with a warning (`orchestrator.py:344-367`).
6. **Persist counters back** (`phase_manager.py:1280-1289`): if `orchestrator.total_gotos` or the per-phase retry count changed, write them back to `Workflow.total_gotos` / `Phase.retry_count` immediately.
7. **Dispatch on `evaluation.action.value`** via a string-keyed table (`_EVALUATION_ACTION_VALUES`, `phase_manager.py:1119-1126`) to `_handle_evaluation_{action}` (`phase_manager.py:870-1110`):
   - `continue`/`skip` → close execution, `_start_next_phase` or `_complete_workflow`.
   - `retry` → reset `execution.status="pending"`, `started_at=None`, **and `task_creation_claimed_at=None`** (`phase_manager.py:905-912` — without this reset the retry cycle can never get a fresh task claimed).
   - `goto` → close the source phase, resolve target by name/order, **reset every `PhaseExecution` at/after the target's order that's `in_progress`/`completed` back to `pending`** (excluding the just-closed source execution itself — `phase_manager.py:950-984`), clear `task_creation_claimed_at`/`started_at` on each, then `_consume_gate_artifacts_for` (`phase_manager.py:1010-1057`) deletes the stale result artifacts that fed this score so a later re-run doesn't re-score the same file forever.
   - `arbitrate` → set `execution.status = "in_progress"` (deliberately **not** `"pending"` — see the inline rationale at `phase_manager.py:1068-1079`: `"pending"` would be invisible to the sweep's "next pending phase after the latest completed" ordering), clear the claim, return control to the caller so it can spawn an arbitration agent (`_trigger_arbitration`, `src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:1958`).
   - `fail` → close execution as `"failed"`, `_fail_workflow`.
8. **`_tag_completing_task`** (`phase_manager.py:1128-1157`) is the single choke point that writes `Task.action`/`Task.action_target_phase` for every `goto`/`retry` result, regardless of which handler produced it — the frontend badge and `_start_next_phase`'s explicit-target lookup (#3 above) both depend on this.

**Review-mode / cap-out pause points**: `_cap_out_review_phase` (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:2412`) stops a review phase from re-running past `max_review_runs` (opt-in, distinct from `max_retries` — `orchestrator.py:87-92`) by force-completing it via `_fire_phase_transition(..., force_continue=True)`, which routes to `mark_phase_complete(force_action="continue")` and bypasses `evaluate()` entirely — deliberately, because `evaluate()`'s own retry-count check (step 5 above) would otherwise short-circuit straight to `ARBITRATE` before ever reading the synthetic "clean" score (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:1701-1727` explains this exact interaction and an observed-live infinite loop it caused before the fix).

---

### 4. Task-creation-per-phase mechanics

A phase's first (or next-cycle) task is created from **multiple, independently-triggered code paths**: `server.py`'s synchronous "start workflow" step, the sweep's `_case_start_first_phase`/`_case_in_progress_no_tasks`, `_fire_phase_transition`'s goto/continue dispatch, `_trigger_arbitration`, and `_negotiate_validation_fix`'s corrective-task path. Nothing about "does this phase already have a task" is safe to check with a plain count — `Task.count()==0` followed by "so create one" is a read-then-write race between any two of these paths.

**The claim mechanism** — `PhaseExecution.task_creation_claimed_at` (`database.py:552-561`):
- `_claim_phase_task_creation(db, phase_id)` (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:758-790`): a single `UPDATE phase_executions SET task_creation_claimed_at=now() WHERE phase_id=? AND task_creation_claimed_at IS NULL`, committed immediately. Returns `True` only if *this* call's UPDATE affected a row — SQLite serializes writes to the same row, so exactly one concurrent caller wins regardless of interleaving (this is why it closes the race "by construction instead of by timing," per its own docstring).
- `_release_phase_task_creation_claim(db, phase_id)` (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:791-852`): clears the claim once the guarded task actually exists, and flips `PhaseExecution.status` to `in_progress` if still `pending`/`completed`. Uses `.populate_existing()` (`phase_transitions.py:837`) because sessions run with `expire_on_commit=False` — without it, a stale in-memory copy loaded before the claim would silently no-op the release. `started_at` is anchored to the **guarded task's own `created_at`**, not `datetime.utcnow()` (`phase_transitions.py:822-848`) — this is the `75e1e52` fix: stamping "now" here (which can be several real seconds after the task committed, due to enrichment/embedding/dedup work in between) made every cycle-scoped query downstream (`Task.created_at >= cycle_start`) wrongly exclude the very task the claim was created for, producing a phantom "zero tasks this cycle" and a duplicate self-heal task.
- `_release_stale_task_creation_claims` (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:599-662`) — self-heal for an abandoned claim (process died mid-creation): if held past `CLAIM_STALE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (a module constant near `phase_transitions.py:54`), clears it and, if a task already exists, repairs the phase's status too. Must run before `_get_phase_statuses` for the cycle, because a phase whose claim never released also never got flipped to `in_progress` — it's invisible to the sweep's dispatch cases otherwise.
- Every task-creation call site follows the pattern: claim → create → release-in-`finally` (e.g. `_create_phase_task`, `phase_transitions.py:2499`, and `_create_corrective_task`/`_create_corrective_task_body`, `phase_transitions.py:2865`/`2922`), with a "claim already stale, force-clear and retry once" fallback (e.g. `phase_transitions.py:2538-2560`).

**The races the recent fixes closed** (commit subjects verified against the code as it stands now):
- **`45dd5a2`** — `_create_phase_task`'s callers claimed the *source* phase but handed off to a *different* target phase (goto/arbitration) with no claim on it at all; a slow dispatch (worktree setup + tmux launch) crossing the orphan cutoff let a second caller spawn a sibling task on the same target. Fixed by having `_create_phase_task` claim its own `phase_id` internally by default, with call sites that already pre-claim passing `target_already_claimed=True` to skip the redundant attempt.
- **`85fc3c5`** — `_create_corrective_task` had no claim at all; fixed identically (wrap body in claim/release, split into `_create_corrective_task_body`, `phase_transitions.py:2865-2921`).
- **`084edcf`** — `_handle_evaluation_goto`/`_handle_force_goto` reset stale executions "at/after target order" but didn't exclude the source phase's own just-closed execution from that same reset query (since a phase's own order is always `>=` its goto target's order) — undoing `mark_phase_complete`'s idempotency guard and letting the same stale gate result fire the same goto twice, 84 seconds apart in the observed incident. Current code explicitly excludes `PhaseExecution.id != execution.id` in both handlers (`phase_manager.py:831`, `phase_manager.py:968`).
- **`75e1e52`** — described above (claim-release `started_at` anchoring).
- **`54d1814`** — `_retry_failed_tasks`'s two sibling-lookup queries filtered `Task.phase_id == phase_id`; when `phase_id` is `None` (phase-less, ad-hoc tasks an agent creates directly), SQLAlchemy compiles this to `IS NULL`, matching *every* phase-less task in the database regardless of workflow or age. Current code guards both queries with `if phase_id:` before running them (`src/autopilot/orchestrator.py` near line 1729/1787, per the diff), mirroring the pre-existing guard on `create_agent_for_task_direct` just above.
- **`903d76a`** — `_trigger_arbitration`'s cap counted raw arbitration attempts, not whether any had converged; a phase re-arbitrated 3 times with an identical "continue" answer against unchanged input still hit the cap and failed the workflow. Fixed to check whether the most recent arbitration task already holds an unconsumed converged decision before giving up.
- **`46f8b0c`**/**`ff02edc`** — goto-budget corruption: `total_gotos`/arbitration-cap counters never reset on their own, so a workflow that exhausted either stayed permanently unrecoverable via Retry even after being reactivated; and a global (not per-workflow) mutation of a **shared** `WorkflowDefinition.orchestrator_config` row let one workflow's launch (`run_phase0`, hardcoded `max_iterations=3`) silently corrupt every other concurrently-active workflow's real budget. Both are why `_get_orchestrator` (`phase_manager.py:1323-1393`) now re-reads static config fresh from the DB on every call and applies `max_iterations` only to `Workflow.launch_params`, never to the shared definition row.

---

### 5. State machine

**`PhaseExecution.status`** (`PhaseExecutionStatus`, `database.py:100-109`): `pending | in_progress | completed | failed | skipped`.

| Transition | Function (file:line) |
|---|---|
| — → `pending` (created) | `PhaseManager.initialize_workflow` (`phase_manager.py:301-307`), `start_execution` (`phase_manager.py:2290-2296`) |
| `pending` → `in_progress` | `PhaseManager._start_phase` (`phase_manager.py:1444-1452`, first-phase launch); `_start_next_phase` (`phase_manager.py:1636-1653`, subsequent phases); `_release_phase_task_creation_claim` (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:841`, self-heal after claim release); `_release_stale_task_creation_claims` (`phase_transitions.py:650`, self-heal); `_release_pending_phases_with_done_tasks` (`phase_transitions.py:663`, self-heal for a stuck-pending phase with an already-done task) |
| `in_progress`/`completed` → `pending` (goto rewind) | `_handle_evaluation_goto` / `_handle_force_goto` (`phase_manager.py:973-982`, `phase_manager.py:836-850`) — resets every execution at/after the goto target's order |
| `pending`/`in_progress` → `completed` | `PhaseManager._close_execution` (`phase_manager.py:725-739`), called from every `continue`/`skip`/`goto`/`retry`-source-close path |
| `pending`/`in_progress` → `failed` | `_close_execution` via `_handle_force_fail`/`_handle_evaluation_fail` (`phase_manager.py:796-804`, `phase_manager.py:1097-1110`); also directly in `_case_in_progress_complete`'s exhausted-retry-cap branch (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:1394-1400`) |
| `pending` → `skipped` | `_start_next_phase`'s jump-handling (`phase_manager.py:1621-1634`) — only downgrades genuinely `pending` executions skipped over by an explicit-target jump; never overwrites `completed` |
| `pending` → `in_progress` (re-arbitration hold) | `_handle_evaluation_arbitrate` (`phase_manager.py:1080`) — sets `in_progress` specifically so the sweep's ordering logic doesn't lose track of a phase awaiting arbitration |

**`Workflow.status`** (`WorkflowStatus`, `database.py:75-83`): `active | paused | completed | failed`. Set to `completed` by `PhaseManager._complete_workflow` (`phase_manager.py:1699-1703`, guarded against firing while a higher-order `Phase` still remains — `phase_manager.py:1679-1697`); to `failed` by `_fail_workflow` (`phase_manager.py:1454-1461`) and directly by the sweep's exhausted-retry-cap path (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:1394-1400`) and `_trigger_arbitration`'s cap-exceeded path; to `paused` by `_pause_for_manual_handoff` and the exhausted-retry auto-pause path (both in `src/autopilot/orchestrator.py`, outside the files in scope).

**`Task.action`/`action_target_phase`** — write-once per completing task via `_tag_completing_task` (`phase_manager.py:1128-1157`), read by `_start_next_phase` (`phase_manager.py:1565-1579`) to prefer an explicit resume target over next-by-order.

---

### 6. Known fragile spots / code smells

- **Three independent YAML readers for the same phase files.** `yaml_loader.build_phase` (`yaml_loader.py:90-135`) builds the structural `Phase`; `config_validator` (`config_validator.py`) re-parses every file again for schema checks; `src/autopilot/spec._load_gated_phases` (`spec.py:44-89`) does a *third* independent `yaml.safe_load` pass over every workflow directory just to collect `spec_gate: true` flags, because `build_phase` never reads that key at all. The `spec_gate` docstring (`spec.py:47-62`) documents that this field used to live in a hand-maintained tuple that silently drifted from the real YAML — the fix moved the flag into the YAML but didn't unify the reading path, so a fourth reader is only one refactor away from drifting again.
- **`config_validator`'s `VALID_CONDITION_ACTIONS` is narrower than `OrchestrationAction`.** `config_validator.py:53` allows only `continue/goto/retry`; the engine (`OrchestrationAction`, `orchestrator.py:55-63`) also has `FAIL`/`SKIP`/`ARBITRATE`. `FAIL`/`SKIP` have working handlers in `phase_manager.py` (`_handle_evaluation_fail`, `_handle_evaluation_skip`) that no YAML condition can currently reach without failing validation — dead code paths reachable only via `force_action`, or a validation gap waiting for someone to add `action: skip` to a phase YAML.
- **`self_review` typed inconsistently across the same pipeline.** `yaml_loader.build_phase` passes it through as a raw dict (`yaml_loader.py:114-116`), unlike `validation`, which becomes a typed `ValidationCriteria`. `phase_manager.py:2269-2277` has an explicit comment flagging that the analogous `validation` line *does* wrap in `serialize_for_text` (JSON-stringifying it), which would silently break a later `phase.validation.get(...)` call — the comment notes this bug is currently latent only because no phase YAML sets `validation:` today. This is a landmine, not a fixed bug.
- **`WorkflowOrchestrator._phase_name_to_order` has a hardcoded fallback vocabulary.** `_LEGACY_NAME_TO_ORDER` (`orchestrator.py:199-215`) is a hand-maintained `{name: order}` dict for the autopilot pipeline specifically, kept only "for when `phase_order_map` wasn't provided." Its own comment admits a prior version had `git_commit_push`/`forensics_analysis` backwards because it trusted `workflow.yaml`'s `session_roles` dict order, which isn't actually load-bearing for execution order. A supposedly generic, config-driven engine still carries one caller's vocabulary baked in as a fallback.
- **`mark_phase_complete` is a single 160-line function juggling six responsibilities**: idempotency, force-override, orchestrator caching/config-refresh, counter sync (two separate persisted counters), evaluation, and dispatch (`phase_manager.py:1159-1321`). The dispatch itself was fixed once already (`_EVALUATION_ACTION_VALUES`/`getattr` dispatch, `phase_manager.py:1112-1126`) specifically because a hand-written if/elif chain silently dropped the `SKIP` action — the fix is defensive (raises nothing on an unregistered action, just falls through to a default `continue`, `phase_manager.py:1304-1309`), so the same class of silent-drop bug can recur for any future `OrchestrationAction` value.
- **`_start_next_phase` is 195 lines** (`phase_manager.py:1463-1657`) combining: workflow-status guard, in-progress-collision guard, explicit-goto-target resolution, next-by-order fallback, skip-marking for jumped-over phases, and execution-status flip with claim reset. Every one of these blocks exists because of a distinct observed-live incident (each has its own multi-paragraph comment) — this function is the textbook definition of patch-on-patch accumulation, and every future edit risks breaking one of five independently-motivated invariants stacked in one function body.
- **The task-creation-claim (`task_creation_claimed_at`) is overloaded for two purposes.** It started as "claim the right to create this phase's first task" but is now also reused as "claim the right to evaluate this phase's transition" (`src/services/task_completion_service.py:604-627`, `src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:797-816`). The comments at both sites explicitly acknowledge this is a repurposing, not the original design — a single boolean-timestamp column is standing in for two different kinds of mutual exclusion.
- **Duplicated stale-execution-reset logic.** `_handle_evaluation_goto` (`phase_manager.py:930-1005`) and `_handle_force_goto` (`phase_manager.py:806-863`) contain near-identical 25-line blocks (query stale executions at/after target order, exclude self, reset status/`completed_at`/`task_creation_claimed_at`/`started_at`) — not extracted into a shared helper despite being copy-adjacent and having independently accumulated the same fixes (`084edcf`'s self-exclusion fix had to be applied to both).
- **Gate-scoring logic (`spec.py`'s `score_*` functions, `spec.py:559-1153`) is entirely separate from `WorkflowOrchestrator`'s condition evaluator.** `_default_evaluate`/`_heuristic_evaluate` (`orchestrator.py:426-496`) exist as one evaluation strategy; gated phases bypass them by writing directly into `phase_output["score"]`/`["spec_gate"]` before `mark_phase_complete` is even called (`src/autopilot/orchestrator.py:5789-5806`, `orchestrator.py:316-317` passthrough). The YAML's `evaluator: heuristic` field is misleading for a gated phase — the real score never goes through `_heuristic_evaluate` at all.

---

### 7. Refactor observations

1. **Unify YAML reading.** Collapse `yaml_loader.build_phase`, `config_validator`'s independent parse, and `spec._load_gated_phases`'s independent parse into a single parse pass that produces one canonical in-memory representation, with `spec_gate` promoted to a first-class field on the sdk `Phase` dataclass instead of a side-channel raw-dict scan.
2. **Add a real schema layer, not a hand-rolled key-presence checker.** `config_validator.py` reimplements JSON-Schema-shaped validation (required keys, enums, cross-references) by hand across ~500 lines. A declarative schema (even a lightweight pydantic model per YAML shape) would close the `VALID_CONDITION_ACTIONS`-vs-`OrchestrationAction` drift (§6) by construction and remove an entire class of "validator forgot to check X" bugs.
3. **Consolidate goto/retry-reset logic.** Extract the stale-execution-reset block duplicated between `_handle_evaluation_goto` and `_handle_force_goto` into one shared method — every future fix to this logic (three already landed: `084edcf`, `75e1e52`'s pairing, the started_at reset) currently has to be applied twice.
4. **Split `task_creation_claimed_at`'s two responsibilities** ("claim to create a task" vs "claim to evaluate a transition") into two distinct locks, or rename/document the column as the general-purpose phase-mutation mutex it has become — the current single field makes every new caller re-derive from first principles whether holding the claim is safe for its use case.
5. **Break up `mark_phase_complete` and `_start_next_phase`.** Both functions have grown by accretion (one incident, one comment block, one fix at a time) into single functions carrying five-plus independently-motivated invariants each. Each stacked concern (idempotency, config refresh, counter persistence, dispatch; and workflow-status guard, collision guard, explicit-target resolution, skip-marking, claim reset, respectively) is a candidate for its own named, independently testable unit.
