6. **Move gate scoring (`spec.py`'s `score_*` family) into the `WorkflowOrchestrator` evaluator abstraction properly**, instead of pre-computing `phase_output["score"]` externally and having the orchestrator's own `evaluator: heuristic`/`llm` labels lie about what actually produced the number. A phase's YAML currently claims one evaluation strategy while a different one silently runs for gated phases — worth making explicit as a third evaluator kind (`evaluator: spec_gate`) rather than an implicit override.
7. **Reconcile the two independent phase-completion entry points** (`fire_spec_gate_if_ready`'s synchronous path and the periodic sweep's `_case_in_progress_complete`) into one, or make the race between them structurally impossible rather than closed by an ad-hoc claim-check added to the synchronous path after the fact (`task_completion_service.py:604-627`'s own comment describes exactly this history).
## Agent Lifecycle Management

### 1. Purpose & responsibilities

Four files own the lifecycle of a tmux-hosted CLI agent process (pi, Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Droid):

- **`src/agents/manager.py`** (3616 lines) — the god-object of this subsystem. `AgentManager` owns: tmux session creation/teardown (`_create_tmux_session`, `src/agents/manager.py:1281-1427`), the full agent-creation orchestration (`create_agent_for_task`, `src/agents/manager.py:259-1243`, ~970 lines end-to-end including its fallback-retry and cleanup-on-failure branches), termination (`terminate_agent`, `src/agents/manager.py:1939-2208`), restart (`restart_agent`, `src/agents/manager.py:2257-2655`), transcript capture/polling (`get_agent_output` and ~10 helper methods, `src/agents/manager.py:2657-3363`), and inter-agent messaging entry points (`send_message_to_agent`, `broadcast_message_to_all_agents`, `send_direct_message`, `src/agents/manager.py:3405-3557`).
- **`src/agents/messenger.py`** (158 lines) — `AgentMessenger`, extracted from `AgentManager` per `docs/SOLID_OO_REVIEW.md` finding 3.1. Owns the mechanics of delivering a single message into a live tmux pane: wedged-shell detection (`_pane_is_wedged`, `src/agents/messenger.py:46-68`, matches zsh/bash continuation prompts so a nudge doesn't get typed into a stuck shell), shell-character escaping, and the actual `send_keys` call (`src/agents/messenger.py:70-157`). `AgentManager.send_message_to_agent` (`src/agents/manager.py:3405-3412`) is a one-line delegator kept public because `guardian.py`/`monitor.py` call it directly on the manager instance.
- **`src/agents/prompt_builder.py`** (367 lines) — `AgentPromptBuilder`, also extracted per finding 3.1. Pure string-formatting: builds the initial task-assignment message (workflow/phase context, resumed-session warnings, ticket notes, open-bug-ticket injection, phase-specific instruction blocks) in `format_initial_message` (`src/agents/prompt_builder.py:35-366`). `AgentManager._format_initial_message` (`src/agents/manager.py:1681-1703`) re-syncs `self._prompt_builder.phase_manager = self.phase_manager` on every call before delegating, because tests mutate `phase_manager` on the `AgentManager` instance post-construction and expect the builder to see the new value.
- **`src/agents/__init__.py`** — empty module docstring only, no exports.

**tmux session ↔ Agent DB row mapping**: one `Agent` row (`src/core/database.py:126-199`) corresponds to at most one live tmux session, named `{tmux_session_prefix}_{agent_id[:8]}` on first launch (`src/agents/manager.py:604`) or `{tmux_session_prefix}_{agent_id[:8]}_r` on restart (`src/agents/manager.py:2373`, the `_r` suffix distinguishes a restarted session's name from the killed original). `Agent.tmux_session_name` is the join key (unique-constrained, `src/core/database.py:141`). The CLI implementation is resolved polymorphically via `get_cli_agent(agent.cli_type)` (`src/interfaces/cli_interface.py:1150-1166`) against the `CLI_AGENTS` registry (`src/interfaces/cli_interface.py:1140-1147`: `claude`, `opencode`, `droid`, `codex`, `pi`, `swarm`).

The CLI abstraction layer lives entirely in **`src/interfaces/cli_interface.py`** (1166 lines, single file). `CLIAgentInterface` (ABC, `src/interfaces/cli_interface.py:67-388`) defines the contract `AgentManager` codes against: `get_launch_command` (abstract), `format_message`, `get_stuck_patterns`, `parse_output` (abstract), plus a long tail of polymorphic hooks added incrementally to replace `isinstance()` branching in `AgentManager`: `needs_chunked_delivery`, `post_launch_confirmation_keys`, `format_goal_command`, `recovery_keystrokes`, `mcp_reconnect_instructions`, `model_fallback_keystrokes`/`model_fallback_confirmed`, `strip_tui_chrome`. Six concrete classes implement it: `ClaudeCodeAgent`, `OpenCodeAgent`, `DroidAgent`, `CodexAgent`, `PiAgent`, `SwarmCodeAgent` (`src/interfaces/cli_interface.py:396-1137`; `SwarmCodeAgent` is explicitly marked "hypothetical" in its docstring and not in general use).

### 2. Agent lifecycle state machine

`Agent.status` is a `CheckConstraint`-enforced enum (`src/core/database.py:134-139`): `idle | working | stuck | terminated | starting`. There is no `Agent.status` value for "restarting" — a restart tears down the old session and, on success, sets status back to `working` (`src/agents/manager.py:2587`) under the *same* `Agent.id`; only the tmux session name and `restart_count` change.

**The invariant** (from this project's CLAUDE.md, and restated in a code comment on the column itself, `src/core/database.py:142-162`): every write of `status="terminated"` must also null `current_task_id` and stamp `terminated_at=datetime.utcnow()`. The column comment explains why current_task_id matters specifically: "a dozen+ call sites" query it without an accompanying `status != "terminated"` filter, so a stale pointer makes a dead agent indistinguishable from a live one to duplicate-dispatch guards and retry logic.

`terminate_agent` (`src/agents/manager.py:1939-2208`) is the single "proper" termination primitive — WIP auto-commit (`:1957-1984`, with a shared-worktree fallback via `_commit_wip_in_shared_worktree`, `:2210-2255`, because `commit_changes`'s `AgentBranch`-keyed lookup only covers the legacy per-agent-worktree path), pane/scrollback capture before kill (`:1986-2079`), a final stable-transcript flush (`:2081-2094`), SIGINT-then-SIGKILL of the pane's process group (`:2121-2143`), cost collection (`:2145-2153`), and only then the DB write (`:2155-2202`). Since `53e933a` it also carries a **safety net** (`:2160-2187`) that resets any *other* `Task` row still pointing at this agent (`assigned_agent_id == agent_id`, status in `assigned/in_progress/pending`) back to `pending` — "closing the gap at the shared primitive instead of requiring every one of terminate_agent's ~15 call sites to remember it."

A **second, parallel** primitive exists: `terminate_agent_direct` in `src/autopilot/orchestrator/engine_client.py:110-150` — a raw DB write with no tmux kill, used at 4 call sites inside that file. It originally lacked the same task-reset safety net; `27e8e88` added it (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/engine_client.py:131-141`).

I grepped every literal `agent.status = "terminated"` / `.status="terminated"` assignment in `src/` (excluding comments) and audited each against the invariant. This matches the count in `27e8e88`'s own commit message ("Audited all 19 raw `agent.status = "terminated"` DB writes") exactly — 19 raw sites, plus `terminate_agent_direct` as the 20th "primitive" covering 4 more callers:

| # | Site | current_task_id=None | terminated_at | Task reset | Status |
|---|------|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `src/agents/manager.py:1101` (fallback-retry cleanup, `create_agent_for_task`) | yes | yes | n/a (task not yet assigned) | OK |
| 2 | `src/agents/manager.py:1163` (final failure cleanup, `create_agent_for_task`) | yes | yes | task marked `failed` at `:1173-1176` | OK |
| 3 | `src/agents/manager.py:2156` (`terminate_agent`, primary primitive) | yes (`:2157`) | yes (`:2158`) | safety net `:2160-2187` | OK |
| 4 | `src/agents/manager.py:2278` (`restart_agent`, max-3-restarts-exceeded path) | yes (`:2282`) | yes (`:2279`) | task marked `failed` `:2285-2290` | OK |
| 5 | `src/mcp/api.py:2174` (`AgentAPI.reset_phase`, force-reset with active agents) | yes (`:2175`) | **missing** | tasks failed separately `:2180+` | **VIOLATION — no terminated_at** |
| 6 | `src/mcp/api.py:3025` (`stop_workflow` route, frontend-API's own copy, distinct from `server.py`'s) | yes (`:3026`) | **missing** | tasks failed separately `:3033+` | **VIOLATION — no terminated_at** |
| 7 | `src/mcp/autopilot_api.py:890` (design-file-edit → terminate affected workflow's agents) | yes | yes | reset `:895-901` | OK |
| 8 | `src/mcp/autopilot_api.py:1072` (`rerun_design`, stop-before-clean-slate) | yes | yes | tasks reset first, `:1064-1071` | OK |
| 9 | `src/mcp/autopilot_api.py:3111` (design delete/orphan-workflow cleanup) | yes | yes | dependent rows deleted afterward | OK |
| 10 | `src/mcp/autopilot_api.py:4007` (`pause_feature`) | yes | yes | task set `blocked` `:4014` | OK |
| 11 | `src/mcp/server.py:766` (startup resume-scan, git-already-landed shortcut) | yes | yes | task marked `done` just above | OK |
| 12 | `src/mcp/server.py:5052` (`/api/workflow-executions/{id}/stop`) | yes | yes | tasks reset, added by `27e8e88` | OK |
| 13 | `src/mcp/server.py:5168` (`/api/workflow-executions/{id}/cancel`) | yes | yes | tasks failed | OK |
| 14 | `src/monitoring/orphan_reaper.py:123` (workflow-no-longer-active cleanup) | yes | yes (`.now()`, not `.utcnow()` — see below) | reset, added by `27e8e88` (`:135-138`) | OK, but see UTC note |
| 15 | `src/monitoring/monitor.py:1809` (`_detect_orphaned_idle_agent`) | **missing on the Agent row** | yes (`:1810`) | Task's `assigned_agent_id` cleared (`:1813-1816`), but the Agent row's `current_task_id` itself is never nulled | **VIOLATION — current_task_id not cleared** |
| 16 | `src/monitoring/monitor.py:2580` (`_auto_restart_agent`, Guardian ignored-steering path) | yes (`:2581`) | no (genuinely omitted — this path expects a follow-up dispatch, not a terminal state) | task reset *first*, `:2536-2551` (fixed by `92caa82`) | task-ordering OK; `terminated_at` gap is pre-existing |
| 17 | `src/autopilot/orchestrator/engine_client.py:110` (`terminate_agent_direct` primitive, 4 callers) | yes | yes | added by `27e8e88`, `:131-141` | OK |
| 18 | `src/autopilot/orchestrator/engine_client.py:203` (`pause_project_workflows`) | yes (`:240`) | yes (`:241`) | `tasks_to_reset` loop `:276-284` | OK |
| 19 | `src/autopilot/orchestrator/__init__.py:2793` (`_register_orchestrator_agent`, evicting a stale `tmux_session_name="orchestrator"` row) | yes | yes | n/a — orchestrator agents aren't dispatched tasks the normal way | OK |

Three monitor.py detectors (`_detect_connection_errors`, `_detect_agent_never_started`, the context-overflow handler) never write the raw DB fields at all — they call `self.agent_manager.terminate_agent(agent.id)`, so they inherit primitive #3's correctness by construction, and are compliant.

**Two live, currently-unfixed invariant violations** worth flagging for the refactor:
- `src/mcp/api.py:2174` and `src/mcp/api.py:3025` both omit `terminated_at`. Neither was touched by `27e8e88`'s audit (that audit specifically grepped for the *task*-reset gap, not the `terminated_at` gap). Any code that later sorts/filters agents by `terminated_at IS NOT NULL`, or reports "when did this agent stop," silently misses agents terminated through these two paths.
- `src/monitoring/monitor.py:1809` (`_detect_orphaned_idle_agent`) never clears `Agent.current_task_id` on the agent row it just terminated, even though it reads that same field one line later (`:1811`) to find the task to fail. This is the exact bug class the column-comment invariant and 3 of the 4 historical fix commits (`27e8e88`, `92caa82`, `83e79ec`) were written to close — it appears this one path was missed by all of them.

Also worth flagging: `src/monitoring/orphan_reaper.py:123` uses `datetime.now()` (naive local time) for `terminated_at`, not `datetime.utcnow()` — a direct violation of this project's own `utc-only` invariant.

### 3. Task dispatch flow

`create_agent_for_task` (`src/agents/manager.py:259-1243`) is the single path that turns a `Task` into a running tmux CLI process. Step by step:

1. **Guardrails** (`:311-361`): reject `task is None`; block launch for `git_commit_push`-phase tasks when the project is in `review_mode` (`:326-341`, a `PermissionError` — "explicit human approval is required"); the **duplicate-dispatch guard** (`:346-361`, see §4).
2. **Stub `Agent` row** (`:416-438`): a minimal `Agent` (`status="idle"`, `system_prompt="(pending: worktree + prompt setup)"`) is inserted and committed *before* worktree creation, so a crash mid-dispatch leaves a discoverable half-created agent rather than losing the `current_task_id` claim entirely. If `assign_to_task=True`, `task.assigned_agent_id`/`status="in_progress"`/`started_at` are set in this same commit.
3. **Worktree resolution** (`:440-500`): shared-worktree workflows reuse `Workflow.working_directory` (fails loudly if the tracked directory is missing rather than silently forking a disconnected replacement); otherwise `WorktreeManager.create_agent_worktree` creates an isolated worktree.
4. **System prompt generation** (`:502-526`) via `self.llm_provider.generate_agent_prompt`.
5. **Model/env resolution** (`:528-608`): GLM env vars, MCP timeout for human-approval workflows, `HEPHAESTUS_AGENT_ID`/`TASK_ID`/`WORKFLOW_ID`/`PHASE_ID`/`API_URL` injected as env vars so MCP tool calls can self-identify. Tmux session created via `_create_tmux_session` (`:1266-1412`, see §6).
6. **The instructions-as-file mechanism** (`ebe3bbd`): `_write_task_instructions` (`:1463-1481`) persists the full initial message to `.hephaestus/tasks/{task_id}.md` inside the worktree, then `_build_instructions_pointer` (`:1483-1507`) builds a short, constant-size tmux message ("Your full task instructions are in `.hephaestus/tasks/{id}.md` — read that file now, then begin.") — this is what's actually typed into the pane, not the full prompt. `OpenCode` is the one CLI without a separate post-launch message step, so its `instructions_pointer` is folded directly into `get_launch_command`'s output.
7. **Launch** (`:796-841`): `cli_agent.get_launch_command(...)` returns a `LaunchResult(command, prompt_delivery)`; env vars are exported into the pane and read back for verification, retries once on a failed readback.
8. **Stub-to-full `Agent` update + task assignment** (`:843-878`): `status="working"`, `tmux_session_name`, `launched_at` set; `Task.assigned_agent_id`/`status="in_progress"`/`started_at` set.
9. **25-second CLI-init wait**, then the **mid-launch abort check** (`:911-965`, extended by `0fe3573` to also check task cancellation, not just agent termination — see §5) — this is the exact bug fixed by `22178b1`/`0fe3573`: pause or task-reassignment during the sleep must stop the coroutine before it delivers the real prompt.
10. **Launch-failure detection** (`:975-1032`): tmux session still alive check; a regex scan of the pane for `command not found`/`No such file or directory` (missing binary); a scan for Claude Code's unhandled "Bypass Permissions mode" confirmation dialog, which `--dangerously-skip-permissions` doesn't suppress.
11. **`/goal` command** (`_send_goal_command`, `:1509-1551`): sent *before* the task pointer, since it's a CLI-consumed command rather than a chat turn; explicitly skipped for `validator`/`result_validator`/`diagnostic`/`arbitration` agent types.
12. **Deliver the pointer** (`_send_initial_prompt_with_retry`, `:1722-1922`) — chunked (2500-char pieces) for CLIs where `needs_chunked_delivery=True` (Claude, Droid, Codex, Pi — all four concrete CLIs except OpenCode); also scans for session/rate-limit rejection phrases right after delivery.
13. **Best-effort read-confirmation** (`_verify_instructions_file_read`, `:1553-1577`): a 15s wait then a pane scan for the instructions filename — warns only, never blocks.

On any exception in this whole flow, the `except` block (`:1080-1228`) first tries the phase's configured `fallback_cli_tool` (recursing into `create_agent_for_task`), and only on total failure marks the stub agent terminated and the task `failed` — including a special case (`:1178-1213`) that pauses the whole workflow on a detected CLI session-limit rejection rather than burning further retries.

### 4. Duplicate-dispatch guarding

Two independent guards exist, at different granularities:

- **Per-task guard**, `src/agents/manager.py:343-361` (`create_agent_for_task`): before creating a stub row, queries for an existing `Agent` with `current_task_id == task.id` and `status IN (working, idle)`. If found, logs a warning and returns the *existing* agent instead of creating a duplicate.
- **Per-phase-sibling guard**, added by `f797a94`, `src/autopilot/orchestrator/engine_client.py:293-383` (`create_agent_for_task_direct`): queries for *any other* `Task` in the same `phase_id` that's `pending/assigned/in_progress/queued` (scoped further, since `f797a94`, to tasks created by `None` or the current orchestrator agent). This is a broader net than the per-task guard — it catches the race described in the commit message: `_retry_failed_tasks` (task-level, no claim) and `_advance_phases` (phase-level, with claim) deciding to dispatch in adjacent sweep ticks for the *same phase* but *different* `Task.id`s (the latter having created a fresh task via `goto`). Returns `None` on a hit, which `create_agent_for_task_direct`'s callers must handle as "dispatch skipped."

These two guards are not unified — `create_agent_for_task` (manager.py) has no phase-sibling check, and `create_agent_for_task_direct` (orchestrator.py) duplicates the per-task DB query pattern independently rather than calling into manager.py's guard.

### 5. Restart/steering/pause mechanics

**`restart_agent`** (`src/agents/manager.py:2257-2655`): kills the existing tmux session (with a final stable-transcript flush first), re-resolves the working directory (shared workflow worktree, or falls back to the agent's own tracked worktree via `WorktreeManager.get_agent_branch_path`, `:2388-2403` — added by `ebe3bbd` so a validator/diagnostic agent restart still lands in its committed worktree instead of nowhere), relaunches with a `⚠️ RESTART:`-prefixed system prompt and a resume message instructing the agent to `git log`/`git status` rather than redo work. **Restart-loop protection**: `agent.restart_count >= 3` short-circuits to termination + task-failure instead of relaunching again.

**Guardian ignored-steering auto-restart**: `MonitoringLoop` tracks `consecutive_stuck` per agent; once it reaches `self.config.max_ignored_steering`, and the agent has been idle ≥300s, it calls `_auto_restart_agent` (`src/monitoring/monitor.py:2512-2582`). Despite the name, this method does **not** call `AgentManager.restart_agent` — it resets the stuck task to `pending` (fixed by `92caa82` to happen *before* the kill, closing a race where a completion call from the dying agent could land on a task already reset out from under it), kills the tmux session, and marks the `Agent` row `terminated`. A later dispatch sweep then picks the now-`pending` task back up as a *fresh* agent — "auto-restart" is a misnomer for what is actually "auto-terminate-and-let-the-scheduler-redispatch."

**Pause mechanics**: no single "pause" primitive exists; each surface implements its own agent-termination + task-reset combo. `pause_project_workflows` (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/engine_client.py:203-292`) is the pipeline-level Pause button path — sets `Feature.status="paused"` (fixed by `22178b1`), terminates working/starting/idle agents on the workflow's tasks, resets their `in_progress` tasks to `pending`. `pause_feature` (`src/mcp/autopilot_api.py:3985-4020`) is the per-feature equivalent, setting tasks to `blocked` instead of `pending` and stamping `wf.paused_by = "user"` so the self-heal sweep's auto-resume doesn't immediately un-pause it. The mid-launch abort check in `create_agent_for_task` is what makes either pause path actually stick for an agent that was still inside its 25s CLI-init wait.

**Safe restart grace window** (`271745f`, `src/mcp/server.py`, `SAFE_RESTART_GRACE_SECONDS = 10`): `_notify_and_pause_for_restart` sends a best-effort checkpoint nudge to every in-flight agent, then keeps the server accepting connections for 10s before pausing pipelines — long enough for an in-flight `complete_my_task` HTTP call to land before the backend actually goes down.

### 6. tmux process management specifics

**Session creation** (`_create_tmux_session`, `src/agents/manager.py:1281-1427`): kills any pre-existing session of the same name; sets a small `history-limit` of 1000 (since the durable transcript comes from `pipe-pane`, not scrollback); explicitly `unset`s `CLAUDECODE`/`CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`/etc. in the fresh pane — new tmux sessions inherit the *server's* environment from whenever it was first started, which can itself be a Claude Code session (`heph restart` invoked from within Claude Code), and `claude` refuses to launch under `CLAUDECODE=1`; sets pane width to 150 columns so captured output isn't hard-wrapped at 80.

**Transcript capture** via `pipe-pane` (`:1334-1392`): pipes raw pty bytes through an inline `perl -e` filter that strips OSC/CSI/charset-selection escape sequences while preserving SGR color codes and `\r`, with explicit `$|=1` autoflush and a `sysread()` loop (not `-pe`'s line-buffered `while(<>)`) — the file's own comment documents a real incident where the line-buffered form left a transcript frozen at the launch command's trailing newline for an agent's entire multi-minute run, because pi's TUI redraws via `\r` and cursor-positioning, not literal newlines. Separately, `terminate_agent` does a final unconditional `capture-pane` snapshot before killing and writes it to both an `AgentLog` and a per-phase `.hephaestus/tmux/{phase}_{agent_id[:8]}.log` file — this is the *only* clean-shutdown-path capture; the Guardian auto-restart, orphan-reaper, and monitor kill paths all bypass it and rely solely on `pipe-pane`'s continuous tee.

**Process teardown** (`terminate_agent`, `:2081-2128`): kills the tmux session via `subprocess.run(["tmux", "kill-session", ...])` (more reliable than the libtmux wrapper), then sends `SIGINT` (`kill -2 -- -{pane_pid}`, negative PID targets the whole process group) to every captured pane PID, waits 1s, then `SIGKILL` to any still alive. Pane PIDs are captured via `tmux list-panes -F '#{pane_pid}'` *before* the session is killed, since they're unrecoverable afterward.

**Worktree association**: `AgentWorktree` (`src/core/database.py:567-605`, aliased `AgentBranch`) is keyed 1:1 by `agent_id`, tracking `worktree_path`, `branch_name` (unique), `parent_agent_id`/`parent_commit_sha`/`base_commit_sha` (isolation lineage), and `merge_status` (`active|merged|abandoned|cleaned`). This table is only populated for the *legacy isolated-per-agent-worktree* path — shared-feature-worktree agents (the common case in a live pipeline) never get a row here, which is why `terminate_agent`'s WIP-commit-via-`branch_manager` step (keyed off this table) fails for them and falls back to `_commit_wip_in_shared_worktree`.

**Injection/leak risk areas**:
- `AgentMessenger.send_message_to_agent` (`src/agents/messenger.py:134-141`) escapes `"`, `$`, `` ` `` before wrapping the message in literal double quotes and `send_keys`-ing it. This defuses shell command substitution *if* the target pane happens to be a bare shell rather than the CLI's own chat prompt — a real scenario the code elsewhere explicitly guards against (`_pane_is_wedged`), but the escaping here assumes the CLI is always still the one reading the input.
- `_create_tmux_session`'s `pipe-pane` command is built with `shlex.quote` on the transcript path and the whole perl filter string — properly guarded.
- `create_agent_for_task`'s task-description echo uses `shlex.quote` — also properly guarded, but is a second, independent place that had to remember to do this (no shared "safe echo" helper).

### 7. Known fragile spots / code smells

- **Termination logic is duplicated across ~20 call sites** (§2's table) instead of behind one primitive. Two of those sites are missing `terminated_at`; one is missing `current_task_id` clearing. This is not a one-time bug — it is the *fourth* time this exact class of bug has been found and fixed (`83e79ec`, `92caa82`, `27e8e88`, and now these two newly-identified gaps), each time at a *different* call site, because the invariant is enforced by convention and comment, not by a single write path.
- **`create_agent_for_task` is ~970 lines** covering guard checks, worktree resolution, prompt generation, env-var/model resolution, session-ID computation, launch, launch-failure detection, prompt delivery, and a full fallback-CLI retry + cleanup-on-failure branch. `restart_agent` (~400 lines) duplicates large portions of the same launch/delivery sequence independently rather than sharing a "launch and deliver" helper.
- **Two unreconciled duplicate-dispatch guards** (§4) at different granularities in different files, neither aware of the other, with no shared query helper.
- **Two termination primitives** (`AgentManager.terminate_agent` vs. `terminate_agent_direct`) with materially different behavior and separately-maintained safety nets for the same invariant.
- **`_auto_restart_agent`'s name is misleading**: it terminates and relies on a separate scheduler sweep to redispatch; it never itself relaunches a tmux session, unlike `restart_agent`.
- **`datetime.now()` vs `datetime.utcnow()` inconsistency** at `src/monitoring/orphan_reaper.py:123` (and one other at `:109`) directly violates this project's own stated `utc-only` invariant.
- **`get_agent_output`'s fallback chain is five levels deep** (stable "clean" transcript → live capture-pane → termination-time `AgentLog` capture → raw pipe-pane transcript → recent `AgentLog` entries) — correct and well-commented, but each layer has its own subtly different content shape.

### 8. Refactor observations

1. **Introduce one termination write path.** Every raw `agent.status = "terminated"` assignment outside `AgentManager.terminate_agent`/`terminate_agent_direct` should be replaced by a call to one of those — or unify the two primitives into a single `terminate_agent(agent_id, *, kill_tmux: bool, reason: str)` that always sets all three fields and always resets stray tasks.
2. **Split `create_agent_for_task` into a pipeline of smaller functions** (worktree resolution → env/model resolution → launch → delivery), sharing the launch/delivery half with `restart_agent`.
3. **Unify the two duplicate-dispatch guards** (§4) behind one query, parameterized by scope (per-task vs. per-phase-sibling), called from both `create_agent_for_task` and `create_agent_for_task_direct`.
4. **Add a DB-level trigger or SQLAlchemy `before_update` hook** enforcing the termination invariant so a future raw write anywhere in the codebase fails loudly at commit time instead of silently reintroducing this bug class a fifth time.
5. **Replace the ad-hoc shell-escaping in `AgentMessenger.send_message_to_agent`** with the same `shlex.quote`-based helper already used elsewhere, rather than a third, independently-hand-rolled escaping scheme.
6. **Rename or restructure `_auto_restart_agent`** to reflect what it actually does (terminate-and-defer-to-scheduler), or actually make it call `AgentManager.restart_agent`.
## Worktree Management & CLI Interface Abstraction

### 1. WorktreeManager — Purpose & Responsibilities

`src/core/worktree_manager.py` (1147 lines) is a `git`-via-GitPython wrapper (`import git`, `from git import GitCommandError, Repo`) around two structurally different worktree models that coexist in the same class:

- **The "official" isolated-per-agent model** (module docstring, `src/core/worktree_manager.py:1-14`): one `AgentWorktree`/`AgentBranch` DB row keyed 1:1 by `agent_id`, one branch (`{branch_prefix}{agent_id}`, default prefix `"agent-"` — `src/core/simple_config.py:60-63`), one worktree at `<repo>/.worktrees/wt_<agent_id>`.
- **The shared/integration-worktree model** actually used by the live pipeline for the common case (a whole feature or design shares one worktree across all its phases) — `WorktreeManager` has **no dedicated API for this model at all**. Callers in `orchestrator.py` construct a `WorktreeManager` purely to get a `.reload()`ed `Repo` handle (`wt_mgr.main_repo`) and `worktree_base`/`config` properties, then perform raw `git worktree add/remove` and `git merge` themselves. This is the root of the "AgentBranch only populated for the legacy path" observation already in the doc — confirmed by reading the actual call sites (§2).

**Full public API surface** (all file:line from `src/core/worktree_manager.py`):

| Method | Line | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `__init__(db_manager)` | `103` | Opens `Repo(main_repo_path)`; raises `ValueError` if not a valid git repo. |
| `reload(new_path)` | `124` | Re-point `main_repo`/`_project_root` at a different project's repo. **Instance-local only** — does not write through to the shared `get_config()` singleton, explicitly to prevent two `WorktreeManager` instances (different concurrent projects) from clobbering each other's `worktree_base`. |
| `worktree_base` (property) | `147` | `<repo>/.worktrees` unless `config.worktree_base_path` overrides it. |
| `_worktree_path_for(agent_id)` | `155` | `worktree_base / f"wt_{agent_id}"`. |
| `_ensure_excludes()` | `158` | Idempotently appends `.worktrees/` and `.hephaestus/` to `.git/info/exclude` (not the user's tracked `.gitignore`). |
| `_acquire_merge_lock` / `_release_merge_lock` | `200`, `230` | `fcntl.flock` on `.git/.hephaestus_merge_lock`, 300s timeout — serializes concurrent `merge_to_main` calls across agents. |
| `create_agent_worktree(agent_id, parent_agent_id, base_commit_sha, context_files)` | `241` | Creates branch `{branch_prefix}{agent_id}` from a resolved parent commit, `git worktree add`, writes `context_files` into git-excluded `.hephaestus/`, inserts an `AgentBranch` row. |
| `create_agent_branch(...)` | `364` | Deprecated alias for the above (docstring says so explicitly). |
| `switch_to_branch` / `switch_to_main` | `376`, `385` | No-ops — kept only for call-site compatibility with a pre-worktree-isolation API shape. |
| `commit_changes(agent_id, message)` | `433` | Stage+commit all changes in the agent's worktree, `commit_type="auto_save"`. |
| `commit_for_validation(agent_id, iteration, message)` | `439` | Same, `commit_type="validation_ready"`. |
| `merge_to_main(agent_id)` | `448` | Full merge pipeline: lock → commit any dirty work → checkout+clean `main_repo` → `git merge --no-ff` → on conflict, `_resolve_conflicts` (newest-file-wins) → mark `AgentBranch.merge_status="merged"`. |
| `merge_to_parent(agent_id)` | `785` | Alias for `merge_to_main`. |
| `_resolve_conflicts(agent_id, session, repo)` | `597` | Newest-file-wins: compares each conflicted file's last-commit timestamp on `HEAD` vs `MERGE_HEAD`, keeps the newer, records a `MergeConflictResolution` row. |
| `get_workspace_changes(agent_id, since_commit)` | `726` | Diff stats (`created`/`modified`/`deleted`, insertions/deletions) between `parent_commit_sha` and current `HEAD`. |
| `get_agent_branch_path(agent_id)` | `774` | Returns `AgentBranch.worktree_path` if a record exists, **else falls back to `str(self._project_root)` — the main repo path, not `None`**. |
| `_remove_worktree(worktree_path, require_clean)` | `791` | The one hard safety choke point: refuses to touch a path that resolves to the main repo; if `require_clean=True` (default), refuses to remove a worktree with uncommitted/staged/untracked changes — documented as closing a real incident where a `security_review` agent's uncommitted fixes were destroyed by a false "abandoned" sweep. Falls back to `shutil.rmtree` + `git worktree prune` if `git worktree remove` fails. |
| `cleanup_worktree(agent_id, delete_branch=False)` | `877` | Calls `_remove_worktree(..., require_clean=False)` — always force-removes, deliberately, since both live callers pass `delete_branch=True` (discard semantics) — then optionally `git branch -D`, sets `merge_status="cleaned"`. |
| `discard_agent(agent_id)` | `918` | `cleanup_worktree(agent_id, delete_branch=True)` — "what replaces the Repair flow: failed work never touches main." |
| `cleanup_all_stale_branches()` | `925` | Broad self-heal sweep (detailed in §2). |

**Direct git interaction**: every git operation is a GitPython call (`self.main_repo.git.branch(...)`, `.worktree(...)`, `.merge(...)`, `.stash(...)`, `.reset(...)`, `.clean(...)`) — no raw `subprocess` calls inside `WorktreeManager` itself (unlike `orchestrator.py`'s `_archive_and_cleanup`, which does use raw `subprocess.run(["git", ...])`, see §2). Branch naming: `{branch_prefix}{agent_id}` for the isolated-agent path (default prefix `"agent-"`); the shared/integration path uses entirely different conventions defined in `orchestrator.py`, not in `WorktreeManager` (see §2).

### 2. Worktree Lifecycle — and Which Model Actually Runs in Production

**Isolated-per-agent path** (rare — only reached when a `Task.workflow_id` has no shared `Workflow.working_directory`, e.g. validator/diagnostic/arbitration agents): `AgentManager.create_agent_for_task` calls `wt_mgr.create_agent_worktree(...)` directly (`src/agents/manager.py:489-500`). Cleanup goes through `discard_agent` on the failed-launch fallback path (`src/agents/manager.py:1130`) or through `cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s AgentBranch-scoped sweep. **`WorktreeManager.merge_to_main`/`merge_to_parent` are never called anywhere in the codebase outside `worktree_manager.py` itself** (verified: `grep -rn "merge_to_main\|merge_to_parent" src/` matches only the method definition and its alias) — the newest-file-wins merge pipeline with proper locking is dead code for the live pipeline. Legacy isolated branches that do get merged are actually merged by `cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s own separate `_merge_and_delete` inline closure (`:1090-1129`), a **third, independent merge implementation** with the same newest-file-wins conflict strategy but none of `merge_to_main`'s locking.

**Shared/integration-worktree path** (the common case — one worktree per design or per feature, reused across every phase): `WorktreeManager` is used purely as a scoped `Repo`-handle-and-path helper, never for its own create/cleanup/merge API:
- **Creation**: `_create_integration_worktree` (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/worktree_integration.py:61-128`) and the inline block in `run_single_workflow` (`__init__.py:857-884`, feature-architect/design worktree) both call `wt_mgr.main_repo.git.branch(...)` and `wt_mgr.main_repo.git.worktree("add", ...)` directly, bypassing `create_agent_worktree` entirely — no `AgentBranch` row is ever written for these worktrees. Branch naming here is **not** `{branch_prefix}{agent_id}` — it's `feature_architect/{design_id}` (`__init__.py:1405`, `__init__.py:1470`), `feature/{design_slug}/{feature_key}` (`worktree_integration.py:626`, `__init__.py:2168`, `__init__.py:2208`), or `feature/{design-label}` for the Phase 0/design-level shared branch (`__init__.py:869`).
- **Removal**: `_cleanup_worktree` (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/worktree_integration.py:129-227`) — called from `run_phase0` (`__init__.py:1729`), `_run_one_feature` (`__init__.py:2173`, `__init__.py:2362`), the background sweep `sweep_completed_workflow_worktrees` (`worktree_integration.py:321`), and three `autopilot_api.py` routes: `rerun_design` (`:1209-1215`), `delete_design` (`:3195-3201`), `delete_feature` (`:4622-4631`) — six call sites funneling into one function, so `_cleanup_worktree` itself is *not* duplicated. But it does **not** call `WorktreeManager.cleanup_worktree`/`discard_agent`/`_remove_worktree` — it calls `wt_mgr.main_repo.git.worktree("remove", str(worktree), "--force")` directly (`worktree_integration.py:175`), which means it bypasses `_remove_worktree`'s own main-repo safety guard and `require_clean` check entirely — despite `_remove_worktree`'s docstring explicitly claiming to be "the sole choke point every removal ... goes through." That claim is false for the shared-worktree path.
- **A fourth reimplementation**, `_archive_and_cleanup` (`src/autopilot/orchestrator/__init__.py:2632-2691`), copies `.hephaestus/*` artifacts out then calls raw `subprocess.run(["git", "worktree", "remove", "--force", ...])` — **confirmed dead code**: `grep -rn "_archive_and_cleanup(" src/autopilot/orchestrator/*.py` returns only the `def` line itself, no call sites anywhere in the file. This matches and confirms the existing doc's "apparently-unused" flag exactly.
- **Final merge into `main`**: a fifth, inline reimplementation in `run_single_workflow` (`:7849-7893`, detailed in §3) — its own abort-on-conflict logic, distinct from both `merge_to_main`'s newest-file-wins and `cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s newest-file-wins.

**Broad self-heal sweep**: `cleanup_all_stale_branches` (`worktree_manager.py:925-1143`) is `WorktreeManager`'s own generic sweep, called from `autopilot_api.py:1237` (background thread after `rerun_design`) and `:5509-5511` (a dedicated cleanup endpoint). Three steps: (1) remove every linked worktree not backing an `active`/`paused` `Workflow.working_directory`, using resolved (not raw) paths throughout specifically because macOS's `/var → /private/var` symlink previously made a raw-string "skip the main repo" comparison silently never match, letting this function attempt to delete the main repository itself (live-confirmed incident, independently defended by `_remove_worktree`'s own hard guard); (2) null `Workflow.working_directory` for terminal workflows whose worktree is already gone, using `.git` presence (not directory existence) as the liveness signal, since something keeps appending tmux transcripts to a removed worktree's `.hephaestus/tmux/` path and resurrecting an empty parent directory; (3) merge+delete tracked `AgentBranch` branches plus any **untracked** branch matching `startswith(("agent-", "autopilot-", "feature_architect/"))`.

**Verified gap**: none of the three actual shared-worktree branch naming conventions in production use (`feature/{design-label}`, `feature/{design_id}/{feature_key}`, and `feature_architect/{design_id}` is the only one covered) fully match that prefix filter — `feature/...` branches are invisible to `cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s untracked-branch fallback sweep. An `"autopilot-"` prefix is checked but never produced anywhere (`grep -n 'f"autopilot-' src/autopilot/orchestrator.py` returns nothing) — dead filter entry for a naming convention that doesn't exist. Only `feature_architect/` branches get generic-sweep coverage; `feature/{design}/{feature}` branches rely entirely on their owning workflow's own `_cleanup_worktree` call ever actually running.

**Confirming the existing doc's finding, with more precision**: the doc names three cleanup owners (`_cleanup_worktree`, `sweep_completed_workflow_worktrees`, `_archive_and_cleanup`). Reading `WorktreeManager` shows it's worse than three — `sweep_completed_workflow_worktrees` itself just calls `_cleanup_worktree`, so that's not a fourth independent implementation, but `WorktreeManager.cleanup_worktree`/`discard_agent` (AgentBranch-based, used only by the isolated-agent path) and `cleanup_all_stale_branches` (generic git-worktree-list-based sweep) are two *more* independent removal code paths inside `WorktreeManager` itself that the orchestrator-side finding didn't have visibility into. Total distinct removal implementations across the codebase: `_remove_worktree`/`cleanup_worktree` (WorktreeManager, AgentBranch-keyed, `require_clean`-guarded), `cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s inline worktree-list sweep (WorktreeManager, generic), `_cleanup_worktree` (orchestrator.py, raw `git worktree remove --force`, no require_clean check, no main-repo guard beyond what's implicit in path construction), and `_archive_and_cleanup` (orchestrator.py, raw subprocess, dead code) — **four**, not three, and only two of the four (`_remove_worktree` and its callers) go through the one function that actually guards against deleting the main repo or discarding uncommitted work.

### 3. Merge Conflict Handling

Three independent, behaviorally different conflict-resolution implementations exist for what is conceptually one operation ("merge a worktree's branch into `main`"):

1. **`WorktreeManager.merge_to_main`** (`:448-593`) — newest-file-wins: on `GitCommandError` containing `"CONFLICT"`/`"unresolved conflict"`, calls `_resolve_conflicts`, which for each conflicted path compares the last-commit timestamp on `HEAD` vs `MERGE_HEAD` (falls back to `datetime.utcnow()` if either side has no history) and keeps the newer file's content, logging a `MergeConflictResolution` row per file, then commits with `--no-verify`. **Dead in production** — no live caller (§2).
2. **`cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s `_merge_and_delete`** (`:1090-1129`) — same newest-file-wins strategy via the same `_resolve_conflicts` helper, but on any conflict it *also* falls through to force-deleting the branch (`git branch -D`) if resolution itself raises, logging it as `failed` rather than leaving the branch around. This is the merge path actually exercised for legacy isolated-agent branches.
3. **The final-merge block in `run_single_workflow`** (`:7849-7893`) — the one referenced in the Orchestrator Core section. **Confirmed and detailed**: on a clean merge, commits directly (`status="success"`). On `GitCommandError` where `"CONFLICT" in str(e)`, it does **not** attempt any resolution — it calls `wt_mgr.main_repo.git.merge("--abort")` and logs "Conflict detected — branch {design_branch} preserved for manual merge/PR", then falls through to mark the workflow `"completed"` anyway with the feature/design branch never merged into `main`. This is a materially different strategy from both of `WorktreeManager`'s own conflict handlers (which auto-resolve and always land a merge commit) — the design/feature-level shared-worktree merge (the common case) simply gives up and leaves human intervention as the only path to actually land the work; it doesn't fail the workflow, it silently succeeds with an unmerged branch, distinguishable only by grepping logs for "Conflict detected."

There is no test or alert surfacing case 3's outcome to a human — "preserved for manual merge/PR" is aspirational prose in a log line; nothing actually opens a PR or notifies anyone.

### 4. CLIAgentInterface Contract

`CLIAgentInterface` (ABC, `src/interfaces/cli_interface.py:67-388`):

**Abstract (must override):**

| Method | Line | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `get_launch_command(system_prompt, **kwargs) -> LaunchResult` | `146` | Builds the shell command that launches this CLI in the tmux pane; `LaunchResult.prompt_delivery` tells the caller how the system prompt actually reached the agent (`AGENT_FILE`/`FLAG`/`MESSAGE`/`DEFERRED`/`NONE`). |
| `get_health_check_pattern() -> str` | `160` | Regex checked against pane output to decide the agent launched successfully. |
| `format_message(message) -> str` | `169` | Per-CLI message formatting before it's typed into the pane. |
| `get_stuck_patterns() -> List[str]` | `181` | Regexes indicating the agent is stuck (rate limits, auth failures, etc.). |
| `parse_output(output) -> Dict` | `190` | Extracts last-message/waiting-state from a captured pane snapshot. |

**Polymorphic hooks (concrete defaults, override to opt in):**

| Method | Line | Default | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `get_session_args(session_id)` | `93` | `""` | CLI-specific session resume/create flag. |
| `record_session(session_id, working_directory, launched_at)` | `107` | `False`, no-op | Persist CLI-assigned session state post-launch (only Codex needs this — it mints its own UUID). |
| `prepare_working_directory(working_directory)` | `118` | no-op | One-time per-directory setup before first launch (trust-dialog pre-acceptance). |
| `post_launch_confirmation_keys()` | `127` | `[]` | tmux keys for a second interactive confirmation dialog that skip-permission flags don't suppress. |
| `format_goal_command(condition)` | `136` | `""` | CLI-native "keep working until X" command. |
| `needs_chunked_delivery` (attr) | `77-83` | `False` | Whether the initial prompt must be sent in 2500-char chunks. |
| `default_model` (attr) | `85-91` | `"sonnet"` | This CLI's own default model. |
| `recovery_keystrokes()` | `201` | `[]` | tmux keys to break a stuck TUI before a nudge message. |
| `mcp_reconnect_instructions(server_name)` | `208` | `""` | Chat-message text telling the agent how to reconnect a dropped MCP server, in this CLI's vocabulary. |
| `fallback_model(config, is_primary)` | `218` | role lookup (not overridden per CLI) | Resolves `cli_model_fallback`/`secondary_cli_model_fallback` by role. |
| `model_fallback_keystrokes(model)` | `240` | `[]` | Literal pane inputs (text, wait-seconds) to switch the active model. |
| `model_fallback_confirmed(output, model)` | `252` | `None` | Whether recent output confirms a model switch landed. |
| `get_tui_status_patterns()` | `347` | `[]` | Patterns that look like garbled output but are actually normal TUI chrome. |
| `strip_tui_chrome(text)` | `358` | identity | Strip this CLI's TUI frame (status bars, spinners) from a captured snapshot. |

Shared, non-overridden helpers: `_save_prompt_to_file`, `_get_model`, `_extract_id`/`_extract_ids_from_prompt`/`_build_ids_line`, `is_healthy`/`is_stuck` (built from `get_health_check_pattern`/`get_stuck_patterns`).

### 5. Per-CLI Comparison

| CLI class | `needs_chunked_delivery` | `default_model` | `prompt_delivery` | Launch flags of note | Session resume | Distinctive overrides |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`ClaudeCodeAgent`** (`:396-637`) | `True` | `"sonnet"` (inherited) | `AGENT_FILE` if a `~/.claude/agents/hephaestus-{phase}.md` exists, else `FLAG` via `--append-system-prompt` | `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, `--effort {low/medium/high}` mapped from `thinking_level`, `--mcp-config` if present | UUID-based, two-flag (`--session-id` then `--resume`, order picked by `_claude_session_exists` probing `~/.claude/projects/<sanitized-path>/<uuid>.jsonl`) | `prepare_working_directory` pre-seeds `~/.claude.json` trust flags under an `fcntl` lock; `format_goal_command` → `/goal {condition}`; `model_fallback_keystrokes` → one-line `/model {name}` |
| **`OpenCodeAgent`** (`:640-717`) | `False` (default) | `"anthropic/claude-sonnet-4"` | `MESSAGE` — system prompt **is** the initial message, `instructions_pointer` folded in here since OpenCode gets no separate post-launch message step | `-i` (stay interactive after first message — bare `opencode run` is one-shot and exits) `--dangerously-skip-permissions` | none (no `get_session_args` override) | Only CLI with `needs_chunked_delivery=False` among the five real CLIs — because its prompt delivery model is different in kind (one message at launch, not a launch + follow-up pointer) |
| **`DroidAgent`** (`:720-769`) | `True` | `"sonnet"` (inherited) | `NONE` — system prompt is dropped entirely; launch command is just `droid` with no prompt flag | none beyond the `PATH` prefix | none | Thinnest implementation in the file — no overrides beyond the abstract methods and `needs_chunked_delivery`; relies entirely on the caller's task message for all context since the system prompt is discarded |
| **`CodexAgent`** (`:772-904`) | `True` | `""` (empty — no `--model` flag emitted unless a caller supplies one) | `DEFERRED` — system prompt appended to the task-instructions file, agent told to read it | `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox --no-alt-screen` | Own UUID session map on disk (`.hephaestus/codex_sessions.json`), reconciled post-launch by scanning `~/.codex/sessions/**/*.jsonl` for a matching `session_meta` transcript (`record_session`); launch does `codex resume {id} ... \|\| codex ...` to fall back to a fresh session if the saved one is already claimed by another process | Only CLI that overrides `record_session` (all others accept the deterministic ID directly or need no persistence) |
| **`PiAgent`** (`:907-1111`) | `True` | `"Qwen3.8-27B-UD-Q4_K_XL.gguf"` — the only local/self-hosted default among the six | `FLAG` via `--append-system-prompt` (agent-file body with YAML frontmatter stripped, or raw `system_prompt` as fallback) | `--thinking {level}`, `--approve --no-context-files`, plus `get_session_args` | `--session-id {id}` (create-or-resume, single flag — unlike Claude's two-flag dance) or `--no-session` | Only CLI overriding `get_tui_status_patterns` and `strip_tui_chrome` (strips a persistent status bar + braille spinner frames via a hand-rolled frame-walk); only CLI overriding `recovery_keystrokes` (`["Escape"]`) and `mcp_reconnect_instructions` (`mcp connect <server>`); `model_fallback_keystrokes` uses a single atomic `/model {name}` send (comment explains a two-step picker-based approach was tried and failed 58/64 times live) with a matching `model_fallback_confirmed` regex |
| **`SwarmCodeAgent`** (`:1114-1136`) | `False` (default — never set) | `"sonnet"` (inherited default, never overridden) | `NONE` | `--autonomous --context {file}`, prompt written via `echo` to a `/tmp` file rather than `_save_prompt_to_file` | none | Docstring says `"hypothetical advanced agent"`; only class overriding `format_message` to prefix `"TASK: "` despite never actually being dispatched to (§6 confirms) |

`CLI_AGENTS` registry (`:1140-1147`): `{"claude": ClaudeCodeAgent, "opencode": OpenCodeAgent, "droid": DroidAgent, "codex": CodexAgent, "pi": PiAgent, "swarm": SwarmCodeAgent}`. `get_cli_agent(agent_type)` (`:1150-1166`) is a flat dict lookup raising `ValueError` on an unknown type — no lazy loading, no plugin discovery, all six classes imported unconditionally at module load.

**`SwarmCodeAgent` usage check**: `grep -rn "SwarmCodeAgent\|\"swarm\"\|'swarm'\|cli_type.*swarm" src/ config/` turns up only the class definition, its `CLI_AGENTS` registry entry, and no config file, workflow YAML, or code path that sets `cli_type="swarm"` or references `SwarmCodeAgent` anywhere else. The existing doc's "hypothetical/not in general use" characterization is confirmed accurate — it is registered and fully importable/instantiable but has zero real invocation paths.

### 6. Known Fragile Spots / Code Smells

- **`get_agent_branch_path`'s fallback silently returns the main repo, not `None`**. `restart_agent` (`src/agents/manager.py:2388-2403`) calls this exactly when `Workflow.working_directory` is unset/missing, then checks only `Path(candidate).exists()` before using it as the relaunch directory. Since the main repo path always exists, an agent with no `AgentBranch` record that needs a restart-without-workflow-worktree will silently relaunch **into the main project repository** rather than failing loudly or falling back to `project_path` explicitly. This is the same class of bug the shared-worktree "fail loudly instead of silently forking a disconnected replacement" comment in `create_agent_for_task` was written to prevent — but the guard doesn't cover this call path.
- **`_cleanup_worktree`'s raw `git worktree remove --force` bypasses `_remove_worktree`'s safety net entirely** (§2) — no `require_clean` check, so a genuinely-dirty shared worktree (e.g. a straggler goto-triggered task's agent still mid-write) removed via `_cleanup_worktree` from `delete_feature`/`delete_design`/`rerun_design` has no protection against destroying uncommitted work, unlike the isolated-agent path which does.
- **Three independent conflict-resolution strategies for conceptually one operation** (§3): two auto-resolve with newest-file-wins, one aborts-and-preserves-with-no-notification. A future contributor fixing a merge bug in one has no way to know two siblings exist doing something different.
- **`merge_to_main` and `cleanup_worktree` (WorktreeManager's own primitives) are effectively dead code** for the shared-worktree model that the live pipeline predominantly uses — `WorktreeManager`'s advertised API surface (create → commit → merge → cleanup) is only exercised end-to-end for the rare isolated-agent path.
- **`_archive_and_cleanup` is confirmed dead code** — defined, never called. It also duplicates `_cleanup_worktree`'s tmux-transcript-archiving logic with different exclusion rules and uses raw `subprocess.run(["git", ...])` instead of GitPython, the only place in the worktree-management code that does so.
- **`cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s untracked-branch prefix filter is stale relative to actual branch-naming conventions** (§2): checks `("agent-", "autopilot-", "feature_architect/")`; the shared/integration path's actual branches are `feature/{design-label}` and `feature/{design_id}/{feature_key}`, neither matched. `"autopilot-"` is dead filter text — nothing in the codebase produces a branch with that prefix.
- **CLI hook coverage is uneven in ways that look like bugs, not deliberate design**: `DroidAgent` has `needs_chunked_delivery=True` but overrides nothing else — no `recovery_keystrokes`, no `mcp_reconnect_instructions`, no `strip_tui_chrome` — meaning a stuck Droid agent gets no mechanical Escape-key recovery and a dropped MCP connection gets no CLI-specific reconnect instructions, unlike `PiAgent` which has both. It looks like `DroidAgent` was copy-pasted from an earlier pre-hook-addition state and never revisited when the polymorphic hooks were added.
- **`SwarmCodeAgent` overrides `format_message` to prefix `"TASK: "`** — the only CLI doing so — for a class with zero live dispatch paths. If it's ever wired up, this formatting choice has never been exercised against a real CLI and is unverified.
- **Per-CLI `parse_output` implementations are near-identical hand-copies** (`ClaudeCodeAgent`, `OpenCodeAgent`, `DroidAgent`, `PiAgent` — four separate ~15-line backward-scan-for-prompt-marker implementations differing only in which literal marker strings they check) rather than one parameterized helper taking a list of prompt markers.
- **`CodexAgent.default_model = ""`** is a special-cased empty string specifically so `model_flag` is omitted from the launch command; every other CLI's `_get_model` fallback assumes a non-empty default is always meaningful to pass; Codex silently breaks that assumption in a way a future refactor of `_get_model`/`default_model` could easily miss.

### 7. Refactor Observations

1. **Give the shared/integration-worktree model a real `WorktreeManager` API.** Right now `orchestrator.py` treats `WorktreeManager` as a `Repo`-handle factory and reimplements branch creation, worktree creation, removal, and merging from scratch four separate times (§2). A `create_shared_worktree(branch_name, base_ref)` / `merge_shared_branch(branch_name, *, on_conflict)` pair on `WorktreeManager` — used by both the isolated-agent and shared-worktree callers — would collapse four ad-hoc implementations into one tested path and let `_remove_worktree`'s safety guards actually apply everywhere.
2. **Pick one conflict-resolution strategy and delete the other two.** `merge_to_main` (dead), `cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s inline merge (live, legacy-branch only), and `run_single_workflow`'s abort-and-preserve block (live, shared-worktree final merge) should not diverge in behavior for what is the same conceptual operation.
3. **Remove `_archive_and_cleanup` and `merge_to_main`/`WorktreeManager.cleanup_worktree`-if-truly-unreachable**, or wire them in if they represent intended-but-never-finished functionality — both are confirmed dead code carrying real behavioral logic that a future reader will reasonably assume is load-bearing.
4. **Reconcile `cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s branch-prefix filter with actual naming conventions** (drop `"autopilot-"`, add `"feature/"`), or centralize branch-name construction into `WorktreeManager` so there's one source of truth instead of prefix strings duplicated across two files.
5. **Fix `get_agent_branch_path`'s fallback** to return `None` when there's no `AgentBranch` record, matching the "fail loudly rather than silently fork/redirect" principle `create_agent_for_task` already applies to the shared-worktree case.
6. **Extract a shared `_parse_prompt_style_output(output, markers)` helper** for the four near-identical `parse_output` implementations in `cli_interface.py`, and add a lightweight test check that every concrete `CLIAgentInterface` subclass explicitly acknowledges (even if declining) `recovery_keystrokes`/`mcp_reconnect_instructions`/`strip_tui_chrome` — so a `DroidAgent`-shaped gap gets caught at review time instead of discovered live.
## Monitoring: Guardian, Conductor, Trajectory

### 1. Purpose & responsibilities

The names suggest four peer subsystems; the code shows something closer to one orchestrator (`MonitoringLoop` in `src/monitoring/monitor.py`) with two LLM-calling collaborators it composes and two collaborators for reconciliation/diagnostics — plus a fifth piece (dedicated persistence tables) that neither collaborator actually touches itself.

- **`MonitoringLoop`** (`src/monitoring/monitor.py`, 3676 lines) is the actual engine. Its `start()` loop (`monitor.py:233-249`) is the single entrypoint for everything else in this section — it owns scheduling, ~13 cheap regex/pattern-based "mechanical recovery" detectors (connection errors, spend limits, MCP disconnects, frozen output, etc.), calls into Guardian and Conductor, persists their results to dedicated DB tables (Guardian/Conductor don't persist their own output — see below), runs the stuck-task nudge/fail sweep, delegates orphan-session reconciliation, and runs the diagnostic-agent stuck-workflow state machine. It is the composition root: `Guardian` and `Conductor` are instantiated inside `MonitoringLoop.__init__` (`monitor.py:202-210`) and never used anywhere else in the codebase.
- **`Guardian`** (`src/monitoring/guardian.py`) is a *per-agent* trajectory analyzer: one LLM call per agent per cycle that decides whether that one agent is on-track, and if not, produces a steering message. It has no scheduling of its own — `MonitoringLoop._guardian_analysis_for_agent` (`monitor.py:2276-2510`) drives it once per active agent per monitoring cycle.
- **`Conductor`** (`src/monitoring/conductor.py`) is *system-wide*: one LLM call per cycle that takes the whole batch of Guardian summaries and looks for cross-agent duplicate work, resource conflicts, and overall coherence. It only runs if Guardian produced at least one summary that cycle (`monitor.py:2110`).
- **`OrphanSessionReaper`** (`src/monitoring/orphan_reaper.py`) is unrelated to trajectory/LLM analysis — it's pure DB/tmux reconciliation: kill tmux sessions with no live `Agent` row, and terminate `Agent` rows whose workflow is no longer active. No LLM calls, no steering.
- **`TrajectoryContext`** (`src/monitoring/trajectory_context.py`) is a regex-based context-extraction utility (goals, constraints, "current focus", blockers) built from `AgentLog` history. Notably, **it is dead code from Guardian's actual call path** — see §7.
- **`prompt_loader.py`**'s `PromptLoader` class is *not* what Guardian/Conductor use to format their LLM prompts today (see §7) — it formats validator prompts (`format_result_validation_prompt`, `format_task_validation_prompt`) that are unrelated to Guardian/Conductor steering.
- **`signals.py`**'s `SignalQueue`/`MonitorSignal` is a thread-safe, in-memory (not DB-backed) queue that `_guardian_analysis_for_agent` emits into (`monitor.py:2454-2467`) and `src/autopilot/orchestrator.py` (line ~7920) polls from — a genuinely separate one-way channel from Guardian/Conductor's DB-row outputs.

### 2. Trigger mechanism

Everything in this section is **polling**, driven by one loop:

- `MonitoringLoop.start()` (`monitor.py:233-249`) is a `while self.running:` loop that calls `await self._monitoring_cycle()` then `await asyncio.sleep(self.config.monitoring_interval_seconds)` (`monitor.py:244-249`). `monitoring_interval_seconds` defaults to 60s (`src/core/simple_config.py:137`, `monitoring.interval_seconds` in `hephaestus_config.yaml`). It also writes a heartbeat file each iteration (`monitor.py:241-242`) for external watchdogs.
- `_monitoring_cycle()` (`monitor.py:1984-2274`) runs, in order, every cycle:
  1. **Phase 0 — mechanical recovery** (`monitor.py:2026-2055`): ~13 cheap detectors run per-agent, sequentially, no LLM (credit exhaustion, never-started, frozen-output/keystroke recovery, model fallback, repetition loop, dangerous-command confirmation, max-token-limit, unconfirmed completion, MCP disconnect, connection errors, bad-model error).
  2. **Phase 1 — Guardian** (`monitor.py:2057-2107`): one `asyncio.create_task` per non-mechanically-intervened agent, gathered in parallel via `asyncio.gather` (`monitor.py:2082-2084`).
  3. **Phase 2 — Conductor** (`monitor.py:2109-2139`): runs once against the whole batch of Guardian summaries, only if the batch is non-empty.
  4. **Orphan cleanup** (`monitor.py:2141-2145`) — delegates to `OrphanSessionReaper.cleanup_orphaned_tmux_sessions()`.
  5. Workflow auto-discovery / workflow-switch bookkeeping (`monitor.py:2147-2202`).
  6. **Phase 3 — system health audit** (`monitor.py:2216-2220`), which itself does two things: delegates duplicate/consistency checks to `run_health_audit()` (`src/mcp/autopilot_api.py`), then runs an inline stuck-task nudge/fail sweep (`monitor.py:2900-3061`).
  7. **Diagnostic stuck-workflow check** (`monitor.py:2257-2264`) — `_check_workflow_stuck_state()`.

There is no event-driven trigger anywhere in this subsystem — no webhook, no DB trigger, no "agent just went stuck" callback. Everything is discovered by re-scanning DB + tmux state once per `monitoring_interval_seconds`. The one exception to "poll only" is that Guardian's steering decision is *read* asynchronously by the orchestrator via `SignalQueue` (`signals.py`), which is itself polled by the orchestrator's own loop, not pushed.

### 3. Guardian deep-dive

**Per-agent entrypoint**: `Guardian.analyze_agent_with_trajectory` (`guardian.py:115-289`), called from `MonitoringLoop._guardian_analysis_for_agent` (`monitor.py:2406-2410`).

**Signals/context fed into the LLM call**:
- `_build_accumulated_context` (`guardian.py:291-361`) — reads *all* `AgentLog` rows for the agent (`guardian.py:309-314`, unbounded — no limit), extracts overall goal/done-definition from the `Task` row, and folds in `constraints`/`lifted_constraints`/`evolved_goal` from `past_summaries`. This is a much simpler, DB-log-based context builder than `trajectory_context.py`'s regex-heavy `TrajectoryContext.build_accumulated_context` — and it's the one Guardian actually calls. `TrajectoryContext` is unused by Guardian (see §7).
- `_get_agent_task` (`guardian.py:631-649`) and `_get_phase_context` (`guardian.py:651-726`) — task/phase metadata, including the phase's `required_output` override resolved fresh from `workflow.yaml` via `load_phase_output_artifacts` (`guardian.py:696`) rather than the DB's possibly-stale `phase.outputs` snapshot.
- Raw tmux output, sanitized via `_sanitize_tmux_output_for_llm` (`guardian.py:39-43`) which strips Claude Code's benign session-ID-reuse error text (`_BENIGN_SESSION_ERROR_RE`, `guardian.py:34-36`) before the LLM sees it — added because the LLM was previously misreading that as a live problem and fabricating a "fix your session conflict" steering message (see the comment block `guardian.py:23-33`).

**The LLM call**: `self.llm_provider.analyze_agent_trajectory(...)` (`guardian.py:196-212`), wrapped in `asyncio.wait_for(..., timeout=90)` (`guardian.py:194-196`). Response is validated against `GuardianTrajectoryAnalysis` (`models.py:8-46`) somewhere inside the LLM provider (not visible in this file). On timeout, `_consecutive_timeouts[agent.id]` increments (`guardian.py:215-216`); after `GUARDIAN_TIMEOUT_ESCALATION_THRESHOLD = 3` (`guardian.py:21`) consecutive timeouts, Guardian stops returning the benign "aligned" default and instead returns `_get_timeout_escalation_analysis` (`guardian.py:745-779`) — `needs_steering=True, steering_type="stuck"` — deliberately routing a *broken analysis pipeline* into the same steering/auto-restart path a real stuck-trajectory detection would use. This was a direct fix for an observed incident (comment block `guardian.py:222-231`): an agent hard-stopped on a model error and Guardian reported "aligned, no steering needed" for 12+ minutes because every cycle independently timed out and independently fell back to the neutral default.

**Decisions produced**: `needs_steering`, `steering_type` (one of `SteeringType` — `guardian.py:46-54`), `steering_message`. These are NOT written directly to `SteeringIntervention` by Guardian — Guardian itself never touches that table (confirmed: no reference to `SteeringIntervention` anywhere in `guardian.py`; only `src/mcp/api.py` and `src/mcp/server.py` read/delete from it, and nothing writes to it — it appears to be dead/never-populated, see §7).

**How interventions actually get applied**:
1. `Guardian.steer_agent` (`guardian.py:363-452`) is called directly by `MonitoringLoop._guardian_analysis_for_agent` (`monitor.py:2469-2475`) — this is a **direct call into the live agent**, not a DB-row-that-something-else-polls. It:
   - Skips if the task is already `done` (`guardian.py:383-389`).
   - Gates on `_evaluate_steering_eligibility` (`guardian.py:473-531`): STUCK/idle acts on first flag; everything else (drifting/off_track/over_engineering/confused/violating_constraints) requires 2 consecutive same-type flags within a 10-minute window (`guardian.py:498-517`) plus an overall 10-minute cooldown (`guardian.py:522-529`) — this consolidated gate replaced a docstring-only claim ("flagged multiple times") that the caller previously never enforced (`guardian.py:396-404`).
   - Skips if a message is already queued in the CLI (`guardian.py:415-424`, checks for `"Press up to edit queued messages"` in live tmux output).
   - For STUCK/idle only, sends recovery keystrokes first (`guardian.py:433-434`) — deliberately *not* done for softer concerns, since interrupting a real in-flight file write for a single LLM judgment call destroyed real progress in the past (`guardian.py:426-432`).
   - Sends the message via `AgentManager.send_message_to_agent` (`guardian.py:436`) and logs an `AgentLog` row with `log_type="guardian_steering"` (`guardian.py:440-452`) — **not** `SteeringIntervention`.
2. **Auto-restart escalation** is orchestrated by `MonitoringLoop`, not Guardian: `_guardian_analysis_for_agent` counts `consecutive_stuck` from the last 5 past summaries (`monitor.py:2425-2431`), and if `>= self.config.max_ignored_steering` (default 3, `hephaestus_config.yaml:106`) and the agent hasn't had activity in the last 300s (`monitor.py:2480-2484`), calls `MonitoringLoop._auto_restart_agent` (`monitor.py:2512-2593`) — which kills the tmux session, resets the `Task` to `pending` (`monitor.py:2542-2554`, a fix for a previously-observed bug where the task stayed `assigned`/`in_progress` pointing at a corpse agent, `monitor.py:2515-2533`), and marks the `Agent` `terminated`.
3. `Guardian.record_auto_restart` (`guardian.py:612-619`) is a thin public wrapper `MonitoringLoop` calls afterward purely to keep `steering_history` accurate — not a separate action path.

**Persistence**: `MonitoringLoop._update_agent_health_from_trajectory` (`monitor.py:2655-2725`), called right after `_guardian_analysis_for_agent` gets its analysis back (`monitor.py:2502`), is what actually writes the `GuardianAnalysis` row (`monitor.py:2694-2712`) and increments `Agent.health_check_failures` (`monitor.py:2679-2683`). Guardian itself only has an **in-process cache** (`self.trajectory_cache`, `guardian.py:98`, `271-275`) — this is explicitly for Conductor to read (`get_cached_trajectory`, `guardian.py:781-783`), though nothing in `conductor.py` actually calls it (Conductor gets its `guardian_summaries` as a plain argument from `MonitoringLoop`, not from Guardian's cache — see §7).

### 4. Conductor deep-dive

**Entrypoint**: `Conductor.analyze_system_state(guardian_summaries)` (`conductor.py:59-239`), called once per cycle from `_monitoring_cycle` (`monitor.py:2115-2117`) with the full list of that cycle's Guardian analysis dicts (already collected by `MonitoringLoop`, not fetched by Conductor itself).

**Inputs**: purely the in-memory `guardian_summaries` list passed in — no direct DB reads of `GuardianAnalysis` rows, no reads of `Agent`/`Task` state beyond what the caller already put in each summary dict. `system_goals` is a hardcoded dict (`conductor.py:111-115`) — not configurable, not read from workflow config.

**LLM call**: `llm_provider.analyze_system_coherence(guardian_summaries=..., system_goals=...)` (`conductor.py:149-152`) — **no timeout wrapper** (contrast Guardian's `asyncio.wait_for(..., timeout=90)`). A hanging Conductor LLM call blocks the entire `_monitoring_cycle`, which blocks the next `asyncio.sleep` iteration for all agents system-wide, not just one. The whole call is wrapped in a bare `try/except Exception` (`conductor.py:99-239`) that falls back to `_get_empty_analysis()` (`conductor.py:354-364`) on any failure — but that's a fallback on *exception*, not on *timeout*, so an LLM call that hangs rather than throws is not covered.

**Response validated against**: `ConductorSystemAnalysis` (`models.py:49-73`) presumably inside the LLM provider (not visible here either).

**Decisions produced** (`SystemDecision` enum, `conductor.py:16-23`): `TERMINATE_DUPLICATE`, `COORDINATE_RESOURCES`, `ESCALATE` (built from LLM output at `conductor.py:170-204`; `CREATE_MISSING_TASK` is declared in the enum but never constructed anywhere in this file).

**How decisions get applied**: `Conductor.execute_decisions` → `_execute_single_decision` (`conductor.py:241-337`), called directly from `_monitoring_cycle` (`monitor.py:2126-2129`) right after the analysis — again a **direct call**, not a DB-row-polled pattern:
- `TERMINATE_DUPLICATE` (`conductor.py:262-297`): has a hardcoded safety check against terminating `validator`/`result_validator` agent types (`conductor.py:270-276`), logs an `AgentLog` row (`conductor.py:283-294`), then calls `agent_manager.terminate_agent(agent_id)` (`conductor.py:297`) — this DOES go through the shared `terminate_agent` primitive (which has its own task-reset safety net per the commit history, §6), unlike Guardian's auto-restart path in `MonitoringLoop` which does its own raw DB write.
- `COORDINATE_RESOURCES` (`conductor.py:299-330`): sends priority/wait messages to agents sharing a resource, skipping any agent whose task is already `done` (`conductor.py:305-319`).
- `ESCALATE` (`conductor.py:332-336`): only `logger.critical(...)` — comment explicitly says "Could trigger alerts, send notifications, etc." (`conductor.py:337`) — **no actual escalation mechanism exists**.

**Persistence**: same pattern as Guardian — Conductor itself never writes `ConductorAnalysis`/`DetectedDuplicate`. `MonitoringLoop._save_conductor_analysis` (`monitor.py:2727-2788`), called right after `analyze_system_state` returns (`monitor.py:2123`), does that: builds `ConductorAnalysis` (`monitor.py:2749-2758`), then one `DetectedDuplicate` row per duplicate (`monitor.py:2761-2770`), plus a backward-compat `AgentLog` entry (`monitor.py:2773-2780`).

**Downstream consumers of `ConductorAnalysis`**: read-only, via `src/mcp/api.py` (dashboard endpoints, lines ~1272-1439) and `monitor.py:3505-3506` (the diagnostic-agent context gatherer reads the latest `ConductorAnalysis` for its prompt). Nothing re-derives control flow from a *past* `ConductorAnalysis` row — it's write-once-per-cycle, read-for-display/context only.

### 5. Orphan reaper deep-dive

`OrphanSessionReaper.cleanup_orphaned_tmux_sessions` (`orphan_reaper.py:33-243`), called every cycle via `MonitoringLoop._cleanup_orphaned_tmux_sessions` → `self._orphan_reaper.cleanup_orphaned_tmux_sessions()` (`monitor.py:3063-3075`). Extracted from `MonitoringLoop` in commit `1998a11` ("SOLID review 3.4", per the class docstring `orphan_reaper.py:1-12`) — the pre-extraction code is byte-for-byte what's here (verified via `git show 1998a11`).

Two distinct notions of "orphaned" are handled, in this order:

1. **Orphaned *agent*** — a `working`/`idle`/`stuck` `Agent` row whose current task's `Workflow.status` is not `active`/`running` (`orphan_reaper.py:64-99`). Grace window: skipped if `agent.last_activity` is within the last 30 seconds (`orphan_reaper.py:109-119`) — deliberately tolerant of a workflow-status flip that hasn't propagated yet while the agent is demonstrably still alive. If stale, sets `agent.status = "terminated"`, clears `current_task_id`, sets `terminated_at` (`orphan_reaper.py:123-125`), and — per the most recent commit touching this file (`27e8e88`, 2026-08-13) — also resets the `Task` back to `pending` if it was `assigned`/`in_progress`/`pending` (`orphan_reaper.py:137-139`), closing a previously-audited gap where a dangling task pointed at a terminated agent indefinitely.
2. **Orphaned *tmux session*** — a tmux session named `agent*` (`orphan_reaper.py:44-45`) with no matching `Agent.tmux_session_name` among the active-agent set (`orphan_reaper.py:161-177`). Protected by `GRACE_PERIOD_SECONDS = 120` (`orphan_reaper.py:26`) measured from `self.last_check_time`, which is itself only advanced once a full detection pass runs (`orphan_reaper.py:150-159, 180`) — the very first call after startup unconditionally returns without killing anything (`orphan_reaper.py:150-155`), specifically to give freshly-launched agents time to register in the DB before being mistaken for orphans.

Cleanup action for an orphaned session: best-effort final flush of the stability-tracked "clean" transcript before it's lost (`orphan_reaper.py:193-222`, looks up *any* `Agent` row — live or dead — that last used that session name, since by definition no live one exists), then `tmux_sess.kill_session()` (`orphan_reaper.py:227`).

### 6. Timestamp/staleness audit

Every staleness/timeout comparison found across the 9 in-scope files, with UTC-consistency verdict:

| # | File:Line | Comparison | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `guardian.py:274` | `"timestamp": datetime.utcnow()` (trajectory cache write) | OK |
| 2 | `guardian.py:340` | `"session_start": ... datetime.utcnow()` fallback | OK |
| 3 | `guardian.py:448` | `AgentLog.details["timestamp"] = datetime.utcnow().isoformat()` | OK |
| 4 | `guardian.py:467-469` | `_should_steer_agent`: `datetime.fromisoformat(s["timestamp"]) > datetime.utcnow() - timedelta(minutes=10)` | OK (both sides utcnow-derived) |
| 5 | `guardian.py:500-505` | `_evaluate_steering_eligibility` consecutive-flag staleness: `now = datetime.utcnow()`, `(now - flag_state["last_seen"]) > timedelta(minutes=10)` | OK |
| 6 | `guardian.py:526` | steering cooldown: `datetime.fromisoformat(s["timestamp"]) > datetime.utcnow() - timedelta(minutes=10)` | OK |
| 7 | `conductor.py:208, 223, 344, 357` | timestamps on analysis records / `get_system_summary` age calc | OK, all `datetime.utcnow()` |
| 8 | `trajectory_context.py:60` | context cache TTL: `cached["timestamp"] > datetime.utcnow() - self.cache_ttl` | OK |
| 9 | `trajectory_context.py:111, 127, 509` | `last_activity`/cache-write fallbacks | OK |
| 10 | `monitor.py:448, 1880` | `workflow.paused_at = datetime.utcnow()` | OK |
| 11 | `monitor.py:510, 2677` | `_db_agent.last_activity = datetime.utcnow()` | OK |
| 12 | `monitor.py:1810` | `db_agent.terminated_at = datetime.utcnow()` | OK |
| 13 | `monitor.py:1946` | `_detect_agent_never_started`: `elapsed = (datetime.utcnow() - agent.last_activity).total_seconds()` vs `NEVER_STARTED_GRACE_SECONDS` (240s, `monitor.py:1895`) | OK |
| 14 | `monitor.py:2292` | Guardian grace period: `agent_age_seconds = (datetime.utcnow() - agent.created_at).total_seconds()` vs `guardian_min_agent_age_seconds` | OK |
| 15 | `monitor.py:2482` | auto-restart idle check: `(datetime.utcnow() - agent.last_activity).total_seconds()` vs 300s | OK |
| 16 | `monitor.py:2905, 2931, 2950` | stuck-task idle cutoff: `idle_cutoff = datetime.utcnow() - idle_minutes`, `last_seen >= idle_cutoff`, `datetime.utcnow() - nudged_at < idle_minutes` | OK |
| 17 | `monitor.py:3019` | `task.completed_at = datetime.utcnow()` | OK |
| 18 | `monitor.py:3185` | phase-completion cooldown: `(datetime.utcnow() - recent_phase_completion.completed_at).total_seconds()` vs 120s | OK |
| 19 | `monitor.py:3259` | diagnostic cooldown: `(datetime.utcnow() - last_diagnostic.triggered_at).total_seconds()` vs `diagnostic_cooldown_seconds` | OK |
| 20 | `monitor.py:3294` | diagnostic stuck-time: `(datetime.utcnow() - latest_task_time).total_seconds()` vs `diagnostic_min_stuck_time_seconds` | OK |
| 21 | `monitor.py:267, 476, 592, 673, 860, 976, 1027, 1225, 1285, 1386, 1480, 1557` | mechanical-recovery detectors use `time.time()` against in-memory-only dicts (`self._stuck_state`, `self._denied_dangerous_cmds`, etc.) | Not a DB-timestamp comparison — self-consistent monotonic epoch time, resets cleanly on process restart. Not a violation, but worth flagging as a **third** time representation alongside naive-UTC and tz-aware-UTC (see #22-25). |
| 22 | **`orphan_reaper.py:111`** | `(datetime.now() - agent.last_activity).total_seconds() < 30` | **VIOLATION.** `agent.last_activity` is populated by `datetime.utcnow()` everywhere it's written (`src/core/database.py:164` column default; `monitor.py:510, 2677`). Comparing a `datetime.now()` (naive local time) against a `datetime.utcnow()`-stamped column means this 30-second grace check is silently wrong by however many hours this process's local TZ offset is from UTC — on a positive-UTC-offset host it's permanently satisfied (agent looks "just active" even when stale), on a negative-offset host it's permanently *unsatisfied* (grace window never actually applies). This is the exact bug pattern documented in CLAUDE.md's critical-invariants section, present in the monitoring code the incident narrative singles out. |
| 23 | **`orphan_reaper.py:125`** | `agent.terminated_at = datetime.now()` | **VIOLATION.** Every other `terminated_at` write in the codebase uses `datetime.utcnow()` (e.g. `monitor.py:1810`). This makes `terminated_at` a mixed-timezone column: rows terminated via this path store local time, rows terminated elsewhere store UTC — any future query ordering or diffing across `terminated_at` values (e.g. "how long between last activity and termination") is silently wrong for rows from this path. |
| 24 | **`orphan_reaper.py:147`** | `current_time = datetime.now()`, later compared only against `self.last_check_time` (also `datetime.now()`, in-memory, never persisted) | Self-consistent in isolation (both sides `now()`), so not currently miscomparing — but it's the same file, same function, mixing conventions with #22/#23 just a few lines away, and it's exactly the kind of "looks fine today, breaks the next time someone touches it" landmine the CLAUDE.md invariant exists to prevent. |
| 25 | `signals.py:53` | `MonitorSignal.timestamp` default: `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` (tz-*aware* UTC, not the naive-UTC `datetime.utcnow()` convention used everywhere else) | Not currently compared against anything (no code reads `MonitorSignal.timestamp` for staleness — `SignalQueue` has no age-based eviction, only a max-count eviction, `signals.py:83-85`).
