If someone later adds an age check here against a naive `datetime.utcnow()` value elsewhere, Python raises `TypeError: can't compare offset-naive and offset-aware datetimes` — a loud failure, not a silent one, but still a third, incompatible timestamp convention in this same subsystem. |

**Verdict**: `orphan_reaper.py` lines 111 and 125 are genuine, reproducible violations of the project's core timestamp invariant, in the exact subsystem (staleness/timeout detection) the CLAUDE.md incident narrative calls out. Confirmed via `git show 1998a11` that this is pre-existing code carried over verbatim during the SOLID-review extraction from `monitor.py` — not introduced by that refactor — and confirmed via `git show 27e8e88` (2026-08-13, the most recent commit to this file) that a careful, test-covered audit of this exact function ("reset dangling tasks at every other agent-termination site missing it") touched line 125's neighborhood without correcting the `datetime.now()` call it added right next to.

### 7. Known fragile spots / code smells

- **`TrajectoryContext` is dead code on Guardian's actual path.** `Guardian._build_accumulated_context` (`guardian.py:291-361`) reimplements a much simpler version of what `TrajectoryContext.build_accumulated_context` (`trajectory_context.py:37-133`) already does — full `AgentLog` scan, goal/constraint extraction — but Guardian never imports or calls `TrajectoryContext`. `MonitoringLoop` instantiates a `TrajectoryContext` (`monitor.py:211`) but a search shows it's never called anywhere in `monitor.py` either (no `self.trajectory_context.` call sites found). The entire 598-line regex-extraction engine in `trajectory_context.py` — goal evolution tracking, reference resolution ("this/that"), constraint-violation checking — appears unreachable from the live monitoring path.
- **Four DB tables written by exactly one caller each, and that caller is `MonitoringLoop`, not the subsystem the table is named after.** `GuardianAnalysis` and `ConductorAnalysis`/`DetectedDuplicate` are written only by `monitor.py:2694` and `monitor.py:2749/2763` respectively — never by `Guardian`/`Conductor` themselves, despite living conceptually "inside" those classes. `SteeringIntervention` is **never written anywhere in `src/`** (grep confirms only reads/deletes in `src/mcp/api.py` and `src/mcp/server.py:3574`) — it's a fully dead table that API endpoints query and always get empty results from. A refactor should either wire Guardian to actually populate `SteeringIntervention` on every `steer_agent` call, or delete the table and the dashboard endpoints reading it.
- **`Conductor.analyze_system_state`'s LLM call has no timeout** (`conductor.py:149-152`), unlike Guardian's explicit `asyncio.wait_for(timeout=90)` (`guardian.py:194-212`). Since Conductor runs synchronously inside `_monitoring_cycle` after all per-agent Guardian tasks have already completed, a hung Conductor call stalls the *entire* monitoring loop for *all* agents system-wide, not just delays one agent's analysis.
- **`Conductor.execute_decisions`'s `ESCALATE` decision is a no-op beyond a log line** (`conductor.py:332-337`, comment literally says "Could trigger alerts... " with no implementation) — low system coherence currently produces nothing a human would see outside the logs.
- **`_mechanical_recovery_for_agent` is ~460 lines** (`monitor.py:256-715`) doing at least 6 distinct concerns in one function body: pane-capture fallback logic, spend/session-limit detection, frozen-signature tracking, keystroke recovery, and model-fallback dispatch — all inside one `try` block with a single bare `except Exception` swallowing everything.
- **`_monitoring_cycle` is ~290 lines** (`monitor.py:1984-2274`) and interleaves genuinely different concerns — agent-level mechanical recovery, Guardian/Conductor dispatch, orphan cleanup, workflow-tracking auto-discovery/switch bookkeeping (`monitor.py:2147-2202`, itself opening/closing a raw DB session inline rather than using `session_scope()`), and diagnostic-check triggering — in one method with heavy inline `logger.info("[DIAGNOSTIC...")`/emoji debug logging that reads like it was left in from active debugging rather than intentional structured logging.
- **`_audit_system_health`'s stuck-task sweep is ~160 lines** of nested conditionals (`monitor.py:2900-3061`) mixing nudge-count bookkeeping, gated-phase-specific promotion logic (`monitor.py:2993-3037`, which reaches into `src.autopilot.spec` and constructs a fresh `PhaseManager` inline), and cost-collection side effects (`monitor.py:3053-3057`) — a lot of cross-subsystem knowledge concentrated in what's nominally a "health audit."
- **`_get_agent_task` (guardian.py) vs. inline task-fetching in `monitor.py`**: Guardian has a documented, deliberate pattern (`guardian.py:631-636` docstring: "Returns a dict... to avoid DetachedInstanceError across await boundaries") for detaching ORM objects into plain dicts before crossing an `await`. `monitor.py` does not consistently follow this — e.g. `_guardian_analysis_for_agent` passes the live `agent` object itself across multiple `await` points using the still-open `session` from `db_manager.get_session()` at `monitor.py:2289`, relying on `expire_on_commit=False` rather than the same defensive dict-conversion pattern Guardian uses for the same class of object.
- **Guardian's LLM prompt formatting does not visibly go through `PromptLoader.format_guardian_prompt`** (`prompt_loader.py:39-166`) — `Guardian.analyze_agent_with_trajectory` calls `self.llm_provider.analyze_agent_trajectory(...)` directly with structured kwargs (`guardian.py:197-210`), not a pre-formatted prompt string. `PromptLoader` appears to be a parallel, differently-designed prompt-formatting path used for validator prompts, not the one actually driving Guardian/Conductor's LLM calls.
- **Mixed timestamp conventions across the same subsystem**: naive UTC (`datetime.utcnow()`, the dominant convention), naive local (`datetime.now()`, `orphan_reaper.py` — a bug), tz-aware UTC (`datetime.now(timezone.utc)`, `signals.py`), and monotonic epoch float (`time.time()`, `monitor.py`'s in-memory recovery-state dicts) all coexist in files that get read/modified together. See §6.

### 8. Refactor observations

1. **Collapse the four "dedicated analysis table" writers into the classes they're named after.** `GuardianAnalysis`/`ConductorAnalysis`/`DetectedDuplicate` persistence currently lives in `MonitoringLoop` rather than `Guardian`/`Conductor` themselves — either have `Guardian.analyze_agent_with_trajectory` persist its own row, or make the split explicit.
2. **Delete or wire up `SteeringIntervention`.** It's a fully-defined table with API read/delete endpoints and zero writers. Either `Guardian.steer_agent` should insert a row on every steering attempt, or the table and its API surface should be removed as dead weight.
3. **Resolve whether `TrajectoryContext` is intentionally dead or an incomplete migration.** If Guardian's own `_build_accumulated_context` is the intended replacement, delete `trajectory_context.py` (598 lines) and its instantiation in `MonitoringLoop.__init__`. If it was meant to replace Guardian's simpler version, wire it in — but don't leave both.
4. **Extract a single `is_stale(timestamp, cutoff)` / `staleness_seconds(timestamp)` helper used everywhere**, so the next staleness check is not another hand-rolled `datetime.utcnow() - x` (or, as happened in `orphan_reaper.py`, a hand-rolled `datetime.now() - x`). A single helper (ideally one that only accepts already-UTC-aware or explicitly-UTC-naive inputs, and raises rather than silently comparing mismatched conventions) would have made the `orphan_reaper.py` bug impossible to introduce and trivial to grep-audit going forward.
5. **Fix `orphan_reaper.py:111,125,147` to `datetime.utcnow()`** — a two-line, well-isolated, low-risk fix given the file's small size (243 lines) and existing test coverage.
6. **Give `Conductor.analyze_system_state`'s LLM call the same `asyncio.wait_for` timeout treatment Guardian's already has** — currently the one subsystem-wide call with no timeout can stall the entire monitoring loop.
7. **Break up `_mechanical_recovery_for_agent` (460 lines) and `_monitoring_cycle` (~290 lines) into named sub-checks/sub-phases** — both already have clear internal seams that would make natural method boundaries without changing behavior.
## Task & Dispatch Services

This section covers seven files under `src/services/` that together own a Task row's journey from creation through dispatch, blocking, and completion: `agent_dispatch_service.py`, `agent_communication.py`, `queue_service.py`, `task_blocking_service.py`, `task_completion_service.py`, `task_enrichment_service.py`, `task_similarity_service.py`. None of these files were designed as a cohesive layer — five of the seven are explicit line-for-line extractions from a single 450+ line route handler in `src/mcp/server.py` (per each file's module docstring), and the extraction preserved the original procedural sequencing rather than introducing new abstractions. A second, largely independent dispatch pathway lives in `src/autopilot/orchestrator.py` (`create_agent_for_task_direct`, `_create_phase_task`, the task-creation-claim primitives) — it duplicates rather than reuses parts of what these services do.

### 1. Purpose & responsibilities

| File | Owns | Does NOT own |
|---|---|---|
| `task_enrichment_service.py` (131 lines) | Resolving a task's `phase_id` (UUID vs. order-number vs. "current phase"); running the RAG+LLM enrichment pipeline that turns a raw description into `enriched_description`/`completion_criteria`/`agent_prompt` | Dispatch, persistence of the enriched task (caller does that) |
| `agent_dispatch_service.py` (197 lines) | Gathering everything `AgentManager.create_agent_for_task` needs (project/phase context, RAG memories, working directory, phase CLI config); invoking agent creation; writing `Task.assigned_agent_id`/`status`/`started_at` after a successful dispatch | Queueing decisions, concurrency limits, duplicate-dispatch guards (live in `queue_service.py` and `orchestrator.py` instead) |
| `queue_service.py` (679 lines) | FIFO/priority queue admission (`should_queue_task`, `enqueue_task`/`dequeue_task`), queue-position math, per-(cli_tool,cli_model) concurrency reservation, resolving what CLI/model a task would dispatch on | Actually creating the agent (delegates to `AgentDispatchService`/`AgentManager`) |
| `task_blocking_service.py` (330 lines) | Ticket-dependency blocking: is a task's ticket blocked by another unresolved ticket; flipping `Task.status` between `blocked`/`queued` | Queue position recalculation after unblocking (borrows `QueueService` for that) |
| `task_similarity_service.py` (384 lines) | Embedding-based duplicate/related-task detection at task-creation time; storing the relationship fields directly on `Task` | Nothing writes to the `DetectedDuplicate` table (see §5 — that table belongs to an unrelated subsystem) |
| `task_completion_service.py` (1125 lines, by far the largest) | Every side effect of an agent reporting task status: memory persistence, output-artifact/schema/open-ticket hard-floor gates, forensics→ticket auto-creation, spec-gate firing (goto/continue/arbitrate), validator spawning, git commit + ticket linking, cost collection | Task creation, dispatch, queueing |
| `agent_communication.py` (299 lines) | Parent→child agent bookkeeping: enumerate an agent's children (agents whose current task was created by the parent), read/send tmux messages, nudge stuck children, create a child task | Nothing about phase/dispatch mechanics — largely orthogonal to the other six |

**Call graph between these 7 files** — almost none exists; they are siblings coordinated by an external caller, not a layered stack:

- `queue_service.py:288` (`enqueue_task`) → `TaskBlockingService.check_task_blocked` (`task_blocking_service.py:16`)
- `queue_service.py:457` (`get_next_queued_task`) → `TaskBlockingService.check_task_blocked` (`task_blocking_service.py:16`)
- `task_blocking_service.py:181-184` (`unblock_task`) → constructs a fresh `QueueService(db_manager)` and calls `._recalculate_queue_positions()` (`queue_service.py:537`)

Both edges are deferred (function-local) imports — a circular-import avoidance pattern, not a design choice (see §8). Every other cross-service interaction is orchestrated externally, from `src/mcp/server.py`'s route handlers (`create_task`, `process_queue`, `update_task_status`, `bump_task_priority_endpoint`, `restart_task_endpoint`) which call `TaskEnrichmentService` → `TaskSimilarityService` → `QueueService` → `AgentDispatchService` → (later) `TaskCompletionService` in sequence, or from `src/autopilot/orchestrator.py`'s own dispatch path (`create_agent_for_task_direct`), which reimplements several of these decisions independently rather than calling into the services.

### 2. Task lifecycle

Narrated as the sequence `src/mcp/server.py`'s `create_task` handler actually runs:

1. **Phase resolution** — `TaskEnrichmentService.resolve_phase_id` (`task_enrichment_service.py:21-53`). Accepts a UUID, a digit-string phase order, or nothing (falls through to `phase_manager.get_phase_for_task` for "current phase").
2. **Phase context string** — `TaskEnrichmentService.get_phase_context_str` (`task_enrichment_service.py:55-67`).
3. **Enrichment** — `TaskEnrichmentService.enrich` (`task_enrichment_service.py:79-131`). Fetches RAG memories, project context, calls `llm_provider.enrich_task`, and normalizes `enriched_description` to always be a string (`_normalize_enriched_description`, `task_enrichment_service.py:69-77`).
4. **Row insert** — `server.py` builds the `Task` row and commits it directly (not through a service), including inheriting `validation_enabled` from the phase's `validation.enabled` YAML key.
5. **Duplicate check** — `TaskSimilarityService.check_for_duplicates` (`task_similarity_service.py:35-205`), gated on `config.task_dedup_enabled`. If `is_duplicate`, the handler sets `task.status = "duplicated"`, `duplicate_of_task_id`, `similarity_score` directly and returns — no agent is ever dispatched for a duplicate. Otherwise `TaskSimilarityService.store_task_embedding` (`task_similarity_service.py:207-249`) persists the embedding and `related_task_ids`.
6. **Queue admission** — `QueueService.should_queue_task`/`enqueue_task` (`queue_service.py:252-270`, `272-331`). If at `max_concurrent_agents` (optionally scoped per-project), the task is parked with `status="queued"` and a computed `queue_position` (unless its ticket is blocked, in which case `enqueue_task` redirects it to `status="blocked"`, delegating to `TaskBlockingService.check_task_blocked`).
7. **Dispatch context** — `AgentDispatchService.build_dispatch_context_from_existing`/`build_dispatch_context` (`agent_dispatch_service.py:47-85`, `123-144`). Reuses the enrichment step's RAG memories/project context rather than re-fetching.
8. **Agent creation** — `AgentDispatchService.dispatch` (`agent_dispatch_service.py:146-163`). Delegates to `server_state.agent_manager.create_agent_for_task`.
9. **Assignment bookkeeping** — `AgentDispatchService.mark_assigned` (`agent_dispatch_service.py:165-197`). Sets `Task.assigned_agent_id`/`status`/`started_at`; supports an already-open `session` to avoid a double-session issue.
10. **Agent works, eventually calls `update_task_status`/`complete_my_task`** (client-side in `mcp/mcp_client.py`, outside this file set — see §7).
11. **Completion side effects, all `TaskCompletionService`, all invoked from `server.py`'s `update_task_status` handler in this order**: `record_learnings` (`task_completion_service.py:23-63`) → `verify_output_artifact` (`65-299`) → `verify_gate_result_schema` (`301-357`) → `verify_no_open_tickets` (`359-435`) → `create_tickets_from_forensics_report` (`499-567`) → (if validation enabled) `spawn_validation` (`815-908`) → `collect_cost_on_completion` (`1109-1125`) → `commit_and_link_ticket` (`1024-1107`) → `verify_output_survived_commit` (`910-1022`) → `fire_spec_gate_if_ready` (`569-813`). Any of the three `verify_*` hard floors can flip the task to `status="failed"` and short-circuit the rest of this chain — the ordering is not incidental: `verify_output_artifact` runs *before* the commit so a missing file never gets committed as "done," and `verify_output_survived_commit` runs *after* the commit specifically to catch a last-write-landed-elsewhere gap the pre-commit check can't see (its own docstring cites a live incident where a full `security.md` completed as "done" with zero trace in git history).
12. **Phase advancement** — `fire_spec_gate_if_ready` (§3 below) either creates the next phase's task directly (synchronous path) or leaves it to the background sweep in `orchestrator.py`.

Archival/blocking are side lanes off this main line: `TaskBlockingService.sync_task_blocking_status` (`task_blocking_service.py:263-330`) periodically reconciles `Task.status` against `Ticket.blocked_by_ticket_ids`, independent of the create→dispatch→complete flow.

### 3. The task-creation-claim pattern

**Mechanism.** `PhaseExecution.task_creation_claimed_at` (`src/core/database.py:561`, nullable `DateTime`) is a database-enforced mutex for "who gets to create this phase's next task." The primitive lives in `src/autopilot/orchestrator.py`, but it's the mechanism `task_completion_service.py` depends on directly:

- **Claim**: `_claim_phase_task_creation(db, phase_id)` (`phase_transitions.py:758-790`) does a single `UPDATE phase_executions SET task_creation_claimed_at = :now WHERE phase_id = :phase_id AND task_creation_claimed_at IS NULL`, then checks the row count. SQLite serializes writes to the same row, so exactly one of any number of concurrent callers can win — closing the race *by construction*, not by a check-then-sleep-then-recheck pattern (a plain `Task.count() == 0` check let two independent callers both observe zero tasks and both create one).
- **Release**: `_release_phase_task_creation_claim(db, phase_id)` (`phase_transitions.py:791-852`) sets `task_creation_claimed_at = None` once the guarded task actually exists, and flips `PhaseExecution.status` from `pending`/`completed` to `in_progress` if needed.
- **Staleness fallback**: `CLAIM_STALE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 480` (8 minutes). `_release_stale_task_creation_claims` (`phase_transitions.py:599-662`) sweeps every `PhaseExecution` per workflow whose claim is older than the cutoff and repairs it. Individual call sites (`_create_phase_task` at `phase_transitions.py:2538-2560`, `_trigger_arbitration` at `phase_transitions.py:1958`) also inline the identical stale-cutoff-and-clear logic rather than calling a shared helper (see §8).

**Fixes closed against this primitive, in order:**

- **75e1e52** ("anchor phase task-creation-claim release to the task's own `created_at`"): `_release_phase_task_creation_claim` originally stamped `execution.started_at = datetime.utcnow()` at release time. Because release always runs *after* the guarded task was already committed (and `create_task` can spend real seconds on enrichment/embedding/dedup/queue checks before returning), "now" was reliably later than the task's own `created_at`. `_case_in_progress_complete` filters `Task.created_at >= execution.started_at` to find a phase's in-cycle tasks, so the too-late `started_at` excluded the very task the claim was guarding, made the phase look task-less, and triggered a duplicate self-heal task ~15s later. **Current fix**: `started_at` is anchored to `earliest_task.created_at`, never `utcnow()`.
- **45dd5a2** ("close the target-phase task-creation race for goto/arbitration dispatch") and **85fc3c5** (same fix applied to `_create_corrective_task`): `_create_phase_task`'s existing-task check couldn't protect a *target* phase (a goto/arbitration destination different from the source phase the caller had already claimed) — `_fire_phase_transition`/`_resolve_arbitration_outcome` claimed only their own source phase. **Current fix**: `_create_phase_task` gained a `target_already_claimed: bool = False` parameter (`phase_transitions.py:2507`); when `False` (the default), the function claims `phase_id` itself before proceeding, with the same 8-minute staleness fallback inlined. `_create_corrective_task` got the identical treatment.
- **fe9a141** ("close spec-gate's own claim gap ... in task_completion_service"): `fire_spec_gate_if_ready` (`task_completion_service.py:569-813`) is a *third*, independent code path that decides phase completion — fired synchronously from `update_task_status` rather than the periodic sweep — and originally took no claim at all before running `build_phase_output` (can run pytest for minutes) and `mark_phase_complete`'s own `evaluate()` (an LLM call). **Current fix** (`task_completion_service.py:622-627`): claims via `_claim_phase_task_creation(session, phase.id)` before the slow work, releases in a `finally` — but deliberately *not* via `_release_phase_task_creation_claim`, since that also flips `PhaseExecution.status`, which would corrupt whatever state `mark_phase_complete` already left the execution in. Instead it does a raw `UPDATE ... SET task_creation_claimed_at = None`. The same commit's second fix closes a "matching exclusion" gap — `fire_spec_gate_if_ready`'s own incomplete-task count had its own independent copy of the diagnostic-task exclusion the sweep applies, which had drifted.
- **54d1814** ("sibling-task dedup query treated NULL `phase_id` as a match") — a distinct dedup mechanism in `_retry_failed_tasks` (`phase_transitions.py:164-238`): its two sibling-lookup queries filtered `Task.phase_id == phase_id`, which SQLAlchemy compiles to `IS NULL` when `phase_id` is `None`. A phase-less task got matched against *every other phase-less task in the database*, regardless of workflow or how many weeks apart they were created. Observed live: task `5b29d427` was marked `duplicated` of task `a7430ccc`, an unrelated task from five weeks earlier. **Current fix**: both queries are wrapped in `if phase_id:` and default to no sibling when `phase_id` is falsy.
- **01558db** ("don't fail a retried task superseded by an already-active sibling"): `create_agent_for_task_direct` returns `None` for two different reasons — a genuine creation failure, or its own deliberate duplicate-dispatch guard declining to dispatch because another active task already owns the phase. `_retry_failed_tasks` previously treated both identically, burning its retry budget for a decision that was actually correct. Fixed by having the sibling check short-circuit before the `create_agent_for_task_direct` call.

**Net effect**: the claim mutex now has real coverage across all identified callers — but it is a hand-claimed/hand-released convention re-derived at each call site rather than a context-managed primitive (see §9).

### 4. Task blocking logic (`task_blocking_service.py`)

Blocking is purely **ticket-driven**, not task-dependency-driven (the `Task.depends_on`/`parallel_group` columns on the model exist but are not read anywhere in this file).

- **`check_task_blocked(task_id)`** (`task_blocking_service.py:16-91`): loads the task's `Ticket` (via `Task.ticket_id`), returns `is_blocked=False` immediately if the task has no ticket or the ticket has no `blocked_by_ticket_ids`.
- **`block_task(task_id, reason)`** (`93-130`): sets `Task.status = "blocked"`, stashes the reason in `Task.completion_notes` as `"Blocked: {reason}"` — there is no dedicated blocking-reason column, `completion_notes` is overloaded for it.
- **`unblock_task(task_id)`** (`132-195`): only proceeds if `Task.status == "blocked"`; sets `status = "queued"` (not `"pending"` — the function's own comment documents this as a prior bug fix: tasks left at `pending` were never picked up by `process_queue()`). Then, outside the `get_db()` context, constructs a fresh `QueueService(db_manager)` and calls `_recalculate_queue_positions()` — wrapped in a broad `try/except` that only logs a warning on failure, so a transient failure here silently leaves queue positions stale.
- **`sync_task_blocking_status()`** (`263-330`): the reconciliation sweep — queries every non-terminal ticketed task and calls `block_task`/`unblock_task` as needed. Not wired to any scheduler within these 7 files; its caller is external (`src/mcp/api.py:2013-2016`, an on-demand endpoint, not a periodic job — drift between ticket state and task blocking persists until someone hits that endpoint).
- Both `queue_service.py:enqueue_task` and `queue_service.py:get_next_queued_task` independently call `TaskBlockingService.check_task_blocked` as a defense-in-depth double-check.

### 5. Duplicate/similarity detection (`task_similarity_service.py`)

**Algorithm**: cosine similarity over embedding vectors, computed pairwise in a Python loop (`task_similarity_service.py:123-128`).

- **Scope**: `check_for_duplicates(task_description, task_embedding, phase_id)` (`35-205`) only compares against tasks in the *same* `phase_id` — cross-phase similarity is never flagged as duplicate. If `phase_id` is `None`, it compares only against other phase-less tasks — but via a **broken filter**: `query.filter(Task.phase_id is None)` at `task_similarity_service.py:71` uses Python's `is None` instead of SQLAlchemy's `.is_(None)`. `Task.phase_id` is a class-level `InstrumentedAttribute`, never actually `None`, so `Task.phase_id is None` evaluates to the Python literal `False` at query-construction time — `.filter(False)` compiles to `WHERE 0`, meaning phase-less duplicate detection here **matches nothing, ever** (a hard bug).
- **Thresholds** (`src/core/simple_config.py:159-161`): `task_similarity_threshold = 0.7` (above → `is_duplicate`), `task_related_threshold = 0.4` (between `0.4` and `0.7` → listed as `related_tasks`, capped to top 10). Excludes tasks with `status in ("failed", "duplicated")` from comparison.
- **Persistence**: `store_task_embedding` (`207-249`) writes directly onto the `Task` row — `Task.embedding`, `Task.related_task_ids`, and, if duplicate, `Task.duplicate_of_task_id`/`Task.similarity_score`. **`DetectedDuplicate`** (`src/core/database.py:809-825`) is never written by this service — it belongs to an entirely unrelated subsystem (the "Conductor" agent-to-agent live-work-duplication detector, keyed by `agent1_id`/`agent2_id`/`conductor_analysis_id`). A naming collision worth calling out explicitly for the refactor.
- `find_similar_tasks(query, limit, threshold)` (`319-384`) is a separate, general-purpose semantic search used for ad hoc lookup, not the create-time gate.

### 6. Dispatch & duplicate-dispatch guard

**Important scoping note**: the guard the `f797a94` commit subject refers to ("guard against duplicate agent dispatch") is **not** in `agent_dispatch_service.py`. The actual guard lives in `create_agent_for_task_direct` (`engine_client.py:293-383`), the orchestrator's own in-process dispatch path (used for phase-transition dispatch, bypassing HTTP) — a **second, parallel dispatch pipeline** from the one `AgentDispatchService` implements for `server.py`'s route handlers. Mechanism: before creating an agent, it queries for another `Task` sharing `phase_id`, `status IN (pending, assigned, in_progress, queued)`, and `created_by_agent_id IN (NULL, orchestrator's own id)` — the last clause deliberately excludes tasks a phase agent creates itself for legitimate subtasks within its own phase, so this guard only catches the orchestrator racing against itself. If a sibling is found, dispatch is skipped and `None` returned.

`AgentDispatchService.dispatch` (`agent_dispatch_service.py:146-163`) itself has **no equivalent guard** — it unconditionally calls `agent_manager.create_agent_for_task`. Whatever duplicate protection exists for the `server.py` route-handler dispatch path comes from `QueueService`'s admission control, not from a sibling-task check analogous to `create_agent_for_task_direct`'s.

Related, **0fe3573** ("abort agent launch when its task was cancelled, not just the agent") closes a gap in the mid-CLI-init abort check (in `AgentManager`) that only inspected the `Agent` row's `status`. A task can be cancelled without that specific agent individually terminating — each fallback-model retry gets a fresh `agent_id` the check had never seen terminated. The fix re-fetches the task fresh at the same checkpoint and aborts if its status is terminal or its `assigned_agent_id` points at a different agent than the one currently launching.

### 7. Retry/POST reliability (e7dd24b)

This mechanism lives entirely **outside** the 7 in-scope files — the CLI-agent-side counterpart to the server-side completion handling in §2/§11:

- **`mcp/mcp_client.py`**: `_post_task_status` (`mcp/mcp_client.py:375-483`) is the shared HTTP call behind both `update_task_status` and `complete_my_task` MCP tools. Retries up to 5 times with exponential backoff (2/4/8/16/20s, ~50s worst case) on connectivity failures and 5xx responses; a 4xx is returned immediately since retrying can't fix it.
- **`src/monitoring/monitor.py`**: `_detect_unconfirmed_task_completion` (`monitor.py:1312`) checks whether an agent's transcript shows a completion call for a terminal status while the task is still non-terminal server-side — the case the retry logic can't fully close (the call rendered as sent in the transcript but never reached the backend, e.g. due to a server restart mid-turn). Nudges the agent to retry, then escalates to a full restart after repeated failures.

None of the 7 in-scope files implement retry logic of their own for the task-status write path — they assume `update_task_status` was called with a durable, already-confirmed status by the time `TaskCompletionService`'s methods run.

### 8. Known fragile spots / code smells

- **`task_similarity_service.py:71`**: `query.filter(Task.phase_id is None)` — Python `is None` instead of SQLAlchemy `.is_(None)`. Always compiles to a hard-`False` filter; phase-less duplicate detection silently matches zero tasks, ever.
- **`queue_service.py:374,380,390`**: `_calculate_queue_position`'s SQLAlchemy `and_()`/`or_()` expression mixes `not new_task.priority_boosted` (a real Python bool — fine) with `not Task.priority_boosted` (the *class-level* `InstrumentedAttribute` — always truthy as a Python object, so `not Task.priority_boosted` is always the literal `False`, evaluated once at query-build time). The "both tasks non-boosted" branches of the priority/queued_at tie-break are dead. Whether queue ordering is still correct in practice needs a regression test to confirm.
- **Circular-import workaround as an architecture**: `queue_service.py:286` and `task_blocking_service.py:180-181` import each other via function-local imports specifically to avoid a module-load-time circular import — a real bidirectional dependency the file split hides rather than resolves.
- **The task-creation-claim pattern is reimplemented per-callsite, not centralized** (§3): the staleness fallback is inlined independently at `phase_transitions.py:2538-2560`, a second site at `phase_transitions.py:2893-2905`, plus the periodic sweep version at `phase_transitions.py:599-662`. Three copies of the same eight-line pattern, each capable of drifting (as `fe9a141` shows one *did*).
- **Two independent dispatch pipelines with two independent duplicate-guards**: `AgentDispatchService.dispatch` (route-handler path, no sibling-task guard) vs. `orchestrator.py:create_agent_for_task_direct` (background/phase-transition path, has a sibling-task guard).
- **`task_completion_service.py` is a 1125-line, 11-static-method god-object for "everything that happens on task completion."** `fire_spec_gate_if_ready` alone is 245 lines and inlines claim-taking, `build_phase_output` execution, `mark_phase_complete` invocation, and three-way branching over `goto`/`continue`/`arbitrate` outcomes — the "service" layer reaches back into the orchestrator module for core behavior rather than the orchestrator calling into services.
- **`AutopilotProject.is_active` (CLAUDE.md invariant check)**: none of the 7 in-scope files read or write `AutopilotProject.is_active` directly — `queue_service.py`'s concurrency scoping filters by `Workflow.project_id` directly, sidestepping the invariant's failure modes entirely rather than needing to respect it. No violation found in this file set.
- **`Memory`/`AgentResult`/`WorkflowResult` are barely touched by this layer** — only `task_completion_service.py:23-63` (`record_learnings`) writes `Memory` rows; nothing in these 7 files reads or writes `AgentResult`/`WorkflowResult` at all, despite both being modeled as "results of task/workflow completion."

### 9. Refactor observations

1. **Extract the task-creation-claim pattern into a single reusable primitive** (e.g. a context manager: `with claim_phase_task_creation(db, phase_id) as claimed: if not claimed: return`), replacing the ≥4 independently-maintained copies of claim/staleness/release logic spread across `orchestrator.py` and `task_completion_service.py`. This is the single highest-value change given the fix history — nearly every commit in the provided list is a variant of "a caller forgot to take/anchor/release the claim correctly."
2. **Unify the two dispatch pipelines.** Either route background dispatch through `AgentDispatchService` too, or give `AgentDispatchService.dispatch` the same sibling-task guard `create_agent_for_task_direct` has.
3. **Break the `queue_service.py` ↔ `task_blocking_service.py` circular dependency** by extracting a shared "recompute queue position" operation into a module both can import from without a cycle.
4. **Split `task_completion_service.py`.** At 1125 lines and 11 static methods, this is not one cohesive responsibility. `fire_spec_gate_if_ready` in particular is really orchestration logic wearing a "completion service" label — it belongs closer to (or merged with) `orchestrator.py`'s own phase-transition code.
5. **Fix the two concrete bugs found during this review** (`task_similarity_service.py:71`'s `is None` vs `.is_(None)`, and `queue_service.py:374/380/390`'s `not Task.priority_boosted` on the class attribute).
6. **Decide what `DetectedDuplicate` is for, or delete the ambiguity.** Its name reads as "the table `TaskSimilarityService` populates," but it's entirely unrelated (Conductor's live-agent-collision detector).
## Result, Ticket & Validation Services

### 1. Purpose & responsibilities

This part of the services layer splits into three unrelated subsystems that happen to sit in the same directory:

| File | Lines | Owns |
|---|---|---|
| `src/services/result_service.py` | 208 | CRUD for `AgentResult` — task-level formal result submissions |
| `src/services/validation_helpers.py` | 103 | File-level/security validation (path traversal, size, extension, task ownership) used at submission time |
| `src/services/workflow_result_service.py` | 299 | CRUD for `WorkflowResult` — workflow-level result submissions (CTF/puzzle-style "found the flag" results) |
| `src/services/result_validation_helpers.py` | 298 | Content-level validation (keyword/criteria matching, markdown structure, evidence detection) against a `WorkflowResult`'s content |
| `src/services/result_validator_service.py` | 164 | Orchestrates whether a validator agent should be spawned for a `WorkflowResult`, and processes the pass/fail outcome |
| `src/services/ticket_service.py` | 1772 | Ticket CRUD, status-column state machine, blocking/dependency graph, human-approval workflow |
| `src/services/ticket_history_service.py` | 310 | Append-only audit trail (`TicketHistory`) + human-readable timeline formatting |
| `src/services/ticket_search_service.py` | 623 | Hybrid (semantic + FTS5 keyword) ticket search, embedding indexing, duplicate/related-ticket detection |
| `src/services/cost_collection_service.py` | 1029 | Per-CLI-tool (pi/Claude Code/OpenCode/Codex) transcript parsing into `CostEntry` rows |
| `src/services/embedding_service.py` | 212 | Hardcoded-OpenAI embedding + cosine-similarity utility class — **effectively dead code**, see §6 |

**Call graph, file:line:**

- `ticket_service.py:21-22` imports `TicketHistoryService` and `TicketSearchService`; every mutating `TicketService` method (`create_ticket` L366, `update_ticket` L731/756/772, `change_status` L911, `add_comment` L997/1024, `assign_ticket` L1302, `link_commit` L1461, `resolve_ticket` L1569/1581) calls into `TicketHistoryService.record_change`/`record_status_transition`/`link_commit` for the audit trail, and into `TicketSearchService.index_ticket`/`reindex_ticket`/`find_related_tickets` for embedding maintenance.
- `ticket_search_service.py:13-14` imports `src.memory.embedding_factory.create_embedding_provider` and `src.memory.store_factory.create_vector_store` — the *configurable* (fastembed/turbovec-by-default) stack, not `embedding_service.py`.
- `result_service.py:9-14` and `workflow_result_service.py:9-13` both import from `validation_helpers.py` (file-level checks).
- `result_validator_service.py:8-11` imports `ValidationResult`/`validate_result_criteria` from `result_validation_helpers.py` (content-level checks), and lazily imports `WorkflowResultService` at L102 to write the outcome.
- `src/mcp/memory_api.py` is the sole HTTP entry point for all five result/workflow-result files: `report_results` (L396-459) → `ResultService.create_result`; `give_validation_review` (L461-…) → `ResultService.get_results_for_task`/`verify_result` (L510-521); `submit_result` (L670-830) → `WorkflowResultService.submit_result` + `server_state.result_validator_service.should_spawn_validator` (L764-765); `submit_result_validation` (L833-954) → `server_state.result_validator_service.process_validation_outcome` (L875).
- `src/mcp/server.py:60,274,331` instantiates the singleton `ResultValidatorService(db_manager, phase_manager)` held on `server_state`.
- `cost_collection_service.py:collect_task_cost` (L749) is called from three sites: `src/agents/manager.py:2151`, `src/monitoring/monitor.py:3055`, and `src/services/task_completion_service.py:1123` — cost collection is triggered redundantly from agent termination, a monitoring sweep, and task completion (see §7).

### 2. Result/output verification pipeline — two independent mechanisms

This codebase has **two structurally separate "result verification" pipelines** that share vocabulary but not code.

**Pipeline A — the main autopilot task-completion path (NOT in this file set).** Every ordinary phase task goes through `TaskCompletionService.verify_output_artifact` (`src/services/task_completion_service.py:66`) and archival logic in `src/phases/phase_manager.py`. This is the "output-existence hard floor" — it checks that a phase's YAML-declared `Phase.outputs` files actually exist in the worktree before a task can be marked `done`, then archives them into the feature folder. **This is what `af99e8d` fixed** — it touched `src/agents/manager.py`, `src/core/status_derivation.py`, `src/mcp/autopilot_api.py`, and `src/phases/phase_manager.py` — never `result_service.py`, `result_validator_service.py`, or `workflow_result_service.py`. Concretely, `af99e8d` changed `verify_output_artifact`'s worktree-recovery logic to prefer the *currently completing task's own agent's worktree* over the workflow's *earliest* `AgentWorktree` row, because a workflow re-entered via review-mode `request_changes` or a manual phase rerun spawns its own disconnected isolated worktree — "earliest" was picking a stale worktree from an unrelated prior round and rejecting genuine output as "missing."

**Pipeline B — the `AgentResult`/`WorkflowResult` submission path (this file set).** This is a parallel, agent-driven "I'm formally reporting a result" mechanism, structured around two DB tables:

- `AgentResult` (`src/core/database.py:692-721`): one row per agent's markdown result for a `task_id`, with `verification_status` (`unverified`/`verified`/`disputed`) and `verified_by_validation_id` FK to `ValidationReview`.
- `WorkflowResult` (`src/core/database.py:724-750`): one row per agent's submitted "workflow result" (explanation + extra evidence files), with `status` (`pending_validation`/`validated`/`rejected`) and a boolean flag `Workflow.result_found` that gets flipped once validated (`workflow_result_service.py:219-223`).

Step-by-step narrative for Pipeline B:

1. Agent calls `POST /report_results` → `ResultService.create_result` (`result_service.py:20-96`): validates path (`validate_file_path`, `validation_helpers.py:7-36`), file existence, size (≤100KB), `.md` extension, and task ownership (requiring `task.assigned_agent_id == agent_id`). Reads file content into `AgentResult.markdown_content`, sets `verification_status="unverified"`, sets `Task.has_results = True`.
2. Separately, agent calls `POST /submit_result` (`memory_api.py:670-830`) → `WorkflowResultService.submit_result` (`workflow_result_service.py:19-141`): same file validation plus per-`extra_file` validation (10MB cap each) and a check that the workflow doesn't already have a `status="validated"` result (first-validated-wins). Creates a `WorkflowResult` row with `status="pending_validation"`. **`memory_api.py` then also creates a second, redundant `AgentResult` row** (L718-745) tagged `result_type="implementation"` — a single `/submit_result` call produces both a `WorkflowResult` and an `AgentResult`, wrapped in its own best-effort try/except that only logs a warning on failure, meaning the two tables can silently diverge.
3. `should_spawn_validator` (`result_validator_service.py:44-70`) checks `PhaseManager.get_workflow_config(workflow_id).has_result` and `.result_criteria`. If both are set, a validator agent is spawned asynchronously.
4. The validator agent eventually calls `POST /submit_result_validation` → `ResultValidatorService.process_validation_outcome` (`result_validator_service.py:72-138`): looks up the `WorkflowResult`, delegates to `WorkflowResultService.update_result_status` (`workflow_result_service.py:175-233`) to set `status="validated"/"rejected"`, `validation_feedback`, `validation_evidence`, and — if validated — sets `Workflow.result_found = True` / `Workflow.result_id`. Then, per `config.on_result_found` (`"stop_all"` vs `"do_nothing"`), either queues `terminate_workflow` (via `WorkflowTerminationHandler`) or `continue_workflow`.
5. On the separate `give_validation_review` path (task-level, not workflow-level), when a `validator` agent passes a task, if `task.has_results` it loops `ResultService.get_results_for_task` + `ResultService.verify_result` to flip every `AgentResult.verification_status` to `"verified"`.

**Architectural conclusion:** Pipeline B (`AgentResult`/`WorkflowResult`) reads as a CTF/SWEBench-style "did the agent find the flag/fix" mechanism — the terminology (`on_result_found`, `stop_all`), the keyword/evidence-pattern validator, and the workflow-termination trigger are all shaped for that use case, not the ordinary phase pipeline. It is orthogonal to and does not feed Pipeline A's output-existence/archival gate at all. A refactor should either fold these into one mechanism or rename to make the split explicit, since "result" currently means two different things depending on which file you're reading.

### 3. Validation helpers — two files, two different jobs

`validation_helpers.py` and `result_validation_helpers.py` are **not duplicates** — they validate different things at different points in the pipeline, but the near-identical filenames make this easy to miss:

- **`validation_helpers.py`** (103 lines) — pure file/security validation, used at *submission* time by both `result_service.py` and `workflow_result_service.py`: `validate_file_path` (path traversal via `".." in str(file_path)` check), `validate_file_size` (KB cap), `validate_markdown_format` (`.md` extension only — no real markdown syntax check happens), `validate_task_ownership` (DB check that `task.assigned_agent_id == agent_id`). None of these functions look at markdown *content*.

- **`result_validation_helpers.py`** (298 lines) — content/semantic validation, used only by `result_validator_service.py` at *validation* time: `validate_markdown_structure` (min length, header regex, code-block-or-evidence-keyword presence), `validate_result_criteria` (keyword extraction from a criteria string, then presence-matching those keywords in content plus regex-detecting code blocks/images/URLs/`$...$` commands as "evidence"), `validate_file_contains_solution`, `validate_evidence_sections`. This is a bag of regex heuristics, not a schema validator — `passed` in `validate_result_criteria` is `has_keywords and has_good_structure and has_evidence`, all three purely lexical/structural, with no actual verification that the claimed fix/solution works.

The naming collision (`validation_helpers` vs `result_validation_helpers`) is a real refactor hazard: an import of the wrong one won't fail type-checking and the mistake would only surface as a silent behavioral difference.

### 4. Ticket subsystem

**Domain model.** A `Ticket` (`src/core/database.py:886-932`) is a Kanban-style work item scoped to a `workflow_id`, with a configurable `status` column-set defined per-workflow by `BoardConfig` (`database.py:1038-1073`). It is **not** the same entity as `Task`: a `Ticket` optionally links to one `task_id` and to a `phase_id`, and separately tracks `related_task_ids`. Tickets support parent/child (`parent_ticket_id`) and explicit blocking dependencies (`blocked_by_ticket_ids`), with circular-blocking prevention in `TicketService._check_circular_blocking` (`ticket_service.py:135-161`, though the comment admits it's "a basic check; more complex cycles would require graph traversal" — it only checks one hop, not full-graph cycles of length >2).

**Human approval.** If `BoardConfig.ticket_human_review` is set, ticket creation blocks on `TicketApprovalManager.wait_for_approval` (`ticket_service.py:38-91`, an `asyncio.Event`-based rendezvous with a timeout, default 1800s). On timeout or rejection the ticket and all its child rows (history/comments/commits) are hard-deleted rather than marked rejected-and-kept — so a rejected ticket leaves no audit trail of having existed, other than log lines and the broadcast event.

**Status/lifecycle.** `TicketService.change_status` (`ticket_service.py:788-939`) is the state machine: validates the new status against `BoardConfig.columns`, refuses the transition if `blocked_by_ticket_ids` is non-empty (returns a soft failure dict rather than raising), sets `started_at` on first move out of the initial column, and auto-sets `is_resolved=True`/`resolved_at` when the ticket reaches the board's *last* column, clearing `is_resolved` again if moved away from the last column. `resolve_ticket` (`ticket_service.py:1477-1665`) is a separate explicit-resolution path that also cascades: finds every other ticket in the workflow whose `blocked_by_ticket_ids` contains this ticket, strips the dependency, and then unblocks any `Task` rows with `status="blocked"` tied to those tickets **only if the ticket has zero remaining blockers** (explicitly commented "BUG FIX: Only unblock task if ALL blocking tickets are resolved") via `TaskBlockingService.unblock_task`.

**History.** `TicketHistoryService` (`ticket_history_service.py`) is a thin, append-only audit-log writer: `record_change` (L15-83) writes one `TicketHistory` row per call with a synthesized human-readable `change_description` (`_generate_description`, a big if/elif chain over `change_type` strings). It accepts an optional `db` session parameter throughout so callers already inside a `TicketService` transaction can pass their session in and avoid a nested commit — but this isn't applied 100% consistently across `TicketService`'s call sites (some intentionally run outside the original transaction after `with get_db()` has already exited).

**Search.** `TicketSearchService` (`ticket_search_service.py`) implements **hybrid search**: `semantic_search` (via the configurable `create_embedding_provider()`/`create_vector_store()`) and `keyword_search` (raw SQLite FTS5 via `ticket_fts` virtual table). `hybrid_search` is the default entry point, merging both via Reciprocal Rank Fusion weighted 70% semantic / 30% keyword. Semantic search gracefully degrades to keyword-only on any exception. A defensive `_fts5_query` sanitizer tokenizes and quotes free text before it reaches FTS5's `MATCH`, added after a live bug where a ticket title containing "notice" caused FTS5 to parse it as a column filter and throw `no such column: notice`. `find_related_tickets` does duplicate/related-ticket detection by vector similarity, with hardcoded thresholds classifying `duplicate` (≥0.9), `related` (≥0.7), `similar` (≥0.5). Ticket indexing happens on creation, on title/description edit, and every 5th comment.

### 5. Cost tracking (`cost_collection_service.py`)

**Trigger points.** `collect_task_cost(task_id)` is called from three independent sites — `src/agents/manager.py:2151` (agent termination), `src/monitoring/monitor.py:3055` (monitoring sweep), and `src/services/task_completion_service.py:1123` (task completion) — meaning cost collection is attempted at least twice per task under normal operation, relying entirely on the checkpoint mechanism to avoid double-counting.

**Flow**: resolves `Task` → assigned `Agent` (falling back to `Agent.current_task_id` match) → skips entirely for subscription-billed CLIs (`_is_subscription_cli`, cached via `@lru_cache(maxsize=1)` — process-lifetime cached, so a mid-run plan change wouldn't be picked up) → for `cli_type == "pi"`, explicitly skips if any real-time `CostEntry(source="pi")` rows already exist for this task+agent → discovers the CLI's session transcript file via CLI-specific path logic → dispatches to one of four `CostCollector` subclasses (`PiJsonlCollector`, `ClaudeCodeCollector`, `OpenCodeCollector`, `CodexUsageCollector`) → each returns `(entries, new_checkpoint_line_count)` → entries are written individually via `record_cost` with per-entry commit/rollback → `SessionCostCheckpoint.lines_processed` is updated (keyed by `session_id` not `Agent.id` per the model docstring, "session outlives any single agent row — an agent retry reuses the same session file").

**Pricing.** `ClaudeCodeCollector.PRICES` is a large hardcoded dict of per-model $/M-token rates, merged (not overridden) with `config/model_pricing.json` if present (external file only fills gaps, never updates existing hardcoded entries). `CodexUsageCollector` instead requires four explicit env vars and reports `cost_status: "unavailable"` if unset — Codex transcripts carry cumulative usage, not billing rates, so this collector computes token *deltas* between consecutive events. `PiJsonlCollector` reads dollar cost straight from the transcript since pi's extension posts real dollar amounts, not raw tokens.

`CostEntry` is documented as "append-only ledger... source of truth... aggregates derived via cost_derivation.py, not hand-maintained."

### 6. Embedding service

`embedding_service.py`'s `EmbeddingService` class is a **hardcoded-OpenAI** embedding + cosine-similarity utility, used only by `src/services/task_similarity_service.py` for `check_for_duplicates`.

This is **structurally superseded and effectively dead**. `src/mcp/server.py:275` declares `self.embedding_service: Optional[EmbeddingService]` and imports the class, but the actual object assigned at runtime (`server.py:344`) is `create_embedding_provider()` from `src/memory/embedding_factory.py` — a duck-typed `EmbeddingProvider` (fastembed-by-default) that happens to expose the same method names (`generate_embedding`, `calculate_cosine_similarity`, deliberately shaped to match). The comment at `server.py:337-339` states this explicitly: *"Previously this was gated on config.openai_api_key, which silently disabled dedup for python-only (openrouter) setups... Now using configurable provider."* So `embedding_service.py`'s class is never instantiated in production — only imported for a type hint that now lies about the actual runtime type.

Meanwhile `ticket_search_service.py` independently uses the same `embedding_factory.create_embedding_provider()` / `store_factory.create_vector_store()` pair directly (own module-level cached instances, not shared with `server_state.embedding_service`) — a second, separate instantiation of the same configurable stack. There is no single shared embedding-provider singleton.

### 7. Known fragile spots / code smells

- **Dead class masquerading as live type**: `embedding_service.py`'s `EmbeddingService` is imported for typing only; the object actually wired at `server.py:344` is a different class from `embedding_factory.py`.
- **Two `validation_helpers`-named files, disjoint responsibility**: `validation_helpers.py` (file/security) vs `result_validation_helpers.py` (content/criteria). No shared base, no cross-reference between them.
- **Redundant `AgentResult` write inside `submit_result`**: `memory_api.py:718-745` creates a second `AgentResult` row as a side effect of `/submit_result`, wrapped in a bare `except Exception` that only logs a warning and rolls back silently — the two tables can diverge with no error surfaced to the caller.
- **Redundant cost-collection triggers**: `collect_task_cost` fires from three independent call sites with no single owner of "when does cost get collected."
- **Circular-blocking check is one-hop only**: `TicketService._check_circular_blocking` only detects direct A-blocks-B-blocks-A cycles, not longer chains.
- **Ticket rejection deletes all history**: rejected/timed-out ticket-approval flows hard-delete the `Ticket`, `TicketHistory`, `TicketComment`, and `TicketCommit` rows rather than marking status `rejected` and retaining them — conflicts with `TicketHistoryService`'s stated purpose as "audit trail."
- **`validate_file_path`'s traversal check is superficial**: rejects any path containing the literal substring `".."`, but never actually confirms the resolved path stays within any expected root directory (contrast with `cost_collection_service.py`'s session-file discovery, which does proper `resolve()` + `startswith(base)` containment checks).
- **`result_validation_helpers.py`'s "evidence" detection is pure regex heuristics**: treats presence of a code fence, an image markdown tag, a URL, or a `$...$` sequence as "evidence" — trivially gameable by an agent padding its result markdown with an empty code block or a stray URL.
- **Two independent embedding-provider instances for tickets vs. tasks**: doubles in-memory model load for local backends like fastembed.

### 8. Refactor observations

1. **Merge or clearly separate the two "result" pipelines.** `AgentResult`/`WorkflowResult`/`result_validator_service.py` (CTF-style, workflow-scoped) is architecturally unrelated to `TaskCompletionService.verify_output_artifact`/`phase_manager.py`'s archival gate (ordinary phase output verification, task-scoped) despite both being called "result verification."
2. **Rename `result_validation_helpers.py` vs `validation_helpers.py`** to something that encodes their actual split — e.g. `submission_security_validation.py` vs `criteria_content_validation.py`.
3. **Delete or genuinely revive `embedding_service.py`.** It's dead weight: not instantiated anywhere in production, superseded by `embedding_factory.py`'s configurable provider, and its lingering type hint actively misleads about the real runtime type.
4. **Unify embedding-provider instantiation** so `TaskSimilarityService` and `TicketSearchService` share one provider/vector-store instance via `app_context`/`server_state`.
5. **Consolidate cost-collection triggering** behind a single call site rather than relying on session-checkpoint arithmetic plus a source-specific short-circuit to prevent double-billing.
6. **Fix `validate_file_path`'s traversal check** to do real path-containment validation instead of a substring match on `".."` that a symlink or absolute path can bypass.
7. **Decide on ticket-rejection retention policy** — either keep rejected tickets for audit purposes, or explicitly document that rejection is a hard-delete and drop the pretense of an audit trail for that path.
## Validator Agents & Memory/RAG Backend

*Deep-read of `src/validation/{validator_agent,check_executors}.py` and `src/memory/{store_factory,embedding_factory,turbovec_store,vector_store,rag}.py`, closing the gaps the Result/Ticket/Validation Services section left open: the internals of `spawn_validator_agent`, and whether the configurable embedding/vector-store stack is genuinely stateless per-call or caches.*

### 1. Validator agent spawning

**Entry point.** `should_spawn_validator` (`result_validator_service.py:44-70`) only ever fires from one call site: `submit_result`'s handler in `src/mcp/memory_api.py:763-792`, immediately after `WorkflowResultService.submit_result` persists a `WorkflowResult` row and a redundant `AgentResult` row. If `should_validate and criteria`, the handler defines and schedules a closure via `asyncio.create_task(spawn_validator_async())` (`memory_api.py:770-791`) — spawning is fire-and-forget from the HTTP handler's perspective; `/submit_result` returns `validation_triggered=True` without waiting for the validator to actually exist.

**`spawn_validator_agent`** (`src/validation/validator_agent.py:17-178`) is a single function handling two structurally different `validation_type`s ("task" and "result") behind one signature. Only the "result" branch is reachable from `should_spawn_validator`'s flow described above; the "task" branch exists for a second, separate caller (see §1.4).

**1.1 — Result-validation branch** (`validator_agent.py:108-147`, the one `/submit_result` actually triggers):
- Looks up the `WorkflowResult` row (target_id) and its `Workflow`; raises `ValueError` if either is missing — an unhandled `ValueError` inside `spawn_validator_async`'s bare `except Exception` (`memory_api.py:788-789`) just logs and silently drops validation, leaving `WorkflowResult.status="pending_validation"` forever with no retry.
- Builds the prompt via `prompt_loader.format_result_validation_prompt` (`validator_agent.py:123-132`, `src/monitoring/prompt_loader.py:296`), passing `result_file_path`, `workflow_name`, `validation_criteria`, `submitted_by_agent`, `submitted_at`.
- Creates a synthetic `Task` row — `parent_task_id` is **not set** for result validation (unlike the task-validation branch), `phase_id` is **not set** either, `assigned_agent_id=validator_agent_id` (an ID minted before the `Agent` row itself exists), `validation_enabled=False`.

**1.2 — Dispatch mechanism.** Both branches converge on one call (`validator_agent.py:157-173`), calling `agent_manager.create_agent_for_task` with `agent_type="result_validator"`/`"validator"` — the **same core method** every ordinary phase agent goes through, and `manager.py:285-303`'s docstring explicitly documents these agent_type values and `commit_sha` "(for validators)" as designed-for cases, not an accident. Both values are legitimate `CHECK` constraint members (`src/core/database.py:184`).

However, it is **not** the same *dispatch path* as ordinary phase agents. The existing document's cross-cutting theme identified two independent dispatch implementations (`AgentDispatchService` for the HTTP route path, `create_agent_for_task_direct` for the orchestrator's in-process path). `spawn_validator_agent` is a **third**, bypassing both: no `resolve_cli_model_dispatch` CLI/model-concurrency check, no sibling-task dedup query. `create_agent_for_task`'s own internal duplicate-agent guard is a no-op protection here because `spawn_validator_agent` always mints a brand-new `uuid.uuid4()` task id before calling in — two concurrent `should_spawn_validator` firings cannot be caught by any existing guard, since each gets its own disjoint task row.

**1.3 — Outcome path back to `WorkflowResultService`.** The validator agent eventually calls `POST /submit_result_validation` (`memory_api.py:833-955`). That handler resolves *which* agent is the validator with:
```python
validator_agent = session.query(Agent).filter(Agent.agent_type == "result_validator")
                                       .order_by(Agent.created_at.desc()).first()
```
(`memory_api.py:854-859`) — **not scoped to `request.result_id` or the workflow at all**, just "most recently created `result_validator` agent, anywhere." If two result-validators are in flight concurrently for two different workflows, this resolves to the wrong one's `agent_id` for whichever `submit_result_validation` call lands second — the outcome would be attributed to (and would terminate) the wrong validator agent. This is a real, currently-live race given `spawn_validator_agent` has no serialization against concurrent invocations.

From there: `ResultValidatorService.process_validation_outcome` → `WorkflowResultService.update_result_status` sets `status="validated"/"rejected"` → reads `config.on_result_found` (`"stop_all"` → terminate workflow; `"do_nothing"` → continue; any exception reading config also defaults to continue) → `memory_api.py:885-903` acts accordingly. Regardless of outcome, the validator agent is always terminated and the dispatch queue re-processed via a second fire-and-forget task.

**1.4 — Task-validation branch** (`validator_agent.py:51-106`) is reachable from a caller outside the result-services file set — it validates ordinary `Task` completions, not `WorkflowResult` submissions. Two things in this branch are computed and then **thrown away**:
- `branch_manager.get_workspace_changes(agent_id=original_agent_id, since_commit=None)` — return value is not assigned to anything.
- `get_agent_results(target_id, session)` — also unassigned; the function itself assembles completion notes / enriched description / done-definition into a string, but that string never reaches `format_task_validation_prompt`'s parameter list — no diff/results parameter exists to receive it. The task validator's prompt is built with no diff summary and no claimed-results text — both are computed for no purpose. This reads as refactor debris: output plumbing was dropped somewhere along the way.

When task validation fails, feedback reaches the *original* (still-running) agent through an entirely separate, out-of-band mechanism: `give_validation_review`'s handler calls `send_feedback_to_agent` (`validator_agent.py:215-262`), which writes the feedback to a temp file and does a literal `tmux send-keys -t agent_{agent_id} "cat {file}" Enter` into the original agent's live pane — not a queued `Task`/DB row, a direct keystroke injection into a running terminal session.

### 2. Memory/vector-store factory layer

**`src/memory/embedding_factory.py`** (132 lines) and **`src/memory/store_factory.py`** (100 lines) are both genuinely **stateless factory functions** — `create_embedding_provider()` and `create_vector_store()` each construct and `return` a brand-new object on every call, with **no module-level cache, no `@lru_cache`, no singleton registry** anywhere in either file. Every call to `FastEmbedEmbeddingProvider.__init__` does a fresh `TextEmbedding(model_name=...)` load; every call to `TurboVecStore.__init__` re-reads every collection's index+metadata files from disk.

Backend selection reads two env vars per factory, both defaulting to the local-only stack:
- `EMBEDDING_BACKEND` (default `"fastembed"`) + `FASTEMBED_MODEL` (default `"BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5"`); falls back to OpenAI only on `ImportError`, not any other failure.
- `VECTOR_STORE_BACKEND` (default `"turbovec"`) + `TURBOVEC_DATA_DIR` (default `"data/turbovec"`); same `ImportError`-only fallback to Qdrant.

**Settling the "two independent instances" question the earlier section raised.** Tracing every caller:

| Caller | Instance lifetime |
|---|---|
| `server.py:344` — `self.embedding_service = create_embedding_provider()` | Called **once**, at server startup, stored on `ServerState`. |
| `TaskSimilarityService.__init__` | Receives that same `server_state.embedding_service` object as a constructor argument — **does not** call `create_embedding_provider()` itself. Its `embedding_service` parameter is typed `EmbeddingService`, the dead class — a stale type hint identical in kind to `server.py:275`'s. |
| `server.py:340` — `self.vector_store = create_vector_store()` | Called **once**, at server startup. Consumed by `RAGSystem` (§4), `TaskCompletionService.record_learnings`, and `memory_api.py`'s `/record_memory` handler. |
| `TicketSearchService._get_embedding_provider()`/`_get_vector_store()` | **Lazily memoized on first call** via class-level attributes — a second, independent singleton per process, never unified with `server_state`'s. |

So the factory functions themselves are correctly stateless; the redundancy is real but lives entirely at the call sites — there are exactly **two** long-lived embedding-provider instances alive in one process (`server_state.embedding_service` for task dedup, `TicketSearchService._embedding_provider` for ticket search), each independently loading a local fastembed model into memory, never sharing.

### 3. `turbovec_store.py` / `vector_store.py`

**`TurboVecStore`** (`turbovec_store.py:19-358`) is the local backend: a `turbovec.IdMapIndex` (quantized ANN index) per collection, backed by a parallel JSON metadata file. Seven **hardcoded** collections (`COLLECTIONS`) each fixed at `dim: 384` (matching fastembed's `bge-small-en-v1.5` default). Point IDs are strings (UUIDs) externally but the underlying index only accepts integer IDs, so `_id_to_int` SHA-256-hashes the string ID down to a 63-bit int, with explicit hash-collision detection/logging. Concurrency model: a single `asyncio.Lock` per collection guarding both `store_memory` and `search`. **Every single write flushes the full index and metadata file to disk** unconditionally — for `IdMapIndex.write()`, this is a full-index serialization per insert, not an append; on a large collection this makes ingestion O(n) per write, a real scaling ceiling the module doesn't guard against (no batching, no debounced flush despite `self._flush_interval = 30` being defined and never actually used anywhere in the file).

**`VectorStoreManager`** (`vector_store.py:20-323`) is the Qdrant-backed equivalent, structurally mirroring `TurboVecStore`'s same seven collection names and method surface (letting `store_factory.py`'s `VectorStoreProtocol` type both interchangeably). Differs in two structural ways: collections are sized `3072` (OpenAI `text-embedding-3-large` dimension), not `384`; and there is no local locking at all — every operation is a direct remote Qdrant call, with per-call `try/except Exception` swallowing failures into a log line and an empty/`False` result rather than raising.

**The 384-vs-3072 dimension split is a real cross-backend trap.** `TurboVecStore.store_memory` explicitly validates `len(embedding) != expected_dim` and raises — caught by its own `try/except` and turned into a logged error + `return False`, a silent failure not a startup-time check. `VectorStoreManager` performs no such validation at all (Qdrant itself would reject a wrong-size vector, surfacing as the same swallowed exception). Concretely: switching `VECTOR_STORE_BACKEND=turbovec → qdrant` without also switching `EMBEDDING_BACKEND=fastembed → openai` (or vice versa) silently breaks every `store_memory`/`search` call — nothing validates that the two independently-configured backends agree on vector width. In this repo's checked-in `hephaestus_config.yaml`, the settings do agree — but nothing in either factory enforces that agreement; it's a config-authoring convention, not a code-checked invariant.

### 4. `rag.py`

**`RAGSystem`** (`rag.py:13-294`) is a *separate, older* code path from the store/embedding factory stack — not a consumer of it. Its constructor is typed `vector_store: VectorStoreManager` (the Qdrant-specific class) and `llm_provider`. At the one production instantiation site (`server.py:324-327`), `self.vector_store` passed in is actually whatever `create_vector_store()` returned (`TurboVecStore` by default) — so the type hint lies about the runtime type exactly the same way `embedding_service.py`'s stale hint does; it works only because `TurboVecStore` duck-types `VectorStoreManager`'s async method surface.

**Embedding source is the real divergence, structural not cosmetic.** `RAGSystem.retrieve_for_task` generates its query vector via `self.llm_provider.generate_embedding(...)` — i.e. `ServerState.llm_provider` → `MultiProviderLLM` → `LangChainLLMClient.generate_embedding`. This is a **third**, independent embedding pathway, governed by a **different config surface** than `EMBEDDING_BACKEND`: `LangChainLLMClient._initialize_models` reads `hephaestus_config.yaml`'s `llm.embedding_provider` key, defaulting to `"openai"` if unset, and only produces fastembed-compatible 384-dim vectors when that key is explicitly `"fastembed"/"local"/"openrouter"`. If unset (or no OpenAI key present), `_embedding_model` stays `None` and `generate_embedding` returns a hardcoded `[0.0] * 1536` zero-vector fallback — which would then fail `TurboVecStore.store_memory`'s dimension check and get silently dropped. In this repo's actual config, `llm.embedding_provider: fastembed` is set, so the pathway is currently dimension-consistent — but it's a second, entirely separate knob from `EMBEDDING_BACKEND`/`FASTEMBED_MODEL` that happens to need to agree with the same `TurboVecStore` 384-dim constant, with nothing cross-checking the two. Also worth noting: `embedding_factory.py`'s `FASTEMBED_MODEL` env var has no corresponding key forwarded through `src/sdk/config.py`'s `to_env_dict()` (which forwards `EMBEDDING_MODEL`/`EMBEDDING_BACKEND` but not `FASTEMBED_MODEL`) — it must be set directly as a process env var, not via `hephaestus_config.yaml`.

**Corpus.** `retrieve_for_task` searches *all* seven collections at once, then reranks by a hand-weighted `vector_score*0.5 + recency_score*0.2 + type_relevance*0.3` formula. The corpus is **not** the `Memory` DB table's rows directly, nor `AgentLog` — it's whatever content has been embedded and pushed into the vector store via `store_memory`, which for the `agent_memories` collection happens from two write sites: `memory_api.py`'s `/record_memory` handler (agent-submitted free-text memories) and `TaskCompletionService.record_learnings` (embeds each `key_learnings` entry reported at task completion). Both writers use the *same* `server_state.llm_provider.generate_embedding` path RAG reads with — so write and read dimensions are self-consistent within this one pathway, just disjoint from the `embedding_factory.py` pathway used for dedup/ticket-search. `ticket_embeddings` is populated exclusively by `TicketSearchService` on its own separate embedding provider, and `RAGSystem.retrieve_for_task` happens to also search that collection as one of the seven — meaning task-enrichment RAG retrieval and ticket search partially overlap on the *store* side (same collection) but never share the *embedding provider* instance that wrote/reads it.

**Callers — confirmed exhaustively.** Exactly two production call sites reach `rag_system.retrieve_for_task`: `TaskEnrichmentService.enrich` and `AgentDispatchService` (for the direct, non-enriched dispatch path).

**Guardian does *not* go through `rag.py`.** `RAGSystem` is passed into `MonitoringLoop.__init__` and stored as `self.rag_system` — but that attribute is never referenced again anywhere in `monitor.py`, `guardian.py`, or `conductor.py`. This confirms the existing Monitoring section's finding: Guardian's `_build_accumulated_context` builds context purely from `AgentLog` rows and `Task`/phase metadata, with no vector search involved. `rag_system` is dead wiring on `MonitoringLoop` as far as Guardian/Conductor are concerned.

`MemoryIngestion` (`rag.py:297-389`) — chunked document ingestion into `static_docs` — has no callers anywhere in `src/` either; a second piece of dead code in this file alongside the unreferenced Guardian wiring.

### 5. Known fragile spots / code smells

- **`spawn_validator_agent` has no concurrency guard**, and the consumer that needs one (`submit_result_validation`'s "most recent `result_validator` agent" lookup) is unscoped by `result_id`/`workflow_id` — two concurrent result submissions across different workflows can cross-wire validation outcomes to the wrong workflow. This is a live correctness bug, not a hypothetical.
- **Two discarded computations in the task-validation branch**: `get_workspace_changes` and `get_agent_results` are called and their return values thrown away — the task validator's prompt never receives a diff summary or the agent's claimed results, despite dedicated code existing to produce both.
- **`check_executors.py` is entirely dead code.** `execute_validation_check`/`ValidationCheckType` are re-exported from `src/validation/__init__.py` but have zero callers anywhere else in `src/` — a fully-built, security-conscious (shell=False, path-traversal checks, dangerous-pattern denylist) command/file/test validation-check executor that nothing in the validator-agent flow actually invokes. Validator agents currently determine pass/fail entirely through their own LLM judgment plus regex heuristics — this module was seemingly built for a criteria-driven automated-check model that was never wired in.
- **`RAGSystem`'s embedding source is a third, independent pathway** from the `embedding_factory.py`/`EMBEDDING_BACKEND` pathway that task-dedup and ticket-search use — two disjoint config surfaces that both have to agree with `TurboVecStore`'s hardcoded 384-dim collections, with no code anywhere cross-validating that agreement. A misconfiguration only surfaces as a silently-swallowed exception (logged, not raised) — `RAGSystem.retrieve_for_task`'s own top-level `try/except` would additionally swallow *that*, returning an empty memory list with no visible symptom beyond degraded enrichment quality.
- **`TurboVecStore` flushes the entire index+metadata to disk on every write** — no batching despite a `_flush_interval` field being defined and never used. An O(n)-per-insert scaling ceiling for any collection that grows large.
- **`RAGSystem`'s type hints are stale** in the same way `embedding_service.py`'s are: constructor-typed for the Qdrant-specific `VectorStoreManager`, but the real runtime object is `TurboVecStore` by default — works only by duck-typing.
- **`MemoryIngestion` and Guardian's `rag_system` wiring are both dead code** — built, never called.

### 6. Refactor observations

1. **Give `spawn_validator_agent` the same duplicate-guard treatment `create_agent_for_task_direct` has.** It is a third dispatch implementation bypassing both existing duplicate-guard/CLI-concurrency mechanisms; at minimum, `submit_result_validation`'s validator-agent lookup needs to be scoped by `result_id`/`workflow_id` instead of "most recently created," which is a correctness bug under concurrent result submissions today.
2. **Wire or delete `get_workspace_changes`/`get_agent_results`'s output in the task-validation prompt**, and delete `check_executors.py` if the criteria-driven automated-check model it implements is not planned to be adopted.
3. **Unify the two embedding-configuration surfaces.** `EMBEDDING_BACKEND`/`FASTEMBED_MODEL` (task-dedup, ticket-search) and `llm.embedding_provider`/`embedding_model` (RAG/memory) are two independent knobs that both have to agree with `TurboVecStore`'s hardcoded per-collection `dim: 384` — nothing validates that agreement, and a mismatch fails silently rather than at startup.
4. **Retire the `VectorStoreManager`-typed `RAGSystem` constructor in favor of `store_factory.VectorStoreProtocol`**, matching what `server.py` actually passes it — the same "type hint that lies about the runtime type" pattern already flagged for `embedding_service.py` recurs here.
5. **`RAGSystem` should route through `embedding_factory.create_embedding_provider()`** instead of `llm_provider.generate_embedding` — collapsing three independent embedding pathways (task-dedup, ticket-search, RAG/memory) down to one or two, removing an entire class of cross-backend dimension-mismatch risk.
6. **Delete unreachable code**: `check_executors.py`'s executor functions, `MemoryIngestion` (`rag.py:297-389`), and the unused `rag_system` parameter/attribute on `MonitoringLoop` — none have any caller in `src/`.
7. **Add a batched/debounced flush to `TurboVecStore.store_memory`** rather than a full index+metadata rewrite on every insert — the `_flush_interval` field already exists as a signal this was the intended design, just never implemented.
## Workflow Termination & API Surface

### 1. Purpose & responsibilities

`src/workflow/termination_handler.py` defines a single class, `WorkflowTerminationHandler`, whose only public entry points are `terminate_workflow()` (`src/workflow/termination_handler.py:26`) and `get_workflow_termination_status()` (`:377`). It is exported from `src/workflow/__init__.py:3-7` as the package's sole symbol. Despite the generic name, it has exactly **one caller in the entire codebase**: `src/mcp/memory_api.py:885-897`, inside the result-validation HTTP handler, and it is only invoked when `src/services/result_validator_service.py:118-119` decides `config.on_result_found == "stop_all"` for a validated `WorkflowResult`. This is the older, generic "workflow with a `has_result`/`on_result_found` config" mechanism, not the Autopilot feature pipeline.

The Autopilot feature pipeline (pause/resume/stop/rerun/requeue/delete) does **not** go through `WorkflowTerminationHandler` at all. It has its own, independently-written termination logic scattered across `src/mcp/autopilot_api.py` (`pause_feature` L3974, `stop_pipeline` L5408, `requeue_design` L830, `rerun_design` L925, `delete_feature` L4515) and `src/autopilot/orchestrator.py`'s `pause_project_workflows()` (L924-1011). None of these call into `src/workflow/termination_handler.py`. This split is itself the first structural problem worth flagging: there are two unrelated "stop a workflow and clean up its agents/tasks" implementations in the codebase, one generic and rarely used, one Autopilot-specific and duplicated five separate times with subtly different cleanup completeness.

`src/mcp/autopilot_api.py` and `src/mcp/projects_api.py` are the HTTP-facing surface that dashboard/CLI clients drive Autopilot through. `autopilot_api.py` (5725 lines) owns: pipeline status, design queue management, project cost rollups, feature pause/resume/review, review-mode toggling, messages/logs, human-input handoff, and pipeline start/stop. `projects_api.py` (354 lines) owns only `AutopilotProject` CRUD and activate/deactivate, enforcing the `max_concurrent_projects` cap described in this repo's `concurrent-active-projects` invariant.

### 2. Workflow termination flow — every way a Workflow can end

| Path | Trigger | Function (file:line) | Agent cleanup | Task cleanup | Feature/Phase cleanup |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Result validated, `on_result_found: stop_all`** | `POST` result-validation endpoint | `WorkflowTerminationHandler.terminate_workflow` (`termination_handler.py:26`) | `_terminate_workflow_agents` (L110) calls `agent_manager.terminate_agent()` (real teardown) for every non-`result_validator` agent | `_cancel_workflow_tasks` (L180) sets every `pending/assigned/in_progress/under_review` task to `status="failed"` | `_cleanup_workflow_resources` (L244) marks active `AgentBranch` worktrees `abandoned`, resolves each `PhaseExecution` to `completed`/`failed`/left-`in_progress` depending on whether it has `done` tasks |
