| **User pause of one feature** | `POST /api/autopilot/features/{id}/pause` | `pause_feature` (`autopilot_api.py:3974-4023`) | Raw field flip only: `agent.status="terminated"`, `current_task_id=None`, `terminated_at=utcnow()` — **does not call `agent_manager.terminate_agent()`** | Every non-done task → `status="blocked"` | `wf.status="paused"`, `wf.paused_by="user"`, `feature.status="paused"` |
| **User pause of whole pipeline** | `POST /api/autopilot/stop` | `stop_pipeline` (L5408) → `pause_project_workflows` (`orchestrator/engine_client.py:203-292`) | Same raw field flip pattern, no `terminate_agent()` call | `in_progress` tasks reset to `pending` | Workflow → `paused`/`paused_by=<caller>`; as of `22178b1`, also sets `Feature.status="paused"` |
| **Requeue a design** | `POST /api/autopilot/queue/requeue` | `requeue_design` (L830-923) | Raw field flip, no `terminate_agent()` | Active tasks reset to `pending`, `assigned_agent_id=None` | Workflow → `paused` |
| **Rerun a design** (hard reset) | `POST /api/autopilot/queue/rerun` | `rerun_design` (L925-1339) | Step 2: raw field flip for stuck agents, no `terminate_agent()` | Step 2: `assigned/in_progress` tasks → `pending`; Step 2b: **hard-deletes** `Task`, `Phase`, `PhaseExecution`, `Workflow`, and dependent rows for the matched workflows | `Feature` rows for the design are deleted, `AutopilotDesign.status` reset to `pending`, worktrees cleaned |
| **Delete a feature permanently** | `DELETE /api/autopilot/features/{id}` | `delete_feature` (L4515-4641) | **Correctly** calls `agent_manager.terminate_agent()` per agent — the only ad hoc Autopilot path that does | Cascade-deletes `Task` and dependents for the workflow | Deletes `Workflow`, `Feature` rows outright; worktree cleanup |
| **Backend restart (safe shutdown)** | FastAPI `shutdown_event` | `_notify_and_pause_for_restart` (`server.py:1175`) → `service.pause_for_restart()` (`service.py:290`) | Does **not** terminate agents — deliberately leaves them running; only sends a tmux text nudge | Untouched | Workflow left `active`/`paused`; the "was running" marker is preserved so `_resume_interrupted_workflows` auto-resumes on next boot |
| **Manual git hand-off required** (`git_commit_push` under review mode) | Phase advancement noticing `git_commit_push` needs retry | `_pause_for_manual_handoff` (`orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:1040-1065`) | None (no failure occurred; PermissionError just blocked dispatch) | None | Workflow → `paused`, `paused_by="review"`, `status_reason="<phase> is manual-only; human approval is required"` |

Every one of the four ad hoc Autopilot paths that skip `agent_manager.terminate_agent()` technically satisfies this repo's `agent-termination` invariant at the DB-row level, but they skip everything `terminate_agent()` does beyond that: WIP-commit of uncommitted worktree changes, tmux scrollback capture to `.hephaestus/tmux/*.transcript.log`, and actually killing the tmux session/process. The tmux session and its underlying CLI process are left running and orphaned; only the DB says the agent is gone.

`WorkflowStatus.ALL` (`database.py:75-83`) and the `workflows.status` `CheckConstraint` (`database.py:390-395`) only permit `active`, `paused`, `completed`, `failed` — there is no `"running"` value. Yet `Workflow.status.in_(["active", "running"])` (or with `"paused"`) appears at seven call sites: `orchestrator/engine_client.py:232`, `orchestrator/queue.py:61`; `autopilot_api.py:527`, `:555`, `:859`, `:1076`, `:5623`. Every `"running"` branch of these filters is dead — it can never match a row.

### 3. The git_commit_push human-approval saga

Reconstructed from `git show` on each commit (all landed the same day, within about eight hours):

1. **`c78a978` "fix: preserve resumable workflow handoffs"** (merged via PR `3f18b53`, chronologically first). Added an **unconditional** guard in `src/agents/manager.py`: any task on a phase named `git_commit_push` raises `PermissionError("git_commit_push is manual-only...")` — regardless of `review_mode`. Paired this with three raw `phase.name == "git_commit_push"` checks in `src/autopilot/orchestrator.py` that pause the workflow with `paused_by="review"` instead of retrying the phase as an ordinary failure. Also added `manual_only: true` to `config/workflows/autopilot/git_commit_push.yaml` — a YAML field that **no code anywhere ever reads** (confirmed by `git log -p -S"manual_only" -- src/` returning zero hits across all history); the actual gating was always the hardcoded phase-name string check in Python, not this YAML flag.

2. **`71d870b` "feat: make git_commit_push autonomous in full autopilot, manual-only in review mode"** (same day, ~2 hours later). Replaced the unconditional guard with a project-scoped one: `src/agents/manager.py:326-341` now only raises `PermissionError` if `AutopilotProject.review_mode` is `True` for the task's project. Centralized the three orchestrator.py check sites behind `MANUAL_ONLY_PHASES = {"git_commit_push"}` (`orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:1017`) and `_manual_handoff_required(workflow_id)` (`phase_transitions.py:1020-1039`), which is just `_should_pause_for_review(project_id)` reusing the same `review_mode` flag. Removed the now-redundant `manual_only: true` line from the YAML.

3. **`6743e0a` "revert: restore manual_only: true on git_commit_push.yaml"** (3 minutes later). Added `manual_only: true` back to the YAML — but did **not** revert any of `71d870b`'s Python changes. Since nothing reads that YAML key, this commit was a functional no-op that left a misleading, dead config flag sitting in the file.

4. **`bc51340` "Revert 'revert: restore manual_only: true...'"** (2 minutes after that). Removed the dead YAML key again, settling on the current state.

5. **`4a97515` "revert: switch autopilot YAML configs from codex back to claude/sonnet"** — unrelated churn from the same session, not causally connected to the gating logic.

6. **`cb8c01a` "fix: test_rejects_git_commit_push_without_human_approval was broken by 71d870b"** (same day, ~7.5 hours later). `71d870b` changed the guard from unconditional to `review_mode`-conditional, but its own test suite still asserted the old unconditional behavior with no `AutopilotProject`/`review_mode` set up — meaning the test read `review_mode=False` by default and the `PermissionError` no longer fired, so the test silently failed on `main` from `71d870b` until this fix. `cb8c01a` renamed the test to `test_rejects_git_commit_push_without_human_approval_in_review_mode`, gave it a real `review_mode=True` `AutopilotProject`, and added a companion `test_does_not_reject_git_commit_push_in_full_autopilot`. It also fixed test infrastructure — `resolve_project_for_workflow` calls `get_db()` directly and was silently reading a different, table-less default database in the test.

**Current final state**: `config/workflows/autopilot/git_commit_push.yaml` has no `manual_only` field at all. The gate lives entirely in code: `src/agents/manager.py:326-341` raises `PermissionError` for a `git_commit_push` task only when the task's resolved `AutopilotProject.review_mode` is `True`; `src/autopilot/orchestrator/phase_transitions.py:1017-1065` mirrors that same check at three phase-advancement call sites so a rejected dispatch pauses the workflow (`paused_by="review"`) instead of retrying forever. In full autopilot (the default, `review_mode=False`), `git_commit_push` dispatches autonomously like any other phase — it will commit, push, merge, and even auto-merge the PR on human approval in review mode. Notably, `git_commit_push.yaml`'s own `description:` block (lines 4-7, still current) reads "Human-only Git hand-off. The pipeline must not commit, push, create pull requests, or merge branches" — this text was never updated across any of the six commits above and now directly contradicts both the STEP 1-5 prompt body beneath it and the actual `review_mode`-gated code behavior.

The thrash — feature → runtime-conditional refinement → dead-flag-restore → dead-flag-re-removal, with the test suite silently broken for 7.5 hours in between — is a direct symptom of the policy being expressed in three disconnected places at once (a YAML field nobody read, a hardcoded phase-name string repeated at 4+ call sites in Python, and a stale prose description), with no single source of truth.

### 4. Pause/resume mechanics

- **`22178b1` "fix: pause button leaving agents running and features showing Active"**: Two independent bugs, fixed together. (a) `src/agents/manager.py:907-931` — an agent mid-launch (inside the ~25s CLI-init wait) had no way to notice a pause hit; the coroutine delivered its initial prompt regardless. Fixed by re-checking `Agent.status == "terminated"` right after the sleep, and if so, killing the tmux session and aborting the launch without delivering the prompt. (b) `pause_project_workflows` never set `Feature.status="paused"`, leaving features stuck showing "Active" — fixed by a loop in `orchestrator/engine_client.py:251-259`. Also tightened `get_pipeline_status`'s "is anything actually still working" checks to exclude `paused` workflows.

- **`c78a978` "fix: preserve resumable workflow handoffs"**: despite the title, this is the commit that introduced the (originally unconditional) `git_commit_push` manual-only guard — see §3.

- **`271745f` "feat: safe restart grace window, fix autopilot status and phase0 persistence"**: Introduces `SAFE_RESTART_GRACE_SECONDS = 10` and `_notify_and_pause_for_restart` (`server.py:1175`). On shutdown it first best-effort-notifies every `working` agent via tmux text injection to checkpoint/save memory, then sleeps 10s (bounded by `mcp_client.py`'s own 10s HTTP timeout) so an in-flight `complete_my_task` call already on the wire can land before the process stops accepting connections, then pauses every running service via `service.pause_for_restart()` rather than `stop()`. `pause_for_restart()` deliberately preserves the "was running" marker so `_resume_interrupted_workflows` (`server.py:600`) auto-resumes each project's pipeline on the next boot without requiring the user to press Play again. Also moved `run_single_workflow`'s persistence of `phase0_workflow_id` to happen immediately after agent launch, and `run_phase0` now persists `AutopilotDesign.designs_folder` before the workflow starts — both so recovery can find in-flight Phase-0 state even across a mid-run crash.

### 5. MCP API surface

Mounted in `src/mcp/server.py`: `autopilot_router` (prefix `/api/autopilot`) and `projects_router` (prefix `/api/projects`), both included inside the `startup_event` handler, after `server_state.initialize()`.

**`src/mcp/projects_api.py`** (`/api/projects` prefix) — project CRUD/activation, small and consistent:
- `GET ""` → `list_projects` — all projects with design counts
- `GET "/active"` → `get_active_project` — every currently-active project (supports >1 given `max_concurrent_projects`)
- `POST ""` → `create_project` — auto-activates the first project ever created if the concurrency cap allows
- `PUT "/{project_id}"` → `update_project`
- `DELETE "/{project_id}"` → `delete_project` — if the deleted project was active, promotes the next project alphabetically
- `POST "/{project_id}/activate"` → `activate_project` — enforces `max_concurrent_projects`, never force-clears other active projects
- `POST "/{project_id}/deactivate"` → `deactivate_project`

**`src/mcp/autopilot_api.py`** (`/api/autopilot` prefix) — grouped by function:

*Pipeline status/lifecycle*: `GET /status` → `get_pipeline_status` (largest read endpoint); `POST /start` → `start_pipeline` → `_start_pipeline_reserved` (concurrency-cap reservation, zombie-pipeline auto-stop-and-restart heuristic); `POST /stop` → `stop_pipeline`; `POST /cleanup-branches`; `GET /health` → `run_health_audit`.

*Design queue*: `GET /queue`, `POST /queue/reorder`, `POST /queue/requeue` (§2), `POST /queue/rerun` (§2, by far the largest single handler at ~415 lines), `POST /queue/repair` (correctly offloads to a thread pool via `loop.run_in_executor`, unlike most other blocking-call sites in this file), `GET /queue/repair/{repair_id}`, `POST /designs/add`, `POST /queue`, `DELETE /queue/{filename}`, `GET /queue/{filename}/content`.

*Projects (cost/design sub-resources, duplicating some of `projects_api.py`'s surface under a different prefix)*: `GET/POST /projects`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /projects/{id}` — a **second** project CRUD surface living under `/api/autopilot/projects/...`, parallel to `projects_api.py`'s `/api/projects/...`. Plus cost rollups (`POST /cost-entries`, `GET /tasks/{id}/costs`, etc.) and design-file browse/sync endpoints.

*Feature/workflow reports & review*: `GET /workflows/{id}/feature_report`, `/decomposition_review`; `GET /features`; `POST /features/{id}/pause` (§2); `POST /features/{id}/resume` — recovers dead-agent tasks, resumes workflow, spawns fresh agents via fire-and-forget `asyncio.create_task`; `PATCH /projects/{id}/review-mode`; `POST /features/{id}/review` — approve/request-changes; on approve, writes a `review_approved` marker file, then **synchronously shells out to `gh pr merge`** (see §6); `DELETE /features/{id}` (§2); `GET /features/{id}` plus docs/report/logs sub-routes.

*Messages / logs / human input*: message archive endpoints, `GET /logs`, `GET/POST/DELETE /input` — human-input request/response handoff.

**Blocking calls in async handlers without a thread pool** (violates this repo's forbidden-pattern rule):
- `rerun_design` (`async def`) calls bare `time.sleep(0.5)` in a stray-subprocess-kill loop, up to 10 iterations, twice — up to ~5.5s of blocking the event loop per rerun request.
- `review_feature` (`async def`) calls `subprocess.run(["gh", "pr", "merge", ...], timeout=30)` directly — up to 30s blocking.
- `remove_project_design` (`async def`) calls `subprocess.run(["tmux", "kill-session", ...], timeout=3)` directly, inside a loop over agents.
- `get_system_health` (`async def`) calls `run_health_audit()` synchronously, which itself runs several `subprocess.run()` calls (`pgrep`, `tmux list-panes`, `git branch`, timeouts 3-10s each) entirely on the event-loop thread.
- By contrast, `repair_design` does this correctly via `loop.run_in_executor(None, _run_repair, ...)`, showing the pattern is known in this codebase but not applied consistently.

### 6. Known fragile spots / code smells

1. **Two unrelated workflow-termination implementations.** `WorkflowTerminationHandler` is fully-featured (agents, tasks, worktrees, phase executions) but used only by the generic result-validation path. The Autopilot feature pipeline reimplements a subset of the same logic ad hoc, five separate times, with none of them delegating to it or to each other.

2. **Inconsistent agent teardown across those five ad hoc paths.** `delete_feature` and `WorkflowTerminationHandler._terminate_workflow_agents` call the real `agent_manager.terminate_agent()` (WIP-commit, tmux scrollback capture, session kill). `pause_feature`, `pause_project_workflows`/`stop_pipeline`, `requeue_design`, and `rerun_design`'s Step 2 instead hand-flip `Agent.status/current_task_id/terminated_at` directly on the ORM row. All four satisfy the letter of this repo's `agent-termination` invariant, but none of them kill the underlying tmux session or preserve uncommitted worktree changes.

3. **`manual_only: true` was a dead YAML field for its entire existence.** Written, removed, restored, and removed again across four commits inside a single day, and no code ever read it. The actual policy has always lived in a Python string comparison duplicated across `manager.py` and three sites in `orchestrator.py`.

4. **`git_commit_push.yaml`'s own description block contradicts its body and the current code.** An agent (or a human reading the config) gets a materially wrong first impression of the phase's actual autonomy.

5. **A test regression sat silent for ~7.5 hours** (`71d870b` → `cb8c01a`) because the runtime behavior changed without the existing test being updated in the same commit.

6. **`"running"` is checked for everywhere but never producible.** Seven call sites filter `Workflow.status.in_(["active", "running"])`, but the CheckConstraint and `WorkflowStatus.ALL` only allow `active/paused/completed/failed`.

7. **`rerun_design` is a ~415-line route handler** doing orchestration (stop service, kill stray subprocess, scope-and-terminate agents, cascade-delete ~10 tables, clean worktrees, restart service, poll for the new workflow) entirely inline. This logic has no owning service class — it duplicates pieces of both `pause_project_workflows` and `delete_feature`'s cleanup without sharing code with either.

8. **`review_feature`'s approve path does real external side effects synchronously inline in the route handler**: writing a marker file, then shelling out to `gh pr merge` with a blocking `subprocess.run(..., timeout=30)`, then re-deriving workflow/feature completion status — all inside one `async def` with no thread-pool offload.

### 7. Refactor observations

1. **Consolidate Autopilot's five ad hoc termination paths behind one service-level function** (or extend `WorkflowTerminationHandler` to cover the Autopilot case), so "terminate agent(s) + reset/fail task(s) + update workflow/feature status" has exactly one implementation that always calls `agent_manager.terminate_agent()` for real teardown.

2. **Give `manual_only`/human-approval gating a single source of truth.** Today it's a hardcoded phase-name check duplicated across `manager.py` and three `orchestrator.py` call sites, gated by a project flag (`review_mode`) whose name doesn't even mention "manual" or "git," plus a stale, contradictory YAML description. A `PhasePolicy`/`is_manual_only(phase, project) -> bool` function all four call sites delegate to would make the policy auditable in one place and prevent the kind of silent test regression `cb8c01a` had to fix after the fact.

3. **Move `rerun_design` and `requeue_design`'s business logic out of `autopilot_api.py` into an orchestrator/service-layer function.** A thin route that calls `orchestrator.rerun_design(project, filename)` would let this logic be unit-tested without FastAPI and would stop it from silently diverging from `delete_feature`'s and `pause_project_workflows`'s overlapping cleanup code.

4. **Wrap the remaining blocking calls in async route handlers with `run_in_executor`**, matching the pattern `repair_design` already uses: `rerun_design`'s `time.sleep` loop, `review_feature`'s `gh pr merge`, `remove_project_design`'s `tmux kill-session`, and `get_system_health`/`run_health_audit`'s several `subprocess.run` calls. This is an explicit forbidden-pattern violation per this project's own conventions.

5. **Delete the dead `"running"` branch from the seven `Workflow.status.in_(["active", "running"])` filters**, or add `"running"` to `WorkflowStatus.ALL`/the CheckConstraint if it was actually meant to exist as a distinct state.

6. **Reconcile the two parallel project-CRUD surfaces** (`/api/projects/*` in `projects_api.py` vs. `/api/autopilot/projects/*` in `autopilot_api.py`). Having project create/read/update/delete duplicated under two different route prefixes is a maintenance hazard even if their current bodies happen to agree.
## Core Agent & Task API (src/mcp/api.py)

### 1. Purpose & route inventory

`src/mcp/api.py` (3,225 lines) is the dashboard-facing FastAPI router: `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api", tags=["Frontend API"])` (`:32`). Almost every handler is a thin wrapper around a method on `FrontendAPI` (`:35-2787`, a stateless-per-call class holding `db_manager`/`agent_manager`/`phase_manager` references); a handful of routes are defined as closures directly inside `create_frontend_routes` (`:2793-3225`) with their query/DB logic inlined rather than delegated to a `FrontendAPI` method. `create_frontend_routes` is called once, from `src/mcp/server.py`'s `startup_event` (`server.py:848`, right after `server_state.initialize()`), and the returned `router` is mounted via `app.include_router(api_router)` (`server.py:849`) with **no auth dependency and no prefix beyond the router's own `/api`** — same mounting pattern as `autopilot_router`/`projects_router` alongside it.

Full surface, 42 routes, grouped by function (method, path, FastAPI closure file:line → `FrontendAPI` method file:line where one exists):

**Agent read/monitoring**
| Method/Path | Closure | Handler |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /agents` | `:2818-2821` | `get_agents` `:317` |
| `GET /agents/{agent_id}/output` | `:2823-2826` | `get_agent_output` `:431` |
| `GET /guardian-analyses/{agent_id}` | `:2897-2902` | `get_guardian_analyses` `:1214` |
| `GET /phases/{phase_id}/agents` | `:3089-3092` | `get_phase_agents` `:2221` |

**Agent control (force actions — see §2)**
| Method/Path | Closure | Handler |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /phases/{phase_id}/reset` | `:3080-3087` | `reset_phase` `:2107` |
| `POST /workflows/{workflow_id}/stop` | `:2995-3072` (inline, no `FrontendAPI` delegate) | — |

**Task read**: `GET /tasks` (`:2807-2816` → `get_tasks` `:237`), `GET /tasks/{task_id}` (`:2887-2890` → `get_task` `:883`), `GET /tasks/{task_id}/full-details` (`:2892-2895` → `get_task_full_details` `:923`), `GET /tasks/{task_id}/prompt` (`:3199-3208`, inline), `GET /blocked-tasks` (`:2980-2983` → `get_blocked_tasks` `:1980`), `GET /blocked-tasks/{task_id}/blockers` (`:2985-2988` → `get_task_blocker_details` `:1991`).

**Task control**: `POST /sync-blocking-status` (`:2990-2993` → `sync_blocking_status` `:2011`). There is **no** dedicated force-fail-task, force-retry-task, reassign-task, or priority-bump route in this file — see §3.

**Phase definitions & prompt-version editor**: `GET /phases`, `GET /phases/{phase_id}/yaml`, `GET /workflow-definitions/{definition_id}/phases`, `PATCH /phases/{phase_id}`, `GET/POST /phases/{phase_id}/prompt/versions[/{version}[/publish|restore]]`, `GET/POST /phases/{phase_id}/prompt/preview`, `GET /phases/{phase_id}/prompt/diff`, `GET/PUT/DELETE /tasks/{task_id}/prompt/overrides` — a full prompt-version-control editor surface (`:2848-3223`).

**Dashboard/monitoring reads (see §4)**: `GET /dashboard/stats`, `GET /memories`, `GET /graph`, `GET /workflow`, `GET /conductor-analyses[/latest]`, `GET /steering-interventions`, `GET /system-overview`, `GET /results` + 5 sub-routes (content/validation/extra-files/download/validation-download).

**Health/diagnostics**: none. Unlike `autopilot_api.py` (`GET /api/autopilot/health` → `run_health_audit`), this file has no dedicated health-check route — `/dashboard/stats` and `/system-overview` are the closest analogues, and both are plain aggregation reads, not audits.

### 2. Agent control routes in depth

Only two routes in this file terminate or force-manipulate an `Agent` row — both raw ORM field flips, neither calling `AgentManager.terminate_agent()`. Extending the Agent Lifecycle Management §2 audit table with the two rows it already flagged, confirmed at current line numbers:

| Route | current_task_id=None | terminated_at | Task reset | Tmux kill | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `reset_phase` (`FrontendAPI.reset_phase`, `:2107-2219`) — agent write at `:2174-2176` | yes | **missing** | `assigned/in_progress/pending` for the phase → `failed`, `:2180-2190` | yes, via `loop.run_in_executor(None, subprocess.run(["tmux","kill-session",...]))`, `:2159-2173` — correctly offloaded | **VIOLATION — no terminated_at** |
| `stop_workflow` (inline closure, `:2995-3072`) — agent write at `:3025-3027` | yes | **missing** | `assigned/in_progress/queued/pending` for the workflow → `failed`, `:3041-3054` | yes, via bare `subprocess.run(["tmux","kill-session",...], timeout=5)` — **not** offloaded to an executor, `:3029-3039` | **VIOLATION — no terminated_at, and blocking subprocess call (see §4)** |

Both routes gate on active-agent presence: `reset_phase` requires `force=true` once `active_agents` is non-empty and returns `requires_confirmation: true` otherwise (`:2139-2145`); `stop_workflow` has no such gate — it unconditionally terminates every `working` agent the moment the route is hit, only 400ing if `workflow.status != "active"` (`:3010-3013`).

Neither route reimplements `AgentManager.terminate_agent()`'s full behavior — both skip WIP-commit of uncommitted worktree changes and tmux scrollback capture, only killing the session and flipping DB fields. This matches the pattern already documented for the five ad hoc Autopilot termination paths in Workflow Termination & API Surface §2/§6: `reset_phase` and `stop_workflow` are effectively a **sixth and seventh** ad hoc termination implementation, in a third file, with the same missing-`terminated_at` defect independently reproduced rather than shared.

### 3. Task control routes in depth

This file has no force-fail, force-retry, reassign, or priority-bump route for tasks — those live in `src/mcp/server.py` and, at workflow scope, in `autopilot_api.py`. The only task-touching writes in `api.py` are the two agent-control routes' cascading task-fail side effects (§2), plus:

- **`POST /sync-blocking-status`** (`:2990-2993` → `FrontendAPI.sync_blocking_status`, `:2011-2020`) calls `TaskBlockingService.sync_task_blocking_status()` directly and synchronously, with no scheduling of its own — this is the exact on-demand trigger point Task & Dispatch Services §4 refers to when it says the reconciliation sweep "is not wired to any scheduler within these 7 files; its caller is external (`src/mcp/api.py:2013-2016`)." Confirmed: the call site is `:2016`, inside a bare `try/except` that maps any exception to a 500. Drift between `Ticket.blocked_by_ticket_ids` and `Task.status="blocked"` persists until a dashboard user hits this exact endpoint — there is no periodic job anywhere in the codebase that calls `sync_task_blocking_status()`.

One notable **omission**: `reset_phase`'s task-fail branch fails every `assigned/in_progress/pending` task in the phase but does not touch `queued` or `blocked` tasks, while `stop_workflow`'s equivalent branch additionally covers `queued`. Two routes doing structurally the same "cascade-fail this scope's active tasks" operation use two different status sets, with no shared helper.

### 4. Dashboard/read-only routes

**`GuardianAnalysis`** (`get_guardian_analyses`, `:1214-1268`): per-agent trajectory reads, order-by-timestamp, with a derived `phase_changed` flag computed client-side-in-Python across consecutive rows — no new DB writes.

**`ConductorAnalysis` / `DetectedDuplicate`** (`get_conductor_analyses`, `:1270-1318`; `get_latest_conductor_analysis`, `:1320-1323`): confirmed read-only. For each `ConductorAnalysis` row, a nested query fetches its `DetectedDuplicate` rows by `conductor_analysis_id` — an N+1 pattern rather than a single joined query, though `limit` defaults to 20 so the practical cost is bounded. `get_latest_conductor_analysis` is a one-line wrapper that calls `get_conductor_analyses(limit=1)` rather than a dedicated `ORDER BY ... LIMIT 1` query.

**`SteeringIntervention`** (`get_steering_interventions`, `:1325-1355`): confirmed **read-only in this file**. Grepping `SteeringIntervention(` (the constructor call) across `src/` turns up **zero instantiations anywhere in the codebase** — confirming Cross-Cutting Themes §5's "zero writers anywhere in `src/`" claim with an explicit grep result. The table is not entirely untouched, however: `src/mcp/server.py:3574`, inside `restart_task_endpoint`'s old-agent trajectory cleanup, does `session.query(SteeringIntervention).filter_by(agent_id=old_agent_id).delete()` — a **delete-only** consumer, in a different file, of a table nothing ever populates. The dashboard's steering-intervention feed will therefore always render empty; the delete call exists purely to pre-emptively clean up rows that can never exist.

**Health checks**: none in this file. `get_dashboard_stats` and `get_system_overview` are the closest analogues — both are plain aggregation reads, not audits with subprocess-based liveness checks like `autopilot_api.py`'s `run_health_audit`.

**Blocking calls in async handlers — new findings beyond §2's `stop_workflow`:**
- `get_agent_output` (`:431-438`) calls `self.agent_manager.get_agent_output(agent_id, lines)` **synchronously, unawaited, with no executor offload**. Per Agent Lifecycle Management §7's "five-level fallback chain," a live (non-terminated) agent whose stable transcript isn't available falls through to `_capture_pane_lines` (`manager.py:3151-3166`), which shells out to `tmux` via libtmux under the hood. A dashboard poll on this endpoint can trigger a synchronous subprocess call on the event-loop thread, with no `run_in_executor` anywhere in the call chain.
- `create_phase_prompt_version` (`:2333-2455`) and `restore_phase_prompt_version` (`:2503-2594`) each wrap their write in a 3-attempt retry loop against `IntegrityError` on the version-number race, calling bare `time.sleep(0.05 * (attempt + 1))` on retry — inside an `async def` handler, un-offloaded. Bounded at ~0.15s worst case per request, small individually, but a second, previously undocumented instance of the same forbidden-pattern violation found in `autopilot_api.py`.
- `stop_workflow` (`:3029-3039`, inside a `for agent in agents:` loop) calls bare `subprocess.run(["tmux", "kill-session", ...], timeout=5)` with no executor offload — up to 5s of blocked event loop *per agent* being terminated, worse than `reset_phase`'s otherwise-identical tmux-kill step three lines away in the same class, which does use `loop.run_in_executor`. Same operation, same file, two different concurrency disciplines a few hundred lines apart.
- `get_extra_file_content` (`:1852-1915`) does plain synchronous `open(file_path, "r"/"rb").read()` on a disk file whose path is stored on `WorkflowResult.extra_files` — unbounded by any size check before the read.

**Inconsistent `DatabaseManager` instantiation**: `post_phase_prompt_preview` (`:3134-3190`) constructs its own `DatabaseManager("hephaestus.db")` (`:3141`) instead of reusing `frontend_api.db_manager` — the only route in the file to do so. `DatabaseManager.__init__`'s env-var fallback (`database_path = os.environ.get("HEPHAESTUS_TEST_DB", "hephaestus.db")`, `src/core/database.py:1342-1343`) only fires when the caller passes `None`; passing the literal string `"hephaestus.db"` short-circuits that branch. If the running server was configured with a different DB path (e.g. under test via `HEPHAESTUS_TEST_DB`), this one route silently reads/writes a different database file than every other endpoint in the same process — engine-caching (`DatabaseManager._engines`, keyed by path) means it won't even error, just silently diverge.

### 5. Cross-references to existing sections

- `reset_phase`/`stop_workflow` are a direct extension of the Agent Lifecycle Management §2 termination-invariant audit table — both routes were already flagged there as the only two, currently-unfixed, `terminated_at`-missing violations found by this whole analysis. This section adds the `run_in_executor` discrepancy between the two routes' otherwise-identical tmux-kill step.
- `reset_phase`/`stop_workflow`'s cascading task-fail logic are two more rows in Workflow Termination & API Surface §2's termination-flow table pattern — ad hoc "terminate agent(s) via raw field flip + fail tasks + touch workflow/phase status" logic, independently written a sixth and seventh time, in a third file (`api.py`, alongside `autopilot_api.py` and `orchestrator.py`), neither calling `WorkflowTerminationHandler` nor any of the five Autopilot-specific paths already documented there.
- `sync_task_blocking_status` trigger confirms, at the precise current line (`:2016`), the claim already made in Task & Dispatch Services §4 that this service's reconciliation sweep has no periodic scheduler and is only reachable via this one on-demand dashboard route.
- `SteeringIntervention` read confirms the "zero writers" dead-table claim with a fresh grep, and adds a previously undocumented detail — the table has exactly one *consumer* pattern in the whole codebase (delete-only, `server.py:3574`).
- `ConductorAnalysis`/`DetectedDuplicate` reads: this file is where that subsystem's data actually surfaces to a human — a dashboard user looking at "detected duplicates" here sees Conductor's live-agent-collision data, not `TaskSimilarityService`'s task-dedup data, even though both concepts share the word "duplicate."
- Blocking-call findings extend Cross-Cutting Themes §7's `autopilot_api.py`-only list with four more instances, all in `api.py`: `get_agent_output`'s subprocess-backed `capture-pane` fallback, `create_phase_prompt_version`/`restore_phase_prompt_version`'s retry-loop `time.sleep`, and `stop_workflow`'s un-offloaded `subprocess.run`. The theme was previously scoped to one file; it recurs in a second.
- Every read-heavy route documented in §1/§4 is a thin, direct-SQLAlchemy projection over the same `Agent`/`Task`/`Phase` tables `AgentManager`/`orchestrator.py`/the services layer already own — this file introduces no new write paths for those tables outside the two force-action routes in §2, so it is overwhelmingly a **read-only reporting layer**.

### 6. Known fragile spots / code smells

- **Two independently-written agent-termination code paths in one class, ~850 lines apart** (`reset_phase` `:2107`, `stop_workflow` `:2995`), both missing `terminated_at`, one additionally missing the `run_in_executor` offload the other has for the identical tmux-kill operation — confirmed present a fourth and fifth time (after `83e79ec`, `92caa82`, `27e8e88`) in a file none of those audits covered.
- **Inconsistent task-status-set on structurally identical cascade-fail logic**: `reset_phase` fails `assigned/in_progress/pending`; `stop_workflow` fails `assigned/in_progress/queued/pending`. No shared helper.
- **`FrontendAPI` methods vs. inline route closures split arbitrarily**: roughly two-thirds of routes delegate to a `FrontendAPI` method; the rest (`stop_workflow`, `get_definition_phases`, `get_task_prompt`, `post_phase_prompt_preview`) have their entire body inline, with no apparent rule for which pattern a given route gets. `stop_workflow` in particular — the file's largest single non-delegated block of business logic — has no unit-testable method to call independently of the FastAPI route machinery.
- **`post_phase_prompt_preview`'s hardcoded `DatabaseManager("hephaestus.db")`**: the one place in this file that doesn't reuse the injected `db_manager`, silently capable of pointing at a different database than the rest of the process.
- **`GuardianAnalysis`/`ConductorAnalysis`/`DetectedDuplicate` N+1 query patterns**: `get_conductor_analyses` issues one `DetectedDuplicate` query per `ConductorAnalysis` row; `get_system_overview` (`:1357-1483`) issues one `GuardianAnalysis` query per active agent plus one more per `ConductorAnalysis` row, while also internally calling three other `FrontendAPI` methods, each opening and closing its **own** `db_manager.get_session()` — a single `GET /system-overview` request can open four or more separate DB sessions sequentially rather than sharing one.
- **`get_graph_data`** (`:499-706`) builds "task spawned task" edges via a nested `for task in tasks: for other_task in tasks:` double loop — O(n²) in the workflow's task count, with no caching or pre-indexing by `created_by_agent_id`.
- **`reset_phase`'s active-agent detection scopes by `Task.phase_id == phase.id` only** (`:2131-2137`) — it doesn't also match the numeric-order-string form of `phase_id` the way `get_workflow_info`/`get_graph_data`/`get_phase_agents` all explicitly handle elsewhere in this same file. If a task's `phase_id` was ever stored as the numeric order string rather than the phase UUID, `reset_phase` would silently fail to detect its agent as active.

### 7. Refactor observations

1. **Route this file's two agent-termination sites through one shared primitive**, consistent with the Tier 1 roadmap item already proposed in Agent Lifecycle Management §8 / Cross-Cutting Themes §2 — a unified `terminate_agent(agent_id, *, kill_tmux, reason)` would close both `reset_phase:2174` and `stop_workflow:3025` in one change and resolve the `run_in_executor` inconsistency for free.
2. **Extract `stop_workflow`'s inline body into a `FrontendAPI` method**, matching `reset_phase`'s shape, so it's unit-testable independent of FastAPI.
3. **Unify the task-fail status set between `reset_phase` and `stop_workflow`** behind one helper — `cascade_fail_tasks(scope_filter, reason)`.
4. **Wrap `stop_workflow`'s per-agent `subprocess.run` in `run_in_executor`**, matching the pattern `reset_phase` already demonstrates three lines away in the same file.
5. **Fix `post_phase_prompt_preview`'s `DatabaseManager` instantiation** to reuse `frontend_api.db_manager`, removing a silent-divergence risk under any non-default DB path configuration.
6. **Either wire `SteeringIntervention` up to a real writer or delete the table + its two consumers** (`api.py`'s read endpoint, `server.py:3574`'s delete-only cleanup) — a dashboard feed that can only ever render empty, and a defensive delete against rows that structurally cannot exist.
## Additional API Surface: Agents, Tickets, Messaging

### 1. Mounting & purpose

`src/mcp/server.py` wires all three routers explicitly rather than through a common convention:

| File | Router declaration | Mount point in `server.py` | Timing |
|---|---|---|---|
| `src/mcp/agents_api.py` | `router = APIRouter(tags=["agents"])` — **no prefix**, every path is hardcoded in full | `app.include_router(agents_router)` (`server.py:147`) | module level (import time) |
| `src/mcp/tickets_api.py` | `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api/tickets", tags=["tickets"])` | `app.include_router(tickets_router)` (`server.py:146`) | module level — the code comment at `server.py:869-872` explains this is deliberate: `TestClient(app)` used without the `with` context manager never fires `startup_event`, so tickets had to move out of it during an M-1 extraction |
| `src/mcp/messaging_api.py` | `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api", tags=["messaging"])` | `app.include_router(messaging_router)` (`server.py:148`) | module level |

Because `agents_api.py` declares no prefix, it mixes two path conventions in one file: most routes are `/api/agents/...`, but `GET /agent_status` (`agents_api.py:648`) and `GET /task_progress` (`agents_api.py:697`) are un-prefixed legacy-style paths — a naming inconsistency signaling these two predate the `/api/*` convention the rest of the file follows.

`tickets_api.py` and `messaging_api.py` are registered at module level; `src/mcp/api.py`'s `api_router` is registered later, inside `startup_event` (`server.py:849`). This registration-order gap is the root cause of a dead-route collision detailed in §3.2.

### 2. Route inventory

#### 2.1 `src/mcp/agents_api.py` (no prefix — full paths shown; 773 lines)

*Agent listing/detail*
| Method | Path | Function (line) | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/api/agents` | `list_agents` (`180`) | Paginated agent list, `status=active\|all` filter, optional `project_id` scoping |
| GET | `/api/agents/{agent_id}` | `get_agent` (`229`) | Single agent detail |
| POST | `/api/agents/{agent_id}/message` | `send_agent_message` (`242`) | Parent/UI nudge into an agent's tmux pane |
| GET | `/api/agents/{agent_id}/logs` | `get_agent_logs` (`264`) | `AgentLog` rows for the agent |
| GET | `/api/agents/{agent_id}/output` | `get_agent_output` (`295`) | Transcript/tmux output, capped at 30000 lines, 0 lines forced if `status=="terminated"` |
| GET | `/api/tasks/{task_id}/instructions` | `get_task_instructions` (`325`) | Reads `.hephaestus/tasks/{task_id}.md` out of the resolved worktree |

*Parent-child agent communication* (all delegate to `AgentCommunicationService`, see §5)
`GET /api/agents/{agent_id}/children` (`394`), `.../children/status` (`409`), `.../children/{child_id}/logs` (`424`), `POST .../children/{child_id}/message` (`444`), `POST .../children/{child_id}/nudge` (`472`), `POST .../children/monitor` (`500`).

*Agent lifecycle*
| Method | Path | Function (line) | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| POST | `/api/create_agent_for_task` | `create_agent_for_task_endpoint` (`528`) | Dispatch — delegates straight to `AgentManager.create_agent_for_task` |
| POST | `/api/terminate_agent` | `terminate_agent_endpoint` (`576`) | Manual UI termination — delegates to `AgentManager.terminate_agent` (see §3.1 audit) |
| GET | `/agent_status` | `get_agent_status` (`648`) | Bare-path status lookup |
| GET | `/task_progress` | `get_task_progress` (`697`) | Bare-path task-progress lookup |

#### 2.2 `src/mcp/tickets_api.py` (prefix `/api/tickets`; 1407 lines)

| Method | Path | Function (line) | Delegates to |
|---|---|---|---|
| POST | `/create` | `create_ticket_endpoint` (`408`) | `TicketService.create_ticket` |
| POST | `/update` | `update_ticket_endpoint` (`509`) | `TicketService.update_ticket` |
| POST | `/change-status` | `change_ticket_status_endpoint` (`546`) | `TicketService.change_status` |
| POST | `/comment` | `add_comment_endpoint` (`588`) | `TicketService.add_comment` |
| GET | `/pending-review-count` | `get_pending_review_count_endpoint` (`627`) | `TicketService.get_pending_review_count`/`get_pending_review_tickets` |
| GET | `/stats[/{workflow_id}]` | `get_ticket_stats_endpoint` (`648`) | **Inline aggregation** — direct SQLAlchemy queries, not `TicketService` (see §4.1) |
| GET | `/{ticket_id}` | `get_ticket_endpoint` (`850`) | `TicketService.get_ticket` |
| POST | `/search` | `search_tickets_endpoint` (`880`) | `TicketSearchService.semantic_search`/`keyword_search`/`hybrid_search` |
| GET | `""`, `"/"` | `get_tickets_endpoint` (`940`) | `TicketService.get_tickets_by_workflow`, looped per-workflow when `project_id` given |
| POST | `/resolve` | `resolve_ticket_endpoint` (`1014`) | `TicketService.resolve_ticket` |
| POST | `/link-commit` | `link_commit_endpoint` (`1051`) | `TicketService.link_commit` |
| POST | `/approve` | `approve_ticket_endpoint` (`1137`) | `TicketService.approve_ticket` — resolves the `TicketApprovalManager` asyncio.Event (see §4.2) |
| POST | `/reject` | `reject_ticket_endpoint` (`1195`) | `TicketService.reject_ticket` — same Event mechanism |
| GET | `/commit-diff/{commit_sha}` | `get_commit_diff_endpoint` (`1261`) | **No service delegation** — inline `git show`/`git diff --numstat`/`git diff` via three raw `subprocess.run` calls (see §6) |

#### 2.3 `src/mcp/messaging_api.py` (prefix `/api`; 338 lines)

| Method | Path | Function (line) | Delegates to |
|---|---|---|---|
| POST | `/broadcast_message` | `broadcast_message` (`88`) | `AgentManager.broadcast_message_to_all_agents` |
| POST | `/send_message` | `send_message` (`126`) | `AgentManager.send_direct_message` |
| POST | `/tickets/request-clarification` | `request_ticket_clarification_endpoint` (`171`) | Gathers 60 recent tickets + 60 recent tasks as LLM context, calls `llm_provider.resolve_ticket_clarification`, then `TicketService.add_comment` to persist the result |

### 3. `agents_api.py` in depth — does it duplicate `AgentManager` logic?

**Dispatch and real termination are correctly delegated.** `create_agent_for_task_endpoint` (`:528-573`) fetches the `Task`, builds an `enriched_data` dict, and calls `await server_state.agent_manager.create_agent_for_task(...)` directly. `terminate_agent_endpoint` (`:576-645`) calls `await server_state.agent_manager.terminate_agent(agent_id)` — the real primitive, not a raw field flip.

#### 3.1 Termination-invariant audit for this file

There is **no raw `agent.status = "terminated"` write anywhere in `agents_api.py`** — both matches for the literal string (`:307`, `:594`) are reads, not assignments. So this file adds no new violation to the 19-site audit table. One coupling worth flagging as its own fragile spot: `terminate_agent_endpoint` reads `task = session.query(Task).filter_by(id=agent.current_task_id).first()` (`:600-601`) **before** calling `terminate_agent()`, then after the await returns, writes `task.status = "failed"` (`:606-609`) onto that same pre-fetched ORM instance. `terminate_agent`'s own safety net independently resets any task still pointing at the agent back to `pending` as part of the same call. The ordering here happens to be correct — the route's `failed` write runs after and overwrites the primitive's `pending` write — but it relies on `task` being the same live-tracked object across an `await` boundary that itself opened and closed a separate DB session inside `AgentManager.terminate_agent`. Given `expire_on_commit=False`/`StaticPool` and the documented stale-cached-object hazard this exact pattern already caused elsewhere in the codebase, this is a candidate for the same class of bug if session-object lifetime assumptions ever change.

#### 3.2 Dead-route collision with `src/mcp/api.py`

`src/mcp/api.py`'s router (`prefix="/api"`) independently defines `GET /agents` (`api.py:2818-2820` → `FrontendAPI.get_agents`, `:317`) and `GET /agents/{agent_id}/output` (`api.py:2823-2826` → `FrontendAPI.get_agent_output`, `:431`). Under that router's `/api` prefix these resolve to the **exact same paths** as `agents_api.py`'s `GET /api/agents` and `GET /api/agents/{agent_id}/output`.

`agents_router` is registered at module level (`server.py:147`); `api_router` is registered later, inside `startup_event` (`server.py:849`). FastAPI matches routes in registration order and stops at the first match — so `api.py`'s two identically-pathed handlers are **unreachable dead code** in a running server. They differ substantively from the versions that actually run: `FrontendAPI.get_agents` has no pagination and no `status` filter; `FrontendAPI.get_agent_output`'s line cap defaults to 2000 vs. `agents_api.py`'s terminated-agent-aware 30000 cap. Neither is a correctness bug today (the dead copies simply never run), but it's a second, silently-abandoned implementation of the same two endpoints.

#### 3.3 Inconsistent auth guarding within the same file

`list_agents` and `get_agent` both call `_require_localhost(request)`. `create_agent_for_task_endpoint`, `terminate_agent_endpoint`, `get_agent_status`, and `get_task_progress` — three of which mutate state — call no such guard at all. `get_agent_status`/`get_task_progress` accept a `requesting_agent_id: str = Header(None, alias="X-Agent-ID")` parameter that is never read anywhere in either function body — dead parameter, no authorization actually happens on either route.

### 4. `tickets_api.py` in depth — delegation to `TicketService`

**Every mutating route in this file delegates to `TicketService`.** A grep for raw `ticket.status =` / `Ticket.status =` assignments in `tickets_api.py` returns zero matches — status transitions, blocking checks, and audit-trail writes all happen inside `TicketService`, and the route layer never bypasses `TicketHistoryService.record_change` or the `blocked_by_ticket_ids` check by writing the column directly. This is a structurally cleaner API layer than `agents_api.py`/`autopilot_api.py`.

#### 4.1 One read-only exception

`get_ticket_stats_endpoint` (`:648-847`) does not call `TicketService` at all — it runs its own `func.count`/`group_by` aggregation queries directly against `Ticket`/`TicketComment`/`TicketCommit`/`BoardConfig`. Since these are pure reads, this doesn't violate the audit-trail concern that applies to writes, but it is a second, independent place that knows the schema shape alongside `TicketService` and `TicketSearchService`.

#### 4.2 Human-approval HTTP surface — confirmed present and correctly wired

`POST /api/tickets/approve` (`:1137-1192`) and `POST /api/tickets/reject` (`:1195-1258`) both call `TicketService.approve_ticket`/`.reject_ticket`, which — after committing `approval_decided_by`/`rejection_reason` to the DB — call `approval_manager.record_decision(ticket_id, approved=..., reason=...)`. `record_decision` (`ticket_service.py:93-112`) looks up the pending `asyncio.Event` for that `ticket_id` and calls `event.set()`, correctly resolving the coroutine blocked inside `wait_for_approval`. This is a fully-correct, fully-delegated HTTP surface for the approval workflow. `GET /api/tickets/pending-review-count` is the read-side companion.

#### 4.3 `get_commit_diff_endpoint` — the one real code-smell in this file

`GET /api/tickets/commit-diff/{commit_sha}` (`:1261-1406`) is the largest single handler in the file (~145 lines) and does not delegate to any service. It resolves the correct repo via `_resolve_repo_path_for_commit`, then shells out to `git show`, `git diff --numstat`, and one `git diff` **per changed file** via three separate `subprocess.run` call sites to build a full unified-diff response. This is presentation logic that has no obvious home in `TicketService`, but it's a route doing its own shell-out orchestration with per-file subprocess fan-out that scales linearly with commit size and no cap on file count.

### 5. `messaging_api.py` in depth

All three routes correctly delegate: `broadcast_message` → `AgentManager.broadcast_message_to_all_agents`; `send_message` → `AgentManager.send_direct_message`; `request_ticket_clarification_endpoint` is not a messaging-delivery route at all despite living in this file — it's an LLM-arbitration endpoint that persists its output as a `TicketService.add_comment` call, grouped here only because the module docstring says "broadcast, direct message, ticket clarification," though it shares no code path with the other two routes.

Neither `broadcast_message` nor `send_message` touches tmux directly — both are genuinely thin routes over the audited `AgentManager` primitives, which delegate to `AgentMessenger.send_message_to_agent` for the actual `send-keys` mechanics.

**However**, `agents_api.py`'s own `/api/agents/{agent_id}/message` route and the six `/children/*` routes are a *second*, independently-written messaging surface that does **not** delegate the same way — see §6.

### 6. Blocking calls in async handlers (forbidden-pattern audit)

1. **`get_commit_diff_endpoint`** (`tickets_api.py:1261`, `async def`) calls `subprocess.run(...)` three times directly on the event loop — `git show` (no timeout), `git diff --numstat` (no timeout), `git diff` per changed file inside a loop (no timeout). None of the three has a `timeout=` argument at all (unlike `agent_communication.py`'s tmux calls, which all set `timeout=5`), so a slow/hung `git` process blocks the event loop indefinitely.

2. **The parent-child agent-messaging surface reimplements tmux delivery outside `AgentMessenger`, and is called blocking.** `agents_api.py`'s six `/children/*` routes (all `async def`) call into `AgentCommunicationService` (`src/services/agent_communication.py`), whose methods are **plain synchronous functions**:
   - `get_child_logs` (`agent_communication.py:75-118`) calls `subprocess.run(["tmux", "capture-pane", ...], timeout=5)` directly.
   - `send_message_to_child` (`:120-168`) calls `subprocess.run(["tmux", "send-keys", ...], timeout=5)` directly, building the keystroke list by iterating the message character-by-character — a **third, independent tmux-injection implementation**, distinct from both `AgentMessenger.send_message_to_agent`'s quote-and-escape approach and `AgentManager`'s own delivery path, with its own per-character-argv escaping strategy nobody has cross-checked against the other two.
   - `nudge_child` and `monitor_and_nudge_stuck_children` both funnel into `send_message_to_child`, so `monitor_and_nudge_stuck_children` can issue **N sequential blocking `subprocess.run` calls** synchronously inside a single `async def` route handler with no `run_in_executor`.

No blocking-call issues were found in `agents_api.py`'s own DB-only routes or in `messaging_api.py`.

### 7. Known fragile spots / code smells

1. **`agents_api.py`'s `GET /api/agents` and `GET /api/agents/{agent_id}/output` shadow identically-pathed, functionally-different dead routes in `src/mcp/api.py`** — registration order makes `api.py`'s versions permanently unreachable, a silent duplicate a future edit would have zero effect on.
2. **Three independent tmux-message-delivery implementations exist in the codebase**: `AgentMessenger.send_message_to_agent` (the audited primitive), and `AgentCommunicationService`'s `get_child_logs`/`send_message_to_child` (raw `subprocess.run(["tmux", ...])`, no shared escaping helper, no async wrapper). The parent-child messaging surface bypasses the audited path entirely.
3. **Inconsistent localhost/auth guarding within `agents_api.py`**: read routes call `_require_localhost`; the two state-mutating lifecycle routes and the two bare-path status routes do not. The latter two also accept but never use a `requesting_agent_id` header.
4. **`get_commit_diff_endpoint`'s subprocess calls have no timeout**, unlike every other subprocess call site surveyed across this file set — this one can hang indefinitely.
5. **`tickets_api.py`'s `get_ticket_stats_endpoint` bypasses `TicketService`/`TicketSearchService` for its own inline aggregation queries** — a third place that has to be kept in sync with the schema.
6. **`request_ticket_clarification_endpoint` lives in `messaging_api.py` but shares no code or purpose with the file's other two routes**.
7. **`get_tickets_endpoint`'s `project_id` branch is an N+1 query pattern**: fetches every `Workflow` for the project, then calls `TicketService.get_tickets_by_workflow` once per workflow in a Python loop rather than a single batched query.

### 8. Refactor observations

1. **Delete `FrontendAPI.get_agents`/`get_agent_output` in `api.py`** (or move `agents_api.py`'s routes to run inside `startup_event` and delete the dead copies deliberately) — a direct, concrete instance of the "dead code accumulates without removal" cross-cutting theme.
2. **Route `AgentCommunicationService`'s tmux calls through `AgentMessenger`/`AgentManager.send_message_to_agent`** instead of a third hand-rolled implementation — this closes the escaping-strategy divergence and gets the parent-child messaging path the same `_pane_is_wedged` stuck-shell detection the audited primitive has.
3. **Wrap `get_commit_diff_endpoint`'s and `AgentCommunicationService`'s blocking calls in `run_in_executor`**, matching the pattern `autopilot_api.py`'s `repair_design` already demonstrates correctly.
4. **Add explicit timeouts to `get_commit_diff_endpoint`'s three `subprocess.run` calls** before wrapping them in an executor.
5. **Normalize auth guarding across `agents_api.py`**: either every route calls `_require_localhost`, or the omission on the two mutating routes and two bare-path routes is a deliberate, documented exception.
6. **Move `request_ticket_clarification_endpoint` into `tickets_api.py`**, or rename `messaging_api.py`'s scope to reflect that it also owns LLM-arbitration-triggered comment writes.

## Bug-Fix History: A Second, Independent Pass

Everything above this point was built by reading the *current* code and citing specific commits as evidence for why it looks the way it does. The five sections below invert that method: they start from the commit history itself — a systematic clustering of the 591 `fix:` commits that have touched Autopilot-relevant paths since this repo's first commit (2025-10-30) — and mine the actual diffs behind each cluster's most illustrative commits, independent of what the file-structure sections above already concluded.

**Why this is a separate pass, not a rewrite of the sections above.** Reading current code answers "what does this look like today, and what does the surrounding evidence suggest about why." Reading the historical diffs answers a different question: "exactly how many times was this specific bug independently rediscovered, by which commits, and what was the precise fix technique each time." The two methods agree far more than they disagree — where they do disagree, or where one surfaces something the other missed entirely, it's called out explicitly in each section's "Doc comparison" subsections rather than silently reconciled.

**Method.** The 591 `fix:` commits touching `src/autopilot`, `src/agents`, `src/phases`, `src/monitoring`, `src/services`, `src/workflow`, `src/mcp`, `src/core/worktree_manager.py`, `src/interfaces/cli_interface.py`, `src/validation`, `src/memory`, and `config/workflows` were clustered by keyword into 8 thematic buckets (worktree/git, task-creation-claim/races, agent termination/restart, agent session/tmux/timeout, dispatch/prompt/launch, goto/retry/arbitration, status/completion-inference & phase-output-routing, services/tickets/queue/cost — sizes ranging 34-152 commits each). All eight clusters have been analyzed as of this revision. For each cluster, roughly 25-45% of its commits were read via `git show` (favoring information-dense subject lines, spread across the full time range rather than clustered at the most recent), and every claim below is backed by at least one diff actually read, not inferred from a commit subject line alone. One cluster (status/completion-inference) went further than the others at this pass's own request: it traced `status_derivation.py`'s caller history specifically, pulling in four commits (`bedfba2`, `2de15ad`, `1493c47`, `22178b1`) outside its assigned bucket to answer a question the file-structure pass's spot-check had left open — see that section's §A5.

**How to read "Doc comparison."** Each sub-pattern below ends with an explicit note on whether the finding is already captured in the file-structure sections above (in which case the historical trace usually *sharpens* it — e.g. correcting a fix-commit count, or revealing that a "duplicated logic" finding was actually the same specific bug independently rediscovered three times, not just copy-pasted code) or whether it's new material the current-code read didn't surface at all (a plurality of the findings below fall in this category — CLI-integration timing races, MCP tool-name registry drift, and several detector-signal-corruption bugs in particular were invisible to a current-code-only read since the code today looks correct; only the commit history shows how many tries it took to get there and what almost went wrong along the way).


## Bug-Fix History: Worktree, Git & Merge Patterns

*Derived from a fresh read of `git show` on 27 of the 57 `fix:` commits in this theme (2026-06-19 through 2026-08-14), independent of the file-structure analysis above. Cross-referenced against this document's own Worktree Management section and the `AUTOPILOT_REFACTOR_PLAN.md` companion document's §3.1/§4.4.*

### 1. Worktree deleted on a non-terminal exit path, destroying resumable state

**Problem.** Cleanup calls (`_cleanup_worktree`) kept getting placed in `finally:` blocks or unconditional post-work steps that fire on *every* exit — success, failure, pause, interrupt, timeout, or an early "already completed" return — not just genuine completion. Since `paused`/`interrupted`/`timeout`/`failed` are all resumable via `existing_workflow_id`, which re-uses that exact worktree path, an unconditional delete destroys the very state the next resume attempt needs (git-excluded `.hephaestus/` state like `features.json`/`scope.md`, or uncommitted work). This bug recurred in at least three separate call sites over a month: `_run_one_feature`'s shared-worktree finally block (`3a6323c`, 2026-07-14), `run_phase0`'s finally block (`a954d10`, 2026-08-08 — nearly a month later, same shape of bug in a sibling function), and an early-return "already completed" path in `_run_one_feature` that skipped cleanup entirely in the opposite direction (`748255f`).

**Proper fix.** Track success with a local boolean set only on the genuine-completion return, and gate the `finally:` block's cleanup call on that flag — not on how the function exited. `a954d10`:
```python
phase0_succeeded = False
try:
    ...
    phase0_succeeded = True
    return features_json, designs_folder
finally:
    if phase0_succeeded:
        _cleanup_worktree(worktree, branch, project_path, logger)
```
For the inverse gap (early-return skipping cleanup), `748255f` computes the deterministic branch/worktree path and calls `_cleanup_worktree` explicitly before the early return rather than relying on a shared code path to reach it. `50c8bf7` adds a third layer — `sweep_completed_workflow_worktrees()`, run once at server startup — specifically for the case a backend restart lands in the narrow window between "workflow reached `completed`" and "the inline cleanup call after it actually runs," since nothing else ever revisits a completed workflow.

**Commits:** `3a6323c`, `a954d10`, `748255f`, `50c8bf7`.

**Doc comparison.** The Worktree Management section above names `_cleanup_worktree`'s six call sites and flags that it bypasses `_remove_worktree`'s safety guard, but doesn't discuss *when* those call sites fire relative to workflow outcome. This sub-pattern is additive: the "four independent removal implementations" framing is about *which function* does the deleting; this is about *whether it should be called at all* for a given exit — a distinct, recurring bug class the same consolidation work (Plan §4.4) should also guard against with a test.

### 2. Uncommitted work destroyed by an unguarded force-delete

**Problem.** `Workflow.status` was treated as sufficient proof a worktree had nothing left to lose. It isn't: a workflow can be marked `failed` by an unrelated self-heal (e.g. an "abandoned: no activity" sweep firing because the *backend* crashed and stopped recording activity, not because the agent stopped working) while an agent's real, uncommitted fixes still sit in that worktree. Observed live: a `security_review` agent's uncommitted report and source fixes were permanently destroyed when a crash-induced false "abandoned" marking let the generic stale-worktree sweep delete the worktree out from under it.

**Proper fix.** `5d59c5c` adds a `require_clean: bool = True` parameter to `_remove_worktree` — the one function every removal path funnels through. Before removing, it opens the worktree as its own `Repo` and checks `is_dirty(untracked_files=True)`; on any uncommitted/staged/untracked content (or if the check itself fails, e.g. a corrupted repo state), it refuses and logs rather than falling through to a force-delete. The two call sites with genuine discard semantics — clearing a path about to be overwritten by a fresh `git worktree add`, and `discard_agent`/`cleanup_branch`'s explicit "failed work never touches main" — pass `require_clean=False` explicitly. The generic staleness sweep (`cleanup_all_stale_branches`) keeps the default `True`, since it's a heuristic sweep, not an intentional discard.

**Commits:** `5d59c5c`.

**Doc comparison.** Already fully captured — this is exactly the guard the Worktree Management section describes (`_remove_worktree`'s `require_clean` guard, and the incident it closed), and the exact guard `AUTOPILOT_REFACTOR_PLAN.md`'s Phase 1 Exception 1 says `_cleanup_worktree` (orchestrator.py's separate reimplementation) still bypasses via a raw `git worktree remove --force` call. This commit is the origin of that now-load-bearing guard; the Plan's work is closing the remaining bypass, not re-adding the guard itself.

### 3. `WorktreeManager` silently operating on the wrong project

**Problem.** `WorktreeManager` wraps one `main_repo` `Repo` handle at a time; it only points at a given project after an explicit `.reload(path)` call. Once multi-project support landed, every place that constructed or reused a `WorktreeManager` without reloading it first silently fell back to whatever `config.main_repo_path` happened to be — the global default project, not the one actually being worked on. This recurred in at least four separate call sites, roughly monthly: `AgentManager.create_agent_for_task` never reloading before creating a worktree (`cbad16a`, 2026-06-23), the shared design-worktree path in `orchestrator.py` defaulting to `config.main_repo_path` instead of `project_path` (`8c4c9db`, 2026-06-24, with a same-day companion fix `63e1bd7` flattening branch names for worktree paths and fixing shared-worktree *detection*), `create_agent_branch` requiring an explicit reload rather than detecting drift itself (`050c588`, 2026-07-07), and — the most consequential recurrence — two `WorktreeManager` construction sites in `autopilot_api.py` (`/queue/rerun`'s background cleanup and `/cleanup-branches`) that were *never* reloaded at all, root-causing roughly 25 stale worktrees silently accumulating on disk (`50c8bf7`, 2026-07-28).

**Proper fix.** Two complementary techniques emerged: (a) explicit reload-before-use at every call site that constructs or receives a `WorktreeManager` for a specific project (`cbad16a`'s `self.branch_manager.reload(Path(wf.working_directory))` before worktree creation; `50c8bf7`'s `bm.reload(project)` added to the two missed API call sites), and (b) a defensive lazy-reload fallback inside `WorktreeManager` itself for the case a caller forgets: `050c588`
```python
config_path = Path(self.config.main_repo_path)
if self.main_repo.working_dir != str(config_path):
    self.reload(config_path)
```
placed at the top of `create_agent_branch`. Neither technique alone fully closed the gap — the lazy fallback only compares against the *global* config default, not per-workflow project paths, which is why `50c8bf7`'s explicit-reload fix was still needed a month later for the two API sites the fallback didn't cover.

**Commits:** `cbad16a`, `8c4c9db`, `63e1bd7`, `050c588`, `50c8bf7`.

**Doc comparison.** Not covered by the file-structure analysis above — that section documents `.reload()`'s current behavior ("instance-local only... to prevent two `WorktreeManager` instances... from clobbering each other's `worktree_base`") as a design property, not as a historically bug-prone contract. This is a sharper, additive finding: the instance-local design is *correct* for concurrent-project safety, but its corollary — every call site is individually responsible for reloading before use, with no enforcement — has independently caused the same class of bug four times over five weeks. Worth adding to the Refactor Observations as a candidate for a "get-or-reload" accessor scoped by project path, so a missed reload fails loudly (wrong repo, caught immediately) rather than silently degrading to the global default.

### 4. `Workflow.working_directory` going stale/null, and self-heal recovery repeatedly picking the wrong signal

**Problem.** The DB-persisted `Workflow.working_directory` field is the single source of truth every phase uses to find its shared worktree, but three independent processes can invalidate it without updating the field: the periodic reaper (`cleanup_all_stale_branches`, independent of the per-workflow `_cleanup_worktree` completion flow), a direct `_cleanup_worktree` call, and a worktree simply never having been recorded due to a creation-time race. Once stale, downstream code either crashed (`Repo()` on a nonexistent directory — `2a9adbd`) or silently corrupted state by falling back to the wrong path. The self-heal built to recover from this had its own bug: `05eef62` (2026-07-27) added recovery-from-`AgentWorktree`, but it looked up the *currently completing task's own agent* — and since an `AgentWorktree` row only exists for an agent that fell back to an isolated worktree in the first place, recovering from "whoever finishes next" meant the recovered path drifted to a different, disconnected worktree every time `working_directory` went empty again. `61b0bfe` (2026-08-08, same day as pattern #1's `a954d10`) fixed this by scoping the query to the workflow's *earliest* `AgentWorktree` record instead of the completing task's agent.

**Proper fix.** Layered, in the order the bugs were found: (1) actively null the field the moment a worktree is actually removed, from both cleanup paths — `0b817a4` for `_cleanup_worktree`, `00e7b34` for the reaper's independent sweep, the latter using `.git` presence rather than bare directory existence as the liveness check (something kept re-appending tmux transcripts to a removed worktree's `.hephaestus/tmux/` path, resurrecting an empty stub directory that a naive `exists()` check would treat as still-live); (2) defensively check existence before dereferencing a stored path (`2a9adbd`); (3) when the field is missing outright, recover it — but scope the recovery query correctly:
```python
# 61b0bfe — scoped to the workflow's EARLIEST AgentWorktree, not the
# completing task's own agent
wt_record = (
    session.query(AgentWorktree)
    .join(Task, Task.assigned_agent_id == AgentWorktree.agent_id)
    .filter(Task.workflow_id == task.workflow_id)
    .order_by(AgentWorktree.created_at.asc())
    .first()
)
```

**Commits:** `0b817a4`, `00e7b34`, `2a9adbd`, `05eef62`, `61b0bfe`.

**Doc comparison.** Partially covered. The Worktree Management section's "Broad self-heal sweep" paragraph documents the `.git`-vs-directory-existence liveness signal from `00e7b34`, but the recovery-drift bug (`05eef62` → `61b0bfe`) is not mentioned. It's a good concrete illustration of that section's own broader recommendation (give the shared-worktree model a real API) — this recovery logic exists at all only because there's no single authoritative "what is this workflow's worktree" accessor; a `WorktreeManager.get_shared_worktree(workflow_id)` primitive would make "which AgentWorktree counts" a one-time design decision instead of a recovery heuristic that needed a second pass to get right.

### 5. Merge-state corruption and conflict-recovery hardening (early stabilization)

**Problem.** In the earliest days of the shared-worktree merge path, `attempt_recovery`'s error-handling did raw branch merging directly against the main repo's working tree; a failed attempt left the repo in an unresolved-merge state, and every *subsequent* merge attempt then hit `fatal: unresolved conflict` (exit 128) because nothing ever cleared that leftover state first. This was diagnosed from a smoke run with 186/186 merge failures — a single unrecovered bad merge state cascading into total merge failure for the rest of the run, which in turn caused the spec gate to never score (result files stuck in worktrees, never reaching `main`), reports never landing in project docs, and worktrees never getting cleaned up (since cleanup only fires post-merge-success in some paths).

**Proper fix.** `a179c05` (2026-06-20) removed `attempt_recovery`'s raw merge entirely — branch merging belongs solely to `WorktreeManager.merge_to_main`, so recovery only cleans repo *state* (`git merge --abort`, then `git reset --hard` / `git clean -fd`, but only if the repo is actually dirty) and never re-does the merge itself. `merge_to_main` itself was hardened to always clear stale conflict state before attempting a fresh merge, and to recognize `"unresolved conflict"` as a conflict signal alongside the previously-handled `"CONFLICT"` string. The same week, `ec8c316`/`414cc23` (2026-06-19) removed a second, independent raw-git fallback in the repair endpoint (routing it through `WorktreeManager.merge_to_main`/`cleanup_branch` exclusively, "no fallbacks, log errors") and standardized the isolated-agent branch prefix from `autopilot-` to `agent-` across the codebase, including the repair endpoint's own branch-matching filter.

**Commits:** `a179c05`, `ec8c316`, `414cc23`.

**Doc comparison.** Confirms and dates a finding already in the doc, with a sharper edge. The Worktree Management section's "verified gap" flags `cleanup_all_stale_branches`' branch-prefix filter as checking `"autopilot-"`, which "nothing in the codebase produces... ever" — `414cc23` is the commit that renamed the prefix away from `autopilot-` two months before that section was written, and its rename evidently never propagated to that later-added filter. That's a second, independent confirmation (from commit history rather than static code reading) that the "single source of truth for branch-naming conventions" recommendation is addressing real, repeatedly-observed drift, not a hypothetical.

### 6. `git_commit_push` idempotency and completion-path parity

**Problem.** `git_commit_push` is architecturally special — it's the one phase whose real "done" signal is external git state (a merged/pushed branch), not a file artifact `verify_output_artifact` can check (`spec.py` lists it in `OPTIONAL_PHASES`, no required output). This caused two distinct classes of bug. First, the phase's own workflow prompt originally routed the actual "land the work" step through an optional GitHub PR (`gh pr create && gh pr merge`), falling back to a local merge only if `gh` was unavailable or there was no remote — meaning the primary path for landing work depended on a CLI tool and network state that might not be present (`fb17e56`, 2026-07-26). Second, once an agent's git work *did* land, nothing else in the system could tell — an orphaned agent (dead tmux session, e.g. its final `complete_my_task` call lost to a connection drop during a backend restart) got blindly redispatched by `_resume_interrupted_workflows` to redo the entire commit+merge+push sequence from scratch, and the human-operator manual-completion path (`complete_task_as_user`) skipped the commit-and-verify step that the normal automated path always runs, letting a human mark a task done while the agent's actual code changes sat uncommitted in the worktree.

**Proper fix.** `fb17e56` rewrote the phase prompt so local merge-to-main is always the primary, required path; `gh pr create` becomes optional, non-blocking visibility only (`|| true`). `1de8933` added `_git_commit_push_already_landed(session, task, config)`, checking git state directly rather than trusting the missing completion signal:
```python
result = subprocess.run(
    ["git", "merge-base", "--is-ancestor", branch, config.base_branch],
    cwd=base_repo, capture_output=True, timeout=10,
)
return result.returncode == 0
```
wired into the orphan-resume loop so a task whose branch is already an ancestor of `main` gets marked done instead of redispatched — deliberately scoped to `git_commit_push` only, since every other phase has a real file artifact to check instead. `ad54049` (2026-08-14, the newest commit in the bucket) closed the human-operator gap by mirroring the automated path's `commit_and_link_ticket`/`verify_output_survived_commit` sequence inside `complete_task_as_user`, skipping only for `git_commit_push` itself since the operator has already committed externally by the time they call that endpoint.

**Commits:** `fb17e56`, `1de8933`, `ad54049`.

**Doc comparison.** Complements, doesn't duplicate, the Workflow Termination & API Surface section's "git_commit_push human-approval saga" (the `manual_only` YAML-flag thrash). That saga is entirely about *whether* the pipeline is permitted to dispatch this phase autonomously (a policy/gating question). This sub-pattern is about a different axis entirely: *given* dispatch happened, how the system detects success when the phase's own completion signal is unreliable by construction. Both are real, independent problem areas inside the same phase; worth a cross-reference so a reader doesn't conflate "the approval-gating thrash is git_commit_push's whole story."

### 7. Worktree retention after merge, for UI and artifact access

**Problem.** Once `git_commit_push` merges and (at the time) removed the worktree, everything else that expected to keep reading from it broke: the UI's FeatureGallery/FeatureDetailModal couldn't reference committed `docs/` output or git-excluded `.hephaestus/tmux/` logs through the stable worktree path stored on the workflow record; per-phase tmux transcripts were lost wholesale except the final phase's own log (written *after* removal); a complexity classifier fell back to a verbose task prompt instead of the actual design doc because the shared worktree has no `.hephaestus/design.md`; and a `/feature-records/{id}/report` endpoint 404'd once `_cleanup_worktree` ran because it only ever checked the live worktree and `docs/` directory.

**Proper fix.** The load-bearing fix was inverting the ordering assumption: stop removing the worktree as part of the merge step at all (`1aae1f3`, 2026-06-26) — `git_commit_push`'s prompt was rewritten for "worktree reality" (the agent is already on the feature branch mid-worktree and must not `git worktree remove` it; `--delete-branch` in the PR-merge flow removes only the *remote* branch), and `phase_manager._populate_feature_folder` was changed to read `docs/` artifacts and tmux logs from the worktree path stored on the workflow record. `0ea0d6c` went further, making tmux-log persistence independent of the worktree's lifetime entirely by resolving the *project root* from the worktree path at write time and writing all phase logs to `<project_root>/.hephaestus/tmux/`, which nothing ever deletes — so logs survive even the later, correctly-scoped worktree removal that `_cleanup_worktree` does perform once the workflow is truly done. `b9cd842` fixed a sibling gap in the same area: a complexity classifier reading from a `.hephaestus/design.md` path that doesn't exist in the shared-worktree model, fixed by sourcing from an explicit priority list (`docs/design-queue/*.md` → `.hephaestus/design.md` → phase-1 `docs/requirements_analysis.md` → task text) instead of one hardcoded path. `6f5f1d7` (2026-08-10, the most recent in this group) closed the remaining gap for data that *is* correctly cleaned up post-completion: the report endpoint now falls back to `_find_archived_feature_report`, which locates the copy `PhaseManager` archives *before* cleanup runs. `3470d5e` is the interesting counter-example: an earlier attempt to solve "find the result file" by iterating worktrees on every read call was reverted the same day it was diagnosed as unacceptable per-turn latency (the monitoring cycle calls these functions many times), once the merge-hardening fix in pattern #5 made merges reliably land files in `<project>/docs/` and removed the need for the fallback.

**Commits:** `1aae1f3`, `0ea0d6c`, `b9cd842`, `6f5f1d7`, `3470d5e`.

**Doc comparison.** Not covered above — both existing docs focus on worktree *creation/removal/merge* mechanics, not on the consumption side (UI, monitoring, classifiers) that depends on worktree contents outliving the worktree's own lifecycle. Worth adding as a Refactor Observation: the shared/integration-worktree model's actual contract is "outlives the merge, gets archived/reaped later" — which is implicit in five separate fixes across six weeks rather than documented anywhere as a designed property. The proposed `create_shared_worktree`/`merge_shared_branch` API should make this retention window an explicit, tested part of the primitive's contract rather than something every new consumer (UI, classifier, report endpoint) has to independently discover the hard way.

### 8. `git mv` doesn't respect `.gitignore` for the destination

**Problem.** The `doc_review` phase's cleanup step sweeps stray/stale report files from git-tracked `docs/` into the git-excluded `.hephaestus/` artifacts directory. `2a5f749` (2026-07-30, 12:31) first fixed this sweep from a plain filesystem move (which left the `docs/` deletion unstaged, so it could reappear on a fresh checkout since nothing recorded it was gone) to `git mv <docs path> <artifacts path>`. Four minutes later, `7b94f4a` (12:35, same day) discovered that fix itself was wrong: `git mv` on a tracked source stages the *destination* as tracked too, regardless of `.gitignore` — verified directly against real git behavior (`git status`/`git ls-files` after the move), not assumed.
