The output-routing half (Part B) shows a structurally similar pattern one layer down: no canonical "resolve this phase's declared output path" or "validate this phase's frontmatter type" function exists, so both application code (B1) and the YAML prompts that tell agents where to write (B2, B3) drift from each other independently, discovered only when a human or forensics-style audit notices a dead read. B4 and B5 show the two halves compounding: a status-forcing code path (`_cap_out_review_phase`) that also has to get an output schema right, and gate-scorers whose schema strictness has directly caused runaway goto/retry-budget loops (`21fa097`'s 162 gotos) — i.e., an output-routing/parsing bug becoming a completion-inference bug in the same incident. None of this theme's fixes attempt a structural fix (a single output-path resolver, a single frontmatter-schema validator shared by all prompts and all scorers); every fix found here is scoped to the one call site or one phase YAML where the specific incident was observed.

---

## Bug-Fix History: Services, Tickets, Queue & Cost Patterns

*Derived from a fresh read of the actual commit diffs behind 34 `fix:` commits in this theme (services layer: tickets, task queue, result/output validation, cost tracking), independent of the file-structure analysis above.*

### 1. Declared-output validation: chasing agents around the filesystem

**Problem.** `TaskCompletionService.verify_output_artifact` and `src/autopilot/spec.py`'s gate-result readers enforce a hard floor — a phase's declared output file must exist before its task can be marked done, and a gated phase's spec-gate JSON must be found before scoring. Both checks assume agents write to one canonical location with one canonical filename. In practice agents wrote to whichever plausible-looking path/name they inferred from context, and every mismatch produced the same false symptom: a genuinely-completed task rejected as "missing from worktree," or a genuine gate result invisible to the scorer, triggering a spurious goto loop.

**Proper fix, by mismatch type — six commits over one month, converging on the same "search more places" shape:**
- **Wrong subdirectory.** `5616785` (2026-08-03) added `docs/` as a fallback root when agents wrote there instead of `.hephaestus/`. `dcb5b48` (2026-07-15) went further in `spec.py`'s `read_result`/`read_report_text`: agents writing to a *feature-specific* subdirectory of `docs/` (e.g. `docs/llm_provider_abstraction/`) rather than `docs/` root were invisible to a flat existence check, fixed by iterating `docs/`'s immediate subdirectories when the direct path misses.
- **Wrong filename, old vs. new.** `2078d4d` (2026-08-03) is the sharpest of the six: `task_completion_service.py` had been through a file-renaming pass (`forensics_report.md` → `forensics.md`, `security_report.md` → `security.md`, etc.), and validation only ever checked the *new* name. Files written under the old convention — either by an agent still using stale prompt text, or created before the rename — read as "missing" even though they existed. Fix is a `_old_name_map` dict and a loop that tries every candidate path for both the new and mapped-old filename before giving up. Notably this required *widening a loop that already had four candidate path templates* into a nested loop over name × path — the fix compounds on top of prior "search more places" fixes rather than replacing them.
- **Wrong required-output granularity.** `c552fa4` and `6072b49` (both 2026-07-15, 2 minutes apart in the log) are config-only fixes: gated phases (`architectural_review`, `adversarial_review`, `feature_review`) had `required_output` pointing only at the human-readable `.md` report, so a task could reach `done` having written the report but never the structured `.json` the spec gate actually scores against. `c552fa4` repointed `required_output` at the `.json` file; `6072b49` then discovered that broke phases needing *both* files, and widened `required_output`'s schema to accept a list, with `get_phase_required_files` handling either a bare string or a list.
- **Downstream consequence of the false positive.** `c679e82` (2026-08-03, same day as `2078d4d`) is the failure mode these gaps produce when *not* caught here: the monitor's stuck-task-recovery path saw a gated-phase task with `completion_notes` set and promoted it straight to `done`, bypassing gate validation entirely — meaning an unvalidated result could ride the "the file must be somewhere, right?" ambiguity all the way into the pipeline. Fixed by making that recovery path check whether the phase is gated and, if so, fail (not promote) so retry re-enters through the proper gate.

**Relation to file-structure analysis:** confirmed and sharpened. The Result/Ticket/Validation Services section's §2 already names Pipeline A (`verify_output_artifact`) as "the output-existence hard floor" and cites `af99e8d`'s worktree-selection fix as its one illustrated failure mode. This trace adds a second, distinct failure family the doc doesn't cover: it isn't just "which worktree," it's "which path *within* the worktree, under which *filename*" — five separate commits, each catching one more shape of the same underlying problem (agents don't reliably follow the declared-output convention), with no single fix ever closing the class. The `2078d4d` nested name×path loop is a concrete argument for a config-driven declared-output *resolver* (canonical name + accepted aliases + accepted search roots, spelled out once per phase in YAML) rather than an ever-growing Python search loop.

### 2. Ticket-creation friction: schema coercion, fatal-by-default, and silent prerequisites

**Problem.** `enable_tickets: true` workflows make `ticket_id` a hard MCP-call precondition, and `ticket_type` a closed enum validated server-side. Every commit in this cluster is a variant of "an agent's honest, plausible call got rejected (or crashed the backend) for a reason it had no way to anticipate."

**Proper fix, by failure mode:**
- **Type coercion the schema didn't expect.** `ade54db` (2026-07-08): agents sent `phase_id` as a JSON int (`10`); the Pydantic schema declared `str`, so every such call 400'd. Fix: accept `Union[str, int]`, coerce to string on store — the schema now matches what agents actually send instead of what the OpenAPI type hint idealized.
- **Hard 400 for a soft problem.** `4e279c7` (2026-06-24): an unrecognized `ticket_type` (not in the board's configured allow-list) raised `HTTPException(400)`, which an agent has no graceful way to route around mid-task. Fix: catch the `ValueError` and return a `status="skipped"` response body describing the problem instead of raising — the agent can read the warning and continue without a ticket rather than the whole tool call erroring out. `0a3ee96` (2026-06-27) is the companion config-side fix: the *actual* type mismatch causing these 500s was that `forensics` agents legitimately use `bug`/`improvement`/`task`/`spike`, none of which were in the board's configured `ticket_types` list (`[infrastructure, feature, bug-fix, security, integration, documentation]`) — the runtime leniency fix and the config-completeness fix are two independent layers of the same underlying mismatch.
- **Undocumented precondition.** `d715120` (2026-07-05): `hephaestus_create_task` unconditionally rejects any call missing `ticket_id` once `enable_tickets` is on, but nothing in the agent's system prompt said so — every agent creating its first subtask discovered the requirement only via a failed call, "wasting a full round trip every single time" (commit's own words, confirmed via smoke testing). Fix: `format_initial_message` now checks `workflow_config.enable_tickets` and appends an explicit instruction to create a ticket first. `9d6bb78` (2026-08-04) closes a related gap one month later: *phase* agents (not just SDK agents) creating same-workflow, same-phase tasks were still being forced through the ticket_id requirement even though they, too, are exempt in intent — the exemption list had only ever been updated for one of the two caller categories.
- **Tooling trap, not a logic bug.** `54ab5d9` (2026-07-20) is worth calling out as a distinct failure mode from the other four: a plain `@validator` (Pydantic v1-style) isn't in `ruff`'s N805 classmethod-decorator allowlist, so `ruff --fix` silently rewrote the `cls` parameter to `self` on every autofix pass — which Pydantic 2.13's compatibility shim then rejects outright at import time, taking the whole backend down. Fix is a one-line `@classmethod` annotation that makes ruff recognize the method correctly. This isn't a services-layer logic bug at all, but it's a real instance of "the ticket_id validator kept getting mangled" recurring in this bucket, and worth flagging precisely because it shows a linter autofix silently corrupting decorator semantics — a class of self-inflicted regression the other fixes in this cluster don't share.

**Relation to file-structure analysis:** partially new. The Result/Ticket/Validation Services section documents `ticket_service.py`'s 1772-line state machine and its call graph but doesn't discuss the *agent-facing* friction of the ticket precondition at all — `d715120`/`9d6bb78`/`4e279c7` are all about what happens at the MCP-call boundary before a `TicketService` method is ever reached, a layer the file-structure read has no visibility into because it's prompt/schema plumbing, not the service itself. Worth adding as a named gap: the doc's "Result, Ticket & Validation Services" section should note that ticket creation has an enforcement precondition (`ticket_id` required when `enable_tickets`) that isn't self-documenting to callers and needed three separate patches (prompt warning, exemption-list completeness, non-fatal-on-invalid-type) to become usable in practice.

### 3. Design-queue / pipeline scoping: one project's state leaking into another's

**Problem.** `run_continuous_pipeline`'s design-queue loop and its supporting helpers (`get_active_workflows`, `rerun_design`'s "stop everything" step) were written assuming a single active project, then had multi-project support layered on top without auditing every query for project/design scoping. Each gap manifested the same way: an action meant to affect *this* project's or *this* design's state instead affected every project's or every design's state system-wide — sometimes destructively (terminating live agents).

**Proper fix, escalating in blast radius across four commits:**
- **Whole-project blocking.** `533de2a` (2026-07-13): `state.current_workflow_id` (persisted pipeline state) and `get_active_workflows()` had zero project scoping — switching the active project in the UI and starting the pipeline blocked behind an unrelated project's paused workflow, then, after retry exhaustion, force-marked *that other project's* workflow "failed" as a side effect. Fix adds `_workflow_belongs_to_project()`, preferring the authoritative `Workflow.project_id` FK, falling back to `Path.is_relative_to()` containment on `working_directory` — explicitly not a raw `str.startswith()` prefix match, which the commit message calls out as wrongly matching sibling directories sharing a name prefix (`project-a` vs. `project-ab`). Wired into three call sites, the most consequential being `run_single_workflow`'s `pause_existing` branch, which *terminates* other workflows' agents, not just blocks them.
- **Whole-project blocking, narrower still.** `f54811f` (2026-07-29): even after `533de2a` scoped the gate to the *project*, it was still too coarse — one in-progress design in a project blocked every *other* design in that same project from being picked, including ones with satisfied dependencies ready to run. Observed live: `credit-system` and `project-management` sat idle 30+ minutes because an unrelated `jwt-auth-service` design's chain was still active in the same project. Fix (`_has_resumable_active_design()`) narrows the gate to only fire for genuinely *new* Phase 0 dispatches (which are destructive project-wide via `pause_existing=True`), while letting an already-decomposed design's remaining features resume in parallel.
- **System-wide agent termination.** `9cb947c` (2026-08-13, this theme's most severe instance): `/queue/rerun`'s "stop everything" step queried `Agent.status.in_(["working","starting","idle"])` and `Workflow.status.in_(["active","running"])` with **no scoping at all** — rerunning any one design terminated every active agent and paused every active workflow across every other project and design. Root-caused from a live incident: a healthy `adversarial_review` agent with a complete, correct 5-BLOCKER report was killed mid-review by an unrelated design's rerun, and — because the old code only flipped the `Agent` row, never resetting the `Task` row pointing at it — the task looked indistinguishable from one still genuinely in progress until an unrelated stale-task sweep eventually failed it, discarding the real work. Fix scopes Step 2 to `Workflow.design_id == this design's id` and adds the missing task-reset-before-terminate step (`status="pending"`, `assigned_agent_id=None`) — explicitly citing the same reset-before-kill ordering established elsewhere for agent termination (see the Agent Termination & Restart Patterns section's §2 in this document).
- **Off-by-a-status-set undercounting.** `a1e91dd` (2026-07-20), a smaller sibling bug in the same subsystem: `queue_depth` filtered `AutopilotDesign.status == "pending"` only, so a design actively being decomposed (`status="decomposing"`) or processed (`status="active"`) vanished from the pipeline-status count, showing a misleading "0 of 0 designs processing" while work was genuinely underway. Fixed by inverting to `status.notin_(["completed","failed","skipped"])` — count everything non-terminal, rather than enumerate every in-flight status by name (a smaller instance of the same "the state-machine has more live states than this filter enumerates" mistake as the other three).

**Relation to file-structure analysis:** new — the CLAUDE.md `concurrent-active-projects` invariant and this doc's `AutopilotProject.is_active` discussion cover the *concurrency-cap* half of multi-project correctness, but neither existing document discusses *query scoping* leaks between projects/designs as their own failure class. `9cb947c`'s incident write-up is a near-verbatim illustration of the CLAUDE.md agent-termination invariant being violated for reasons unrelated to termination logic itself — the bug was upstream, in an unscoped `WHERE` clause feeding into an otherwise-correct termination call. Worth naming as its own review item: every query in `orchestrator.py`/`autopilot_api.py` that selects `Agent`/`Workflow`/`Task` rows for a bulk state-changing operation needs an explicit project/design scope audit, since the pattern of "works fine with one active project, leaks with two" repeated at three different severities (block, coarse-block, terminate) before being caught.

### 4. Design-queue visibility: typos and type mismatches silently dropping rows

**Problem.** A smaller, more mechanical cluster: the design-queue UI's "what tasks does this feature have" query and the queue's on-disk state file location both had off-by-one-symbol correctness bugs — not races, just wrong-variable and wrong-type mistakes that each silently produced an empty result instead of erroring, making them hard to notice until a user reported "nothing shows up."

**Representative commits:**
- `417faa3` (2026-07-05): a raw variable-name typo — `feat.workflow_id` used instead of the freshly-resolved `feat_wf_id` fallback variable one line above it. When `feat.workflow_id` was `NULL`, the fallback logic correctly found the real workflow by `feature_key` and stored it in `feat_wf_id`, but the actual DB query still filtered on the still-`NULL` `feat.workflow_id`, returning zero tasks. One-character-class fix (`feat.workflow_id` → `feat_wf_id`), but a full day of "feature tasks not showing" investigation to find it.
- `4f2345a` (2026-07-04, the day before): a related but distinct bug in the same subsystem — `_relink_features_to_workflows()` called `json.loads()` on `launch_params` values that were sometimes already-parsed dicts (not JSON strings), raising a `TypeError` that a broad `except` silently swallowed and skipped. Fix handles both dict and string forms; the commit also had to manually re-link one already-broken smoke-test feature as a data-repair step, since the silent-skip had already let bad state accumulate.
- `d32343a` (2026-06-28): `.queue_order.json` (the queue's persisted reorder state) was being written to `docs/design/` — a git-tracked directory — meaning server runtime state showed up in `git status` and risked being accidentally committed. Fixed by moving all three write sites (`autopilot_api._get_queue_order_path`, the reorder endpoint, `orchestrator.scan_design_queue`) to resolve under `.hephaestus/` instead, consistent with this project's documented `design-storage` invariant for keeping runtime artifacts out of tracked paths.

**Proper fix technique, in general:** none of these three needed new logic — each is a one-symbol or one-type correction (wrong variable, wrong type assumption, wrong directory constant) — but each also took real debugging time because the failure mode was silence (empty query result, swallowed exception, misplaced-but-still-"working" file) rather than an exception surfacing at the call site. The common thread worth naming: every one of these three would have been caught immediately by a unit test asserting "a feature with `workflow_id=None` still resolves its tasks" — none existed before the fix.

**Relation to file-structure analysis:** not discussed in either existing section — the analysis doc's Task & Dispatch Services section covers `task_similarity_service.py`'s comparable `is None` vs `.is_(None)` bug as a still-open issue, and this cluster is the same defect family (silent-failure-on-NULL/type-mismatch) recurring in the design-queue's own feature↔workflow linking code, independently of that already-documented instance.

### 5. Ticket search & stats endpoints: query-language injection, routing order, and access control

**Problem.** `ticket_search_service.py` and `tickets_api.py`'s stats/search/board endpoints accumulated a cluster of API-surface correctness and safety bugs distinct from the ticket state-machine logic itself — FastAPI route-matching order, an unsanitized full-text-search query language, and missing authentication on state-mutating endpoints.

**Representative commits:**
- `75a35b3` (2026-07-23): `keyword_search` passed agent-supplied search text straight through as the SQLite FTS5 `MATCH` argument. FTS5's query grammar treats colons as column filters and `AND`/`OR`/`NOT`/`NEAR`/hyphens/unbalanced quotes as operators — text containing any of them either broke the query outright or silently changed its meaning. Observed live: searching a ticket titled "...capped-notice run counters..." raised `no such column: notice`, because FTS5 parsed part of the phrase as a column-filter expression, not because any table has that column. Fix: a `_fts5_query()` helper tokenizes to plain words via `\w+` and quotes each token individually — a quoted token is always a literal to FTS5, never an operator — preserving implicit-AND search semantics across terms. The same commit fixes a second, unrelated bug in the same failure mode family: `verify_no_open_tickets`'s rejection message truncated ticket ids to 8 characters (`ticket-a` from `ticket-abc123def...`), which — since real ids already start with `ticket-` — read as a plausible complete id to an agent that then tried to resolve it and got "Ticket not found." Now shows the full id.
- `3347e14` (2026-07-06): FastAPI route registration order bug — `/{ticket_id}` was registered before `/stats`, so a request for `/tickets/stats` matched the path-parameter route first, with `stats` bound as a literal `ticket_id`. Fixed by moving the `/stats` and `/stats/{workflow_id}` routes earlier in the file (FastAPI matches in registration order), alongside a response-schema fix (`total_tickets`, `workflow_id` as string) and switching from `server_state`'s DB handle to the standard `get_db()` context manager.
- `5e9a288` (2026-07-06, same day): the stats endpoint only supported filtering by `workflow_id`; the dashboard needed project-level rollups. Backend gained an optional `project_id` param; frontend's `getTicketStats` now detects whether it has a project or workflow id and routes to the right query shape.
- `ae4305f` (2026-07-06, same day, third fix in this endpoint in one day): when a workflow has no `BoardConfig` (ticket tracking not explicitly configured), the stats endpoint returned `null`, and the Kanban UI had no columns to render. Fix returns a hardcoded default board (Backlog/In Progress/In Review/Done, standard ticket types) instead of null — a fallback default, not a derived one, so the UI always has *something* to draw.
- `7a34870` (2026-07-25): two independent gaps closed in one commit — `POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE /api/autopilot/projects` had no authentication at all (unlike the cost endpoints, which already required `X-Agent-ID`), and the cost *query* `GET` endpoints (tasks/workflows/features/designs/projects costs) had no rate limiting, unlike `POST /cost-entries`. Both fixed by extending patterns that already existed elsewhere in the same file to endpoints that had been added without them — an inconsistency-closing fix, not a new mechanism.

**Relation to file-structure analysis:** new, and worth calling out specifically. The Result/Ticket/Validation Services section documents `ticket_search_service.py`'s hybrid semantic+FTS5 search mechanism and its embedding-provider wiring, but doesn't mention that the FTS5 query path was, until `75a35b3`, an unsanitized-user-input-into-query-language injection surface — a real security-adjacent gap, not just a correctness one, in a service the analysis otherwise treats as pure search infrastructure. The endpoint-auth gap in `7a34870` is similarly a concrete instance of a broader pattern worth flagging for the refactor: new endpoints added to `tickets_api.py`/`autopilot_api.py` over time didn't consistently inherit the auth/rate-limit conventions established for older, similar endpoints in the same file — three of these five commits (`3347e14`, `5e9a288`, `ae4305f`) landed on the *same day*, evidence this endpoint was being actively developed against real dashboard usage rather than spec'd up front.

### 6. Cost/credit collection: parsing what agents actually write, not what the format spec says

**Problem.** A small (two-commit) but sharply-scoped cluster: `cost_collection_service.py`'s per-CLI-tool transcript parsers assume a JSONL schema and file-naming convention, both of which drifted from what the actual CLI tool emits.

**Representative commits:**
- `049775c` (2026-07-27): three independent format-assumption fixes to `ClaudeCodeCollector`, discovered together while getting cost collection working against real transcripts. (1) The parser only recognized `type="message"`; Claude Code's actual JSONL emits `type="assistant"` — every real transcript was silently skipped, collecting zero cost. (2) Model-name normalization: `PRICES` lookup used exact match only, so a versioned model string like `claude-opus-4-8` never matched the `claude-opus-4` price-table key; fix adds a trailing-suffix-strip fallback (`re.sub(r"-\d+$", "", model, count=1)`) tried after the exact match fails. (3) Session-file discovery used a glob (`*_{session_id}.jsonl`) that didn't match Claude Code's real naming (a UUID5 derived from the session id, `uuid5(NAMESPACE_URL, session_id)`, matching what `ClaudeCodeAgent.get_launch_command` itself computes) — fix computes the same UUID5 directly and only falls back to glob if the exact filename isn't found.
- `cf5ade7` (2026-08-04): a much smaller bug in the consumer, not the collector — `FrontendAPI.get_task_full_details` built its response dict and `return`ed it directly, with the cost-enrichment code (`cost_total_usd`) sitting *after* the `return` statement, so it never ran. One-line fix: assign to a `result` variable first, let the cost code populate it, then return.

**Proper fix technique:** both fixes are "read the real artifact instead of trusting the documented/assumed format" — `049775c`'s three sub-fixes were all found by testing cost collection against genuine Claude Code session files rather than a schema description, and all three would have been caught by a single integration test asserting non-zero cost collection against a real transcript fixture (none existed before this fix). `cf5ade7` is unrelated in mechanism (dead code after an early return) but is the same symptom family from the caller's side: cost data computed correctly upstream, silently never reaching the API response.

**Relation to file-structure analysis:** confirmed and narrow. The Result/Ticket/Validation Services section's call graph already notes `cost_collection_service.py:collect_task_cost` is invoked redundantly from three separate sites (agent termination, monitoring sweep, task completion) but doesn't discuss format-parsing correctness at all — this pair of commits is the concrete evidence that even with three redundant *trigger* points, cost collection was returning zero for real transcripts until the parser itself was fixed to match the CLI tool's actual output. Redundant triggering does not substitute for correct parsing.

### 7. Terminal-vs-non-terminal status conflation: workflow and dependency-layer completion

**Problem.** Two structurally similar bugs, in different subsystems, both rooted in the same mistake: treating a function's "nothing more to do right now" return value as equivalent to "this thing is genuinely finished," when the return value is overloaded to also mean "paused," "timed out," or "interrupted."

**Representative commits:**
- `a949043` (2026-08-04): `_start_next_phase` returns `None` for three different reasons — the workflow isn't active/paused, another phase is already `in_progress` (stale state from goto/retry churn), or genuinely no higher-order phase remains — but every caller of `_complete_workflow` treated any `None` identically: mark the workflow `completed`. A goto-limit-exceeded forced "continue" past `product_validation` got treated as full completion while `doc_review`, `forensics_analysis`, `git_commit_push`, and `deploy` were all still pending and had never run — the workflow never reached the phase that merges to main. Fix: `_complete_workflow` now takes `current_phase_id` and independently verifies via a direct `Phase.order` query that no higher-order phase exists before marking completion, refusing (with a warning log) otherwise. The same commit fixes a parallel bug in `derive_workflow_status`: `task_statuses == {TaskStatus.DONE}` was read as "workflow completed," but a phase with *zero tasks yet created* is invisible to a task-status set entirely — "every task that exists is done" is not "every phase ran." Fix cross-checks every `Phase`'s own `PhaseExecution.status` before deriving `COMPLETED`.
- `8249365` (2026-07-30): the dependency-layer analog in `run_feature_pipelines`. The outer loop over topologically-sorted dependency layers records each feature's result status but, by design, doesn't let a `"failed"` dependency permanently block its dependents. The bug: `run_single_workflow`'s poll loop can also return `"interrupted"` (explicit stop) or `"timeout"` (this walk's own wall-clock budget expired, reset fresh on every resume) — neither means the feature is *resolved*, only that this particular walk stopped watching it — but the dispatch loop treated them identically to a genuine resolution and advanced to the next dependency layer anyway. Root-caused from a live incident via log timestamps: a dependency's workflow was still genuinely `active` when its own poll loop merely timed out at the default 7200s, and its dependent was dispatched 21 seconds later in the same walk. Fix: track whether any feature in a layer returned a non-terminal status and `break` before dispatching later layers this walk — the next walk re-resolves `execution_groups` fresh and correctly re-encounters the unresolved layer first.

**Proper fix technique, common to both:** stop overloading a single return value (`None`, a status string) to mean both "final, resolved state" and "stopped observing for now" — either widen the check to explicitly enumerate which outcomes are terminal (`8249365`'s `NON_TERMINAL_STATUSES = {"interrupted", "timeout"}` set), or add an independent verification query that doesn't rely on the ambiguous signal at all (`a949043`'s direct `Phase.order` remaining-work check).

**Relation to file-structure analysis:** new. Neither the Task & Dispatch Services nor Result/Ticket/Validation Services sections discuss workflow-completion or dependency-layer-advancement semantics — that logic lives in `phase_manager.py`/`orchestrator.py`, outside those sections' file scope — but both bugs are close cousins of the task-creation-claim theme's sub-pattern 4 ("sweep vs. synchronous-path races") documented earlier in this same second pass: a status value computed for one purpose (stop polling) gets silently reused for a different, stronger claim (this is done) by a caller that doesn't distinguish the two. Worth flagging for the refactor as a naming/typing fix as much as a logic one: an enum or explicit terminal/non-terminal tagging on these status strings would make the next instance of this bug a type error instead of a live incident.

---

**Overall assessment of this theme.** The clearest structural finding is that "search more places for the file/pattern" (sub-pattern 1) and "scope this query to the right project/design" (sub-pattern 3) are both recurring-fix shapes that never converged on a single correct implementation — each is a chain of 4-6 commits, each widening the previous fix's coverage rather than replacing it with a structurally complete one (a config-driven output-alias table for §1; a shared, audited scoping helper applied to every bulk state-mutation query for §3). The ticket-endpoint cluster (§5) surfaces a security-adjacent gap the file-structure analysis's service-boundary framing didn't catch (unsanitized FTS5 injection) alongside ordinary API-surface bugs, evidence that endpoint-level concerns (routing order, auth, rate limits) drift independently of the service logic they front. Sub-patterns 2, 6, and 7 are each tighter, better-contained clusters — 4-5 commits, clear root cause, no repeated rediscovery — the kind of bug that a services-layer refactor is less urgent to fix than the first three.

---

## Cross-Cutting Themes

These patterns recur across three or more of the eleven subsystem sections above. They are the highest-leverage targets for a refactor because fixing the pattern once (rather than the next instance of it) closes an entire bug class instead of one bug.

### 1. The task-creation-claim pattern is reimplemented at every call site instead of centralized

`PhaseExecution.task_creation_claimed_at` is the mutex that makes "does this phase already have a task" safe to check concurrently. It is claimed and released correctly today — but the claim/staleness-fallback/release triad is hand-copied at no fewer than **five** independent locations: `_create_phase_task` (orchestrator.py), `_create_corrective_task_body` (orchestrator.py), `_trigger_arbitration` (orchestrator.py), `_release_stale_task_creation_claims`'s sweep version (orchestrator.py), and `fire_spec_gate_if_ready`'s claim (task_completion_service.py). Nearly every fix commit referenced in the Orchestrator Core, Phase Management, and Task & Dispatch Services sections (`75e1e52`, `45dd5a2`, `85fc3c5`, `fe9a141`) is a variant of "a caller forgot to take/anchor/release the claim correctly." See Orchestrator Core §6, Phase Management §4, Task & Dispatch Services §3.

### 2. "N-th independent implementation" is the single most common structural defect in this codebase

This pattern recurs so consistently across every subsystem that it deserves naming as the codebase's dominant failure mode: a piece of logic that should be one shared primitive is instead independently reimplemented at each new call site, with each copy accumulating its own subset of fixes. Confirmed instances, worst first:

- **Agent termination**: at least **20 raw DB-field-flip sites** plus 2 "primitive" functions (`AgentManager.terminate_agent`, `terminate_agent_direct`) — independently fixed for the same invariant violation **four times** in git history (`83e79ec`, `92caa82`, `27e8e88`) and found live-violating it **twice more** during this analysis (`src/mcp/api.py:2174` `reset_phase`, `:3025` `stop_workflow` — both missing `terminated_at`; `src/monitoring/monitor.py:1809` missing `current_task_id` clear).
- **Agent/task dispatch**: **three** independent implementations — `AgentDispatchService.dispatch` (HTTP route path), `create_agent_for_task_direct` (orchestrator in-process path), and `spawn_validator_agent` (validator path) — each with different or absent duplicate-dispatch/CLI-concurrency guards.
- **Worktree removal**: **four** independent implementations — `WorktreeManager._remove_worktree`/`cleanup_worktree` (`require_clean`-guarded, the only one with real safety checks), `WorktreeManager.cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s inline sweep, `orchestrator.py`'s `_cleanup_worktree` (raw `git worktree remove --force`, bypasses the safety-guarded primitive entirely despite that primitive's own docstring claiming to be the sole choke point), and `orchestrator.py`'s `_archive_and_cleanup` (confirmed dead code).
- **Merge conflict resolution**: **three** independent strategies for the same conceptual operation — `WorktreeManager.merge_to_main` (newest-file-wins, confirmed dead in production), `cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s inline merge (newest-file-wins, live for legacy branches), and `run_single_workflow`'s final-merge block (abort-and-preserve-with-no-notification, live for the common shared-worktree case).
- **tmux message delivery**: **three** independent implementations — `AgentMessenger.send_message_to_agent` (the audited primitive, with `_pane_is_wedged` stuck-shell detection and consistent quote-escaping), and `AgentCommunicationService`'s `get_child_logs`/`send_message_to_child` (raw `subprocess.run(["tmux", ...])`, its own per-character-argv escaping scheme, no stuck-shell detection, not async-wrapped).
- **Cascading task-fail-on-termination logic**: `reset_phase` and `stop_workflow` (both in `src/mcp/api.py`) implement structurally identical "fail every active task in this scope" logic with two different status sets (`assigned/in_progress/pending` vs. `assigned/in_progress/queued/pending`).
- **Project CRUD**: two parallel route surfaces (`/api/projects/*` and `/api/autopilot/projects/*`).
- **Read-endpoint duplication**: `src/mcp/api.py`'s `FrontendAPI.get_agents`/`get_agent_output` are functionally-different, silently-unreachable dead copies of `agents_api.py`'s `GET /api/agents`/`GET /api/agents/{agent_id}/output` — a router-registration-order accident (`agents_router` at module load, `api_router` inside `startup_event`) rather than a deliberate override, meaning a future edit to the `api.py` copies would have zero effect on the running server and nobody would notice.

Every one of these is the same underlying defect at a different layer: no shared primitive, so no single place to fix the bug once discovered.

### 3. Two independent phase-completion entry points, three independent dispatch pipelines

`_advance_phases`'s periodic sweep and `TaskCompletionService.fire_spec_gate_if_ready`'s synchronous fast path both decide phase completion by calling `PhaseManager.mark_phase_complete` — but they earned their concurrency protection independently and at different times (`fe9a141` closed the synchronous path's claim gap long after the sweep path already had one). The dispatch side is worse than previously documented: `AgentDispatchService.dispatch`, `create_agent_for_task_direct`, and `spawn_validator_agent` are three separate ways to create an agent for a task, with materially different duplicate-guard coverage — a task or validation's protection level against races depends entirely on which of the three paths happened to create it. See Phase Management §3/§7, Task & Dispatch Services §6, Validator Agents & Memory/RAG Backend §1.2.

### 4. Policy expressed in multiple disconnected places drifts silently

The `git_commit_push` manual-only saga (Workflow Termination & API Surface §3) is the clearest example: the policy lived simultaneously in a YAML field that **no code ever read**, a hardcoded Python phase-name string check duplicated across 4 call sites, and a prose description block that was never updated to match either — producing four commits of thrash in eight hours and a test suite that silently failed for 7.5 hours. The same pattern recurs at smaller scale throughout: `spec_gate: true` read by a *third*, independent YAML scanner that `yaml_loader.build_phase` never touches; `self_review` typed inconsistently between the loader and `phase_manager.py`; `config_validator.py`'s `VALID_CONDITION_ACTIONS` narrower than the engine's real `OrchestrationAction` enum; and — newly found — **embedding/vector-store configuration split across two entirely independent config surfaces** (`EMBEDDING_BACKEND`/`FASTEMBED_MODEL` env vars read by `embedding_factory.py`, vs. `hephaestus_config.yaml`'s `llm.embedding_provider` read by `LangChainLLMClient` for `RAGSystem`) that both silently have to agree with `TurboVecStore`'s hardcoded per-collection `dim: 384` — nothing in the code cross-validates that agreement; a mismatch fails as a swallowed, logged-only exception rather than a startup check. See Validator Agents & Memory/RAG Backend §2-4.

### 5. Dead code, dead tables, and stale type hints accumulate without removal

Found across nearly every subsystem: `SteeringIntervention` (fully-modeled DB table, API read endpoint in `api.py`, a delete-only consumer in `server.py:3574`, confirmed via fresh grep to have **zero** `SteeringIntervention(...)` constructor calls anywhere in `src/` — the dashboard feed for it can only ever render empty); `TrajectoryContext` (598 lines, unreachable from Guardian's actual call path); `embedding_service.py`'s `EmbeddingService` class (imported only for a type hint that lies about the real runtime type); `orchestrator.py`'s legacy `api_get`/`api_post` helpers; `SWEEP_ENABLED = False`-gated dead sweep; deprecated `MAX_WORKFLOW_TIME`/`MAX_PHASE0_TIME` constants; the dead `"running"` `Workflow.status` filter branch at 7 call sites; `_archive_and_cleanup` (confirmed dead, orchestrator.py); `WorktreeManager.merge_to_main`/`cleanup_worktree` (confirmed dead for the shared-worktree model the live pipeline predominantly uses); `check_executors.py`'s entire validation-check-executor module (confirmed zero callers); `MemoryIngestion` in `rag.py` (confirmed zero callers); `MonitoringLoop.rag_system` (wired in, never referenced); `src/mcp/api.py`'s `get_agents`/`get_agent_output` (confirmed unreachable dead code, shadowed by `agents_api.py`'s versions registered earlier). The **stale-type-hint-that-lies-about-runtime-type** sub-pattern alone now has three confirmed instances: `embedding_service.py`'s `EmbeddingService` (server.py:275, task_similarity_service.py), and `rag.py`'s `RAGSystem` constructor typed for `VectorStoreManager` when the real object is `TurboVecStore`. None of these are individually load-bearing, but collectively they make the codebase harder to trust by inspection — a reader has no signal distinguishing live code from a documented-dead path without independently re-deriving it, as this analysis had to at every turn.

### 6. God-objects and oversized functions from patch-on-patch accumulation

`orchestrator.py` is 10,246 lines; `task_completion_service.py` is 1,125 lines across 11 static methods; `src/mcp/api.py` is 3,225 lines across 42 routes with no consistent delegation pattern (roughly two-thirds go through a `FrontendAPI` method, the rest — including the file's largest single non-delegated block, `stop_workflow` — are inlined directly in the route registration function); `_case_in_progress_complete` is ~400 lines; `create_agent_for_task` is ~970 lines end-to-end; `mark_phase_complete` is 160 lines carrying six independently-motivated responsibilities; `_start_next_phase` is 195 lines stacking five independently-motivated invariants; `rerun_design` is a ~415-line route handler; `get_commit_diff_endpoint` is a ~145-line route handler doing its own git-plumbing orchestration with no service delegation. In every case, the size is not accidental complexity — it is the visible residue of incident-driven, one-fix-at-a-time development on a system that has never had a dedicated refactor pass.

### 7. Inconsistent async/blocking discipline — now confirmed in three files, not one

Despite this project's own forbidden-pattern rule, and despite several routes (`repair_design` in `autopilot_api.py`, `reset_phase`'s tmux-kill in `api.py`) demonstrating the correct `run_in_executor` pattern, blocking calls inside `async def` handlers with no thread-pool offload were found in **three separate files**: `autopilot_api.py` (`rerun_design`'s `time.sleep` loop, `review_feature`'s `gh pr merge`, `remove_project_design`'s `tmux kill-session`, `get_system_health`'s several `subprocess.run` calls); `src/mcp/api.py` (`get_agent_output`'s subprocess-backed `capture-pane` fallback, `create_phase_prompt_version`/`restore_phase_prompt_version`'s retry-loop `time.sleep`, and — most strikingly — `stop_workflow`'s un-offloaded per-agent `subprocess.run` sitting **three lines of code away**, in the same class, from `reset_phase`'s correctly-offloaded identical operation); and `tickets_api.py`/`agents_api.py` (`get_commit_diff_endpoint`'s three untimed `subprocess.run` calls, `AgentCommunicationService`'s synchronous tmux calls reached from `async def` routes with no executor wrap). The pattern is known and correctly applied in roughly half the places it's needed — the other half accumulated independently without anyone checking for the sibling case nearby.

### 8. Timestamp convention violations concentrate exactly where the CLAUDE.md incident narrative predicts

The project's own CLAUDE.md documents a real incident where a `datetime.now()`/`datetime.utcnow()` mismatch in a staleness check silently broke a claim mechanism for hours. This analysis found the same class of bug still live, specifically in the staleness-detection subsystem: `src/monitoring/orphan_reaper.py:111,125,147` use `datetime.now()` (naive local time) while every timestamp it compares against is written with `datetime.utcnow()`. Confirmed by direct source read (not just subagent report). See Monitoring §6.

---

## Newly Identified Bugs (not yet fixed)

These were found during this analysis (not present in the reviewed git history's list of already-fixed issues) and are independent of the design-level refactor recommendations — each is a small, isolated, likely-fast fix. Items 1-6 were verified by direct source reads during this session (not merely trusted from subagent output); items 7-14 come from the four gap-closing deep-dives and follow the same file:line citation discipline but were not independently re-verified by the lead session.

| # | Location | Bug | Impact | Verified |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `src/monitoring/orphan_reaper.py:111,125,147` | `datetime.now()` used instead of `datetime.utcnow()` for staleness comparisons and `terminated_at` | Silently wrong by the host's UTC offset; the exact bug class CLAUDE.md's incident narrative documents | Yes, direct read |
| 2 | `src/services/task_similarity_service.py:71` | `query.filter(Task.phase_id is None)` uses Python `is None` instead of SQLAlchemy `.is_(None)` — compiles to `WHERE 0` | Phase-less task duplicate detection silently matches nothing, ever | Yes, direct read |
| 3 | `src/services/queue_service.py:374,380,390` | `not Task.priority_boosted` applied to the SQLAlchemy class attribute rather than an instance value | Dead branches in the queue priority/tie-break `or_()` expression | Yes, direct read |
| 4 | `src/mcp/api.py:2174` (`reset_phase`) | Sets `current_task_id=None` but never `terminated_at` on forced agent termination | Agent rows terminated via this path are invisible to any future `terminated_at`-based query/report | Yes, direct read |
| 5 | `src/mcp/api.py:3025` (`stop_workflow`) | Same as #4 — missing `terminated_at` | Same as #4 | Yes, direct read |
| 6 | `src/monitoring/monitor.py:1809` (`_detect_orphaned_idle_agent`) | Sets `terminated_at` but never clears `Agent.current_task_id` | The exact invariant three prior commits were written to close, missed at this one call site | Yes, direct read |
| 7 | `src/mcp/memory_api.py:854-859` (`submit_result_validation`) | Resolves "which agent is the validator" via `ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 1` on `agent_type == "result_validator"`, with no scoping to the specific `result_id`/workflow | Two concurrent result-validation runs across different workflows can cross-wire outcomes to the wrong workflow's validator | Subagent-reported |
| 8 | `src/core/worktree_manager.py:774-783` (`get_agent_branch_path`) | Falls back to the main repo path (not `None`) when no `AgentBranch` record exists | `restart_agent` can silently relaunch an agent into the main project repository instead of failing loudly | Subagent-reported |
| 9 | `src/core/worktree_manager.py`'s `cleanup_all_stale_branches` untracked-branch filter | Checks `("agent-", "autopilot-", "feature_architect/")` prefixes; live shared-worktree branches are actually named `feature/{design}` and `feature/{design}/{feature}` — neither matched; `"autopilot-"` matches nothing ever produced | Shared-worktree branches missed by the generic self-heal sweep, relying entirely on their owning workflow's own cleanup call | Subagent-reported |
| 10 | `src/mcp/tickets_api.py:1261` (`get_commit_diff_endpoint`) | Three `subprocess.run(["git", ...])` calls with no `timeout=` argument | A hung/slow git process on a large or corrupted repo blocks the request indefinitely | Subagent-reported |
| 11 | `src/mcp/api.py` `reset_phase` vs. `stop_workflow` | Identical tmux-kill operation offloaded via `run_in_executor` in one, bare blocking `subprocess.run` in the other, ~850 lines apart in the same class | Inconsistent async discipline; `stop_workflow` blocks the event loop up to 5s per agent terminated | Subagent-reported |
| 12 | `src/memory/turbovec_store.py` vs `src/memory/vector_store.py` | `TurboVecStore` collections are hardcoded `dim: 384`; `VectorStoreManager` collections are hardcoded `dim: 3072`; nothing validates `EMBEDDING_BACKEND` and `VECTOR_STORE_BACKEND` agree on dimension | Switching one backend without the other silently breaks every `store_memory`/`search` call (logged error, not raised) | Subagent-reported |
| 13 | `src/mcp/agents_api.py`'s `GET /api/agents`, `GET /api/agents/{agent_id}/output` | Shadow functionally-different, identically-pathed routes in `src/mcp/api.py` (`FrontendAPI.get_agents`/`get_agent_output`) which are permanently unreachable due to router registration order | The `api.py` copies are dead code that will silently never run if edited | Subagent-reported |
| 14 | `config/workflows/autopilot/git_commit_push.yaml:4-7` | Description text ("must not commit, push...") contradicts both the STEP 1-5 body below it and the actual `review_mode`-gated runtime behavior | Misleads any human or agent reading the phase config about its real autonomy | Yes, from original analysis |
| 15 | Seven call sites (`orchestrator/engine_client.py:232, orchestrator/queue.py:61`; `autopilot_api.py:527,555,859,1076,5623`) | Filter on `Workflow.status.in_(["active", "running"])` — `"running"` is not a value the `workflows.status` CheckConstraint permits | Dead filter branch, signals a misunderstanding of the status model copy-pasted into new call sites | Yes, from original analysis |

## Prioritized Refactor Roadmap

Synthesized from every subsystem section's "Refactor observations," ordered by (a) how many historical incidents or newly-confirmed live bugs the change would have prevented, and (b) how self-contained the change is to land safely.

### Tier 1 — closes entire bug classes, should come first

1. **Extract the task-creation-claim pattern into one reusable primitive**, replacing the five independent hand-copies across `orchestrator.py` and `task_completion_service.py`. Given the fix-commit history, this single change would have prevented or simplified `75e1e52`, `45dd5a2`, `85fc3c5`, and `fe9a141`.
2. **Introduce one agent-termination write path.** Unify `AgentManager.terminate_agent` and `terminate_agent_direct` into a single primitive that always sets all three invariant fields and always resets stray tasks; migrate every raw `agent.status = "terminated"` assignment (now confirmed ≥21 sites across `src/agents`, `src/mcp/api.py`, `src/mcp/autopilot_api.py`, `src/mcp/server.py`, `src/monitoring/`, `src/autopilot/orchestrator.py`) to call it. Pair with a DB-level check or `before_update` hook enforcing the invariant. This is now the single most-repeated defect found across the entire analysis — six confirmed historical/live instances of the same bug.
3. **Consolidate the seven-plus ad hoc workflow/agent-termination paths** (`pause_feature`, `pause_project_workflows`/`stop_pipeline`, `requeue_design`, `rerun_design`, `delete_feature`, `reset_phase`, `api.py`'s `stop_workflow`) behind one service function that always performs real agent teardown, or extend `WorkflowTerminationHandler` to cover the Autopilot case.
4. **Fix the confirmed-live `datetime.now()` violations in `orphan_reaper.py`** (lines 111, 125, 147) — small, isolated, high-signal given the CLAUDE.md incident this exactly reproduces.
5. **Scope `submit_result_validation`'s validator-agent lookup to the specific `result_id`/workflow** instead of "most recently created `result_validator` agent anywhere" — a live cross-workflow-mixup bug under concurrent result submissions (newly identified bug #7).

### Tier 2 — removes structural duplication, medium effort

6. **Unify the three dispatch pipelines** (`AgentDispatchService`, `create_agent_for_task_direct`, `spawn_validator_agent`) behind one code path with one duplicate-dispatch guard.
7. **Give the shared/integration-worktree model a real `WorktreeManager` API** — right now `orchestrator.py` treats `WorktreeManager` as a bare `Repo`-handle factory and reimplements branch creation, worktree creation, removal, and merging from scratch four separate times. A `create_shared_worktree`/`merge_shared_branch` pair used by both the isolated-agent and shared-worktree callers would collapse four ad-hoc implementations into one tested, safety-guarded path.
8. **Pick one merge-conflict-resolution strategy and delete the other two** (`WorktreeManager.merge_to_main`, `cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s inline merge, `run_single_workflow`'s abort-and-preserve block) — three different behaviors for the same conceptual operation is a correctness risk, not just a style inconsistency.
9. **Reconcile the two phase-completion entry points** (`fire_spec_gate_if_ready`'s synchronous path vs. the periodic sweep's `_case_in_progress_complete`) so the race between them is structurally impossible rather than closed by parallel, independently-maintained claim checks.
10. **Give `manual_only`/human-approval phase gating a single source of truth** — a `PhasePolicy.is_manual_only(phase, project) -> bool` all current call sites delegate to.
11. **Route `AgentCommunicationService`'s tmux calls through `AgentMessenger`** instead of a third hand-rolled implementation, closing both the escaping-strategy divergence and the missing stuck-shell detection on the parent-child messaging path.
12. **Unify YAML phase-config reading** — collapse `yaml_loader.build_phase`, `config_validator`'s independent parse, and `spec._load_gated_phases`'s independent parse into one canonical parse pass.
13. **Split the largest god-objects along their already-visible seams**: `orchestrator.py`'s largest functions, `task_completion_service.py`'s 11-method surface, `phase_manager.py`'s `mark_phase_complete`/`_start_next_phase`, `autopilot_api.py`'s `rerun_design`, `src/mcp/api.py`'s `stop_workflow` (extract into a `FrontendAPI` method matching `reset_phase`'s shape). Preserve the incident-driven comments as test names/docstrings during the split.

### Tier 3 — cleanup, low risk, improves legibility

14. **Delete or revive the identified dead code**: `SteeringIntervention` (table + its one read endpoint + its delete-only consumer at `server.py:3574`), `TrajectoryContext`, `embedding_service.py`, `orchestrator.py`'s legacy `api_get`/`api_post`, `SWEEP_ENABLED`-gated dead sweep, deprecated `MAX_WORKFLOW_TIME`/`MAX_PHASE0_TIME` constants, seven dead `"running"` status filter branches, `_archive_and_cleanup`, `WorktreeManager.merge_to_main`/`cleanup_worktree` (if truly unreachable), `check_executors.py`, `rag.py`'s `MemoryIngestion`, `MonitoringLoop.rag_system`, and `src/mcp/api.py`'s dead `get_agents`/`get_agent_output`.
15. **Unify the two embedding-provider instances and three embedding-configuration pathways.** `server_state.embedding_service`/`TicketSearchService`'s independent instance should share one provider; `RAGSystem` should route through `embedding_factory.create_embedding_provider()` instead of `llm_provider.generate_embedding`, collapsing three disjoint config surfaces (`EMBEDDING_BACKEND`, `llm.embedding_provider`, `TurboVecStore`'s hardcoded dims) that currently must silently agree with nothing checking that they do.
16. **Rename the colliding `validation_helpers.py`/`result_validation_helpers.py` files**, and clarify `DetectedDuplicate` belongs to Conductor, not `TaskSimilarityService`.
17. **Reconcile the two parallel project-CRUD route surfaces** (`/api/projects/*` vs. `/api/autopilot/projects/*`).
18. **Wrap all remaining blocking calls in async route handlers with `run_in_executor`** — the confirmed list now spans `autopilot_api.py`, `src/mcp/api.py`, and `tickets_api.py`/`agents_api.py`. Add explicit timeouts to `get_commit_diff_endpoint`'s three untimed `subprocess.run` calls first, before wrapping.
19. **Fix the newly-identified logic bugs**: `task_similarity_service.py:71`'s `is None`, `queue_service.py`'s `not Task.priority_boosted` on the class attribute, `get_agent_branch_path`'s silent main-repo fallback, `cleanup_all_stale_branches`'s stale branch-prefix filter — add regression tests for each, since none are currently covered.
20. **Normalize auth guarding across `agents_api.py`** — some routes call `_require_localhost`, the state-mutating ones don't, with no documented rationale for the split.
21. **Add a batched/debounced flush to `TurboVecStore.store_memory`** instead of a full index+metadata rewrite per insert.
22. **Consolidate cost-collection triggering** behind one call site, or make the three existing triggers' idempotency explicit and tested.

---

## Coverage Notes

This document was extended once after an initial gap-check identified files that were heavily *referenced* across the original seven sections but never themselves *opened*. The four follow-up deep-dives (Worktree Management & CLI Interface, Validator Agents & Memory/RAG Backend, Core Agent & Task API, Additional API Surface) closed the highest-priority gaps: `src/mcp/api.py`, `src/core/worktree_manager.py`, `src/interfaces/cli_interface.py` (per-CLI detail), `src/mcp/agents_api.py`/`tickets_api.py`/`messaging_api.py`, `src/validation/validator_agent.py`/`check_executors.py`, and `src/memory/{store_factory,embedding_factory,turbovec_store,vector_store,rag}.py`.

**Still explicitly out of scope**, and not claimed otherwise anywhere in this document:

- **The React/TypeScript frontend** (`frontend/`) — this document covers the backend engine only. Nothing here describes how the dashboard renders, consumes, or reacts to any of the API surface documented in sections 9-11.
- **`src/interfaces/{langchain_llm_client,llm_interface,multi_provider_llm,openrouter_client}.py`** — the LLM-provider abstraction layer itself (as opposed to its callers, several of which are documented: Guardian's `analyze_agent_trajectory` call, Conductor's `analyze_system_coherence` call, `RAGSystem.retrieve_for_task`'s `generate_embedding` call). How prompts are actually assembled and sent to OpenRouter/OpenAI/Anthropic, retry/rate-limit handling, and response validation for `GuardianTrajectoryAnalysis`/`ConductorSystemAnalysis` were never traced into this layer.
- **`src/sdk/`** (`HephaestusSDK`, `process_manager.py`, `config.py`, `models.py`) — referenced as the interface `orchestrator.py` calls (`sdk.start_workflow`, `sdk.start`, `sdk.shutdown`) and as the source of `WorkflowConfig`/`Phase` dataclasses, but its own internals (process supervision, the `forge_main.py`/TUI entry points under `src/sdk/`) were not analyzed.
- **`src/core/{app_context,status_derivation,cost_derivation}.py`** — referenced repeatedly (`get_app_state()`, `derive_feature_status`, `check_budget_before_new_work`/`record_cost`) as call targets from sections that were analyzed, but not opened and traced themselves.
- **Test suite** (`tests/`) — this document is a map of implementation, not of test coverage. Several findings note "no regression test currently covers this," which is an inference from the absence of a test surfacing the bug during normal development, not a systematic audit of `tests/`.

**Related prior art, not cross-referenced against this document's findings.** This repository already carries a substantial body of earlier design and review documents that were not read for this analysis and may overlap with, already address, or provide additional rationale for findings above:

- `design_docs/` (10 files, ~440KB) — most notably `backend_module_decomposition.md` (37KB) and `autopilot_architecture_review.md` (69KB), both titled closely enough to this document's subject that they may already propose some of the decompositions recommended in the [Prioritized Refactor Roadmap](#prioritized-refactor-roadmap), or document prior decisions behind patterns this document flags as unexplained code smells. Also present: `autopilot_review_mode.md`, `budget_tracking_approval_system.md`, `human_input_intervention_system.md`, `06_ticket_tracking_system.md`, `feature_model_implementation.md`, `unified_project_system.md`, `workflow-phases-merge.md`, `per_task_cost_tracking.md`.
- `docs/SOLID_OO_REVIEW.md` (1015 lines) and its companion `docs/SOLID_REFACTOR_ADVERSARIAL_REVIEW.md` (607 lines) — a prior class/function-design-level review of "the full backend" that likely already covers some of the god-object and duplicated-primitive findings in this document's Cross-Cutting Themes, from an OO/SOLID angle rather than a bug-history angle. `SOLID_OO_REVIEW.md`'s own header references a companion `docs/ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW.md` ("runtime/flow issues... fixed in a prior pass") that does not exist at that path in the current tree — possibly renamed, moved into `design_docs/autopilot_architecture_review.md`, or lost; this was not investigated.
- `docs/COST_TRACKING_DESIGN.md`, `docs/MULTI_PROJECT_CONCURRENCY_DESIGN.md`, `docs/SAFE_RESTART_DESIGN.md`, `docs/PI_MODEL_FALLBACK_DESIGN.md`, `docs/GAP_CHECK_SELF_LOOP_DESIGN.md` — narrower prior design docs whose subjects directly correspond to sections of this document (cost tracking, `max_concurrent_projects`, the safe-restart grace window, pi model fallback) and may contain design rationale this document's file-level analysis couldn't recover from code alone.

Before treating this document's roadmap as a from-scratch plan, a reader should at minimum skim `backend_module_decomposition.md` and `autopilot_architecture_review.md` for overlap — this document was not checked against either.

A reader using this document to scope a refactor should treat the eleven numbered sections as reliable for the files and call chains they name, and treat anything about the frontend, the LLM-provider layer, the SDK's own internals, test coverage, or alignment with prior design docs as unknown rather than assumed-absent or assumed-novel.
